Title: Vampire: The Masquerade- The Velvet Curtain
By: Paddy
Writing Start Date: 13th August, 2026
Writing End Date: ongoing
Fandoms: World Of Darkness (WOD), Vampire: The Masquerade (VTM), Changeling: The Lost (CTL), Orpheus
Genre(s): Horror, Urban Fantasy, Drama, Thriller, Intrigue, Action-Adventure, Gothic Punk, Gothic Horror, Supernatural, Tragedy, Magical Realism, Modern Gothic, Political Horror, Social Horror, Economic Horror
Summary: Where? Magnolia City. When? 201X. Lena Adams is about to find out just what- and who- is on the other side of that velvet curtain. Not all is at it seems in this city. Old rivalries will come to the fore...and the very same velvet curtain shall be torn asunder. Will Lena and those near and dear to her be left standing by the time the velvet curtain is ripped apart?
Content/Trigger Warnings: Intense Violence, Blood & Gore, Torture, Strong Language, Slurs, Bigotry/Hate, Racism, Queerphobia, Sexual Content/Scenes, Full & Partial Nudity, Alcohol/Substance Consumption, Smoking, Drug Usage, Human & Animal Death, Xenophobia, and so on.

Chapter One: Lena Adams
Where?
Magnolia City, USA.
When?
201X.
Who?
She walks down a busy sidewalk. It's a bright, sunny day, the sky a bright shade of blue. She's dressed in a beige duffle coat, slim-fitting slacks and black boots. A pair of inexpensive sunglasses cover her eyes.
Before heading for work, she always stops by the coffee shop off West Street. The bell rings as she pushes the door open. The hubbub of conversation is the first thing that hits her, and she lets her eyes adjust to the interior.
There's a young, blonde barista behind the counter, wearing a tight-fitting black top with a vague, cartoony image of a skull printed on it. The skull is winking. The barista's feet are already aching.
The frantic and chaotic bustle of the coffee shop doesn't let up. Inner-city youth are oft wont to frequent this "hip" establishment. The other minimum wage baristas are baggy-eyed and gaunt- they idly shamble back and forth, prepping coffee for demanding customers.
She takes off her sunglasses, revealing a pair of appealing amber eyes. She waits in line and has a guarded expression on her face.
A young man with spiked hair and an over-sized shirt orders a large espresso.
When the line thins, she finally reaches the counter. The barista smiles, revealing a pair of sparkling braces. His smile is as though a puppeteer has forced his cheekbones to move upwards.
"Hello, what can I get for you?" he asks, trying not to sound so dull and monotonous.
"Can I have a small Americano?" she asks, sparing him a look of sympathy.
"Sure. That'll be uh..." the barista trails off while typing up the order into the cash register. He stares at the glowing screen blankly.
She produces her card; the barista swipes it and hands the machine for her to enter her PIN. She does so without any fuss. After that, she produces some kind of scratch card from her purse.
The barista has a knowing look on his gaunt face. He snatches it from her, clicks a pen and fills in the fifteenth circle. When he's done, he hands it back to her.
He dryly says, "Your twenty-fifth will be free. Keep at it, Miss Adams."
She nods back at him and patiently waits for her coffee, taking a quiet spot in the corner of the shop. She clasps her hands together and waits. Her burgundy fingernails gleam as they reflect the soft interior light.
The barista makes her drink, writes her full name on the styrofoam cup with a sharpie, and calls out, LENA ADAMS! Got a small Americano for Lena Adams."
Lena gets up, grabs the coffee, thanks the employees for their hospitality, and leaves. The bell rings as she pushes the door open. Her jet-black hair is tied up into a high ponytail held in place by a red scrunchie.
She walks down the busy sidewalk while sipping on her usual morning beverage. Faint traces of brick-red lipstick stain the rim of the styrofoam cup.
Smatterings of wispy clouds float by in the blue sky. The sidewalk is crowded with the usual assortment of office drones, tourists, students of all types, and hipsters.
In more ways than one, this Northwestern metropolis is a pretty diverse, vibrant, and interesting place!
Lena turns and takes a left. She walks for half a block until she comes across a set of steps leading down to an underground train station. Namely, Crystal Street Station.
Crystal Street Station is nothing short of an architectural marvel- much like many other structures in Magnolia City. High ceilings, polished marble, and a series of (mostly) working escalators. And it doesn't hurt that the trains...almost always (barring certain exceptional exceptions) arrive on time.
Lena deftly weaves through the throng of people, trying to keep her footing. Not that there are so many people currently present, but the morning rush hour tends, more often than not, to be quite chaotic.
Miss Adams steps onto the escalator, and it takes her down to the subway platform. She waits for the train, standing idly at the platform. She pulls out her makeup compact, flicks it open, and gives her face a quick once-over before tucking it away back into her purse.
She is an Asian American woman in her late twenties. Her complexion is warm. Her pert nose has a faint splash of blackheads across the bridge- something that has come to bother her since quite recently.
Her angular features are enhanced by the sunglasses that she's wearing.
Lena cranes her neck and always finds something new to marvel at in Crystal Street Station. Numerous little details that, upon a closer inspection, have a tendency to captivate her attention for a moment or two.
Or more.
The announcer on the speakers goes on about a train arriving on platform two. Lena's on platform five, so it doesn't really concern her. A minute later, his smooth voice bears some bad news.
"Pink line will be delayed owing to emergency rail works. Nothing too major, folks. We sincerely apologize for the inconvenience."
Lena huffs a sigh, "Ah, great. That's just perfect."
She walks over to the nearby map, scanning over it with narrowed eyes. While she's not late for work, she doesn't quite like showing up mere minutes before the workday officially begins- something (for better or for worse) that she inherited from her father, Yoshiro Akira.
Lena runs her finger across the map and thinks about her ailing father, wistfully wondering if she could do anything for him. Her amber eyes become unfocused, staring off into the farthest distance.
She is snapped out of her thoughts when her smartphone starts to ring. Her purse vibrates in her hand. She fishes it out and sees that it's her mother- Susie Adams- calling.
She taps the green button and raises the phone to her ear, "Mom. H--hi. Everything OK?" Lena's voice is a little deeper than usual, but there is still a certain appeal to it.
"Hello, darling," her mother's voice is a lot more relaxed and melodic. Very Midwestern, very much dripping with genuine honey and not the vague and self-absorbed fakery that so many other people tend to exhibit.
"Yeah, I'm fine. How's dad?"
Susie chuckles feebly, "Oh, you know how Yoshi gets."
"Yoshi", the cutesy nickname that she has for her husband.
Lena smiles a little and asks again, "No, but really, how is he?"
"Listen, you don't worry about the two of us, OK? We're more than capable of taking care of each other. Live your life in Magnolia City. Work hard, have fun, meet somebody special, the usual! And listen, hon, about the--"
Lena emphatically cuts her mom off, "No! I insist, OK?! I saw the horror show that is dad's medical bills! I AM sending you some money and you're accepting it, no arguments!"
Susie's sigh of resignation says it all, "Are--are you sure, hon? What about YOUR living expenses?"
"I'm fine, I'm managing. Somehow, I'm getting by."
"I'd holler for your dad to come say hi, but he's resting his legs, eyes, and everything on his usual spot."
"The chair on the porch outside?"
"Mm-hmm. Sometimes, I--I--check his pulse to make sure that...y'know?"
Lena tightly winces, "Mom..."
Susie's voice breaks and she sniffles, "Don't you worry about us, Lena. We--we're getting by just fine without you here. Hey, I can hear the choo-choo train in the background. Wait, aren't ya usually on it by now?"
Lena softly chuckles and looks over her shoulder, "Delays. Nothing outta the ordinary. Give dad my love, OK?"
"Alright, hon. Have a great day at work!"
"Yup, that's the plan, heh."
Susie's tone of voice is stern, and she speaks in a slow, measured cadence, "Honey, promise me one thing."
Lena blinks and tilts her head to one side, "Sure, mom. Anything."
Susie breathes in, breathes out and lowers her voice, "Promise me that you'll be careful out there, OK? I...occasionally read some stuff on websites about Magnolia City. Now, don't get me wrong: it's a beautiful place- as I'm sure you're well aware, haha! But...there are rumors floating around. About what really goes on out here."
The train hisses to a stop on platform five and the doors slide open. Lena narrows her eyes and walks inside while asking, "What do you mean, mom?"
"Don't you...know?"
"No? What are these websites, anyway? God, I sincerely hope that you're not falling for bullshit conservative conspiracy theories now. Ugh, mom, c'mon."
Susie sighs, "C'mon, hon, cut me some slack here. Like I'd ever lay eyes on those 'news' sites! No, I--I've heard things, read things that sound...legit. They say that there are some really dangerous folks living in the city."
Lena sits down on an unoccupied seat. The interior of this train is bright, sleek and modern- a far cry from the old, run-down cars that were efficiently done away with and replaced many, many years ago.
Lena counters, "Well, I mean, it's not like Indiana doesn't have its fair share of quote-unquote 'dangerous folk'. C'mon, mom, what are you trying to say?"
"I don't want to scare you, but...just be careful. I--I know you have the side...thing and all that. But when you're out there, doing that, just...promise your mother that you'll be careful, OK?"
Lena places her ankle on her knee, "You know that I am. I'm not stupid. Besides, my side 'thing' is actually something that I'm kinda proud of."
"Alright, alright. Don't get too cocky, missy."
Lena chuckles, "I love you, mom. Both you and dad."
Susie smiles broadly, "And we love you, too. We love you for who you are, no matter what. Always remember that."
The call ends. Lena throws her head back and sharply exhales. The train slows down, coming to a stop at its next destination. From there, it's a fairly short walk to her place of work.
Lena swipes her pass card at the turnstile gates, which then proceed to loudly and mechanically click into place. She walks onto the street and looks both ways before crossing the road.
Architecturally speaking, Lena's place of work- not just her office space- was done by the same people who did the Ford Foundation Center For Social Justice. The concrete is covered with gray-pink granite. It rises high up into the sky.
The interior is nothing short of breathtaking. The atrium has a multi-tiered garden that contains an array of rare and exotic plants from all over the world. At the very top is an observation deck that's open to the public.
An elegant fountain made of stone is right in the middle of the vast lobby. Water falls from the tip of a large, abstract monolith-looking sculpture and down into a shallow pool filled with coins.
The glass elevators are always available, and they whisk visitors up to their floors of choice. Lena's heels click against the polished floor as she walks across the atrium.
The rays of the sun stream in from the atrium, casting bright, yellow squares against the dark wood. Numerous office spaces and cubicles are located at every floor of this structure.
This place looks so very similar to the Ford Foundation Center For Social Justice, but it serves an entirely different function. And in a port city like this, that can be pretty relevant.
Numerous shipping companies have their corporate offices located on the topmost floors, along with other maritime-adjacent firms, water-tourism ventures, aqua-preservation initiatives, and so on and so forth and what have you.
The glass elevator takes Lena up to the second floor where the offices of Pluto Enterprises are to be found. Contrary to its name, it has nothing to do with the cosmos. In other words, just the generic, run-of-the-mill company in the Northwestern United States.
They do...stuff. Needless to say, no kids are gonna be job-shadowing their parents, older siblings, guardians, or relatives if said adults work in Pluto Enterprises. But they do pay their employees...decently.
There is far more to the surface than meets the eye.
Lena steps out of the elevator and walks over to the office space, which is located on the end of a long, narrow, and carpeted hallway. The aircon hums and cools the area to an agreeable temperature.
Large plexiglass windows allow sunlight to pour in. Several potted plants are neatly positioned along the expanse of the corridor. Dome-shaped surveillance cameras are positioned in such a way that nothing evades (or is even capable of evading) their line of sight.
The interior is decorated with various abstract- but tasteful- pieces of artwork. The walls are almost a duller shade of Lena's coat.
Security, for some reason, is taken rather seriously. An armed security guard (not of the rent-a-cop variety) runs a security wand over Lena's body, making sure that she isn't carrying any suspicious objects.
She agreeably complies but can't help but occasionally ask herself why such a mundane company would go through such elaborate measures. Hell, the NDAs that she had to sign were enough to set off more than two dozen alarm bells in her head.
Once the security guard is satisfied that Lena is in fact not a threat to the company, he gives her a nod, a practiced smile, and ushers her inside. Her purse slides across the Xray scanner and then into a retractable tray.
Lena slings it back over her shoulder, nods at the guard, and walks over to the sliding door. It gently hisses open once the green light turns on, revealing a bustling workspace.
A decent number of people are seated at their respective desks. An equal amount of them go to and fro, carrying armfuls of printouts and papers, walking off to do last-minute checks, grab a non-alcoholic drink, or have a quick smoke break.
Lena takes off her coat, revealing an off-white dress shirt underneath. She hangs it up and saunters over to her desk. She sits down on the squeaky maroon chair, its wheels slowly beginning to rotate.
She grabs her mouse, taps a few buttons to wake up the computer, and logs into her account. Her password is routinely changed by the people in IT. She receives an auto-deleting text message wherein she is ordered to memorize her password for the next thirty days.
This time around, it's "Pandas^TwoEight%$" (without the quotes).
Her fingers glide over the keyboard as she carefully types in her password. The monitor displays the company's homepage- a plain, dull, boring website that, admittedly, works just as intended. Her desktop background is fairly plain and usually on rotate.
This time, when she tabs out of the homepage, she's greeted by a picture of a sun-soaked vineyard somewhere in Italy along with a little factoid explaining the significance of that photo.
It's eight thirty in the morning. Work usually begins at eight forty-five, with a five-minute leeway given in case of delays.
The coffee machine on the far wall whirrs to life, spitting out a dark, black cup of joe. Lena's ears perk up at the whirring sound of the coffee machine. She...has a thing for coffee.
She swivels her chair, gets up, and walks over to the coffee machine. She gives it a once-over and ensures that the machine is filled, untampered with, and working properly.
Lena shall be paying this machine a visit more than five times during the duration of her workday.
Her job at the office is...eh. It pays the rent, bills, food provisions, cleaning supplies, and just enough left over for the occasional want.
For whatever reason, all of the cabinets here are biometrically locked. The only access-point that exists for this is a fingerprint scanner. Lena's thumb puts some pressure on the pad, and she watches the doors pop open.
Lena's job description is something along the lines of office clerk and admin assistant. Not glam or fancy by any stretched-up stretch of the stretchy imaginations!
But, to Lena, the paycheck is what counts at the end of the day. Her labor does sorta count as "skilled", not like the distinction ever had any genuine meaning in the first place- just another justification for the bourgeoisie to arbitrarily categorize and exploit people.
And this is precisely the sort of workplace where exploitation is...silent and unspoken of. Lena had to sign a pledge to not join any unions (or even commit the dastardly thought crime of thinking about joining up with one), lest she's subjected to termination without severance pay.
Lena puts a stack of papers away in a sliding drawer, sighs, and grabs her first cuppa joe for the long day ahead. The workday officially ends at four forty-five in the afternoon- a good eight hours!
But the clock-watchers are already...eyeing the clocks with their baggy eyes. Like hawks, they refuse to blink as the big hand steadily ticks away. Ticktock, ticktock.
When the alarm on Lena's phone goes off, she lets out a soft yawn and stretches her arms out. She blinks many times and averts her gaze from the monitor, rubbing her eyes with her hands.
Lunchtime! That's why she set the alarm on her phone; she even set up a reminder on her computer. In case she's taking a call or forgets, the other helpful reminder will surely swoop in and save her from overworking and exerting herself.
Loathe as she might be to admit it, she does tend to push herself a bit too much.
There are plenty of small, cozy restaurants located on the ground floor, and Lena is on speaking terms with the majority of them. While she's not exactly a regular (sometimes, she brings lunch from home), she's known as a decent patron.
She walks past the joint that ONLY serves sushi. Despite being part Japanese herself, Lena can't stand the smell, texture, or feel of raw fish. And if she's on a date, she's just about to ready to walk the fuck out if the potential date drags her along to get Japanese food.
No hard feelings, but she's just not a fan. But her ethnicity has gotten her into one too many awkward and sticky situations than she cares to recount.
Lena goes for some pasta. Sautéed vegetables and two pieces of garlic bread accompany the main dish. She wraps her fork around the noodles, brings it up to her mouth, and begins to chew, slowly and carefully.
Nothing outta the ordinary, just a regular, boring meal that's sure to set her up for a satisfying evening- that's when the real fun begins.
She is, however, a bit concerned. What did her mother mean by "what really goes on" out here?
Lena has been living in Magnolia City ever since graduating from college. Hell, her first ever job was as an intern in the very same Pluto Enterprises; so, she's not only been with the company for quite a while, but a significant portion of her adult life has also been spent living in this city.
Lena asks for the check, pays the exact amount- nay a penny more- and leaves. She doesn't bother with tipping, believing it to be a useless gesture. A pointless attempt at papering over the pitiful pay of front-of-the-house restaurant staff.
Lena washes her hands, wipes them with the tissues, scrunches them up, and tosses them away in the appropriate garbage bin. She adjusts her hair, redoes her ponytail, and walks back out into the lobby.
Back upstairs, the guards nod and greet her, and she plainly returns the gesture without a smile on her face. She looks down at her phone and sees that there's still a few more hours to go till she's outta here.
The afternoon rolls by without any huge, fussy fusses being made. As soon as the sun sets, the sky takes on a purplish-orange color. It's just a little past five forty-five- girl shoulda been out of here an hour ago!
Lena groans, clicks her tongue, shuts off her computer, and trudges over to the coat rack. She slips into it with a practiced ease and heads out. She walks down the hallway, steps into the elevator, and taps the button for the lobby.
A cup of coffee warms her hands. The liquid is hot, sweet, and just the way she likes it. Lena likes her coffee in any way, just as long as it's coffee. Milk, no milk. Sugar, no sugar. Black, whatever. Americano, Espresso, this-cano, that-presso. Coffee is coffee and Lena is a sucker for it.
The elevators ding, and she steps out into the lobby.
Now, onto her side...gig. The hip kids these days call it a "side hustle".
Well, whatever the hell it's called, Lena's side thang is way more exciting than the dull and monotonous grind of her day job. She sits on a round, upholstered bench, folds her legs, and takes a moment to think things over.
And to get a bit of rest. This gig of hers is...demanding and kinda risky, too. But she happens to be quite good at it.
Once the coffee cup is drained, Lena stares into the emptiness therein and shakes her head, "Could use another cup." She tosses the cup away and sashays out of the lobby.
The sky above is slowly darkening. A warm breeze wafts throughout the busy streets of Magnolia City. People are everywhere, all of them headed to some place or the other.
Lena proceeds to walk along the usual route- her "office" is actually quite close to her main place of employment, so that's convenient for her. Not that she necessarily likes that, but it sorta saves up on the commute.
She walks at a brisk pace, keeping her head down and her hands shoved deep into her coat pockets.
The brownstone building isn't as tall as the place where Pluto Enterprises is located. Moreover, it's starting to show its age in certain aspects. There are many cracks along the lower portions of the exterior walls.
The air inside is musty, a tad muggy, and stinks of old-ass paint. But the rent required for Lena's office is on the...cheaper side of things. The elevator is out of order.
Lena rolls her eyes and trudges up the stairs. Her little, humble office is located on the fifth floor, and her feet take her there without any hesitation. The coffee that she's been drinking certainly helps!
The interior- much like the exterior- is also showing its age. Water damage runs across the walls, the carpet is frightfully discolored, and the lights are flickering.
Oddly enough, many amateur filmmakers come to this location to film their nineteen-forties noir films. The aesthetic is quite fitting, considering that this building hasn't really been seriously touched since then!
The text on the door simply reads: "LENA ADAMS | PRIVATE INVESTIGATOR" in black block letters and bold print. She's been meaning to get it changed when a person had some difficulty reading the letters, citing certain corneal issues.
Yep, she's a private investigator. A PI. A snoop. A sleuth. A gumshoe. But even after all these years, Lena is still an amateur at it.
But all that was about to change tonight. Soon and very soon, she shall be dragged into a world that she never even knew existed.
A world of secrets. A world...of darkness.
Lena opens the door and heads inside. Her cheeks puff as she exhales sharply and takes off her coat. She hangs it on the coat rack, removes the red scrunchie from her hair, tosses her hair around, and runs her fingers through her scalp.
She lets her hair hang loose and flips the light switch on. She blinks a couple of times and lets her eyes adjust.
A messy oak desk dominates a majority of this relatively tiny space. Rickety filing cabinets (sans fingerprint lock, heh-heh), stacks of overflowing boxes, a few wooden chairs, and fewer assorted potted plants are what the interior consists of.
Lena pours a half-empty bottle of water into the plants sitting by the windowsill. Their leaves look a bit yellowish, withered, and lackluster.
"Ah, shit. I haven't been taking care of them, haven't I? Oh, well..."
Lena shrugs and plops herself down onto the brown leather chair- a far cry from that squeaky maroon chair, that's for sure! She spins around a few times, kicks her feet up on the desk, and leans back, closing her eyes.
She flatly tells herself, "Any second now..."
Many seconds later...
The warm wind wafts in through the open window and causes the rainbow pride flag on her desk to flutter. Her ears do not pick up on any footsteps or anything of the like. The main office door doesn't even creak open.
Lena sighs, wiggles her toes, and slumps deeper into the chair.
She doesn't have any appointments today- this whole thing is very much a one-woman operation. She seeks the potential clientele out (either through word-of-mouth or advertising her services on websites like Facebook and Craigslist) and does her duties accordingly.
Lena gathers the info, writes up all the contracts, and sends the client on their merry way...or in tears when she has to confirm the worst by telling the client that their spouse is indeed having that affair.
She has been doing this for some time now, so this routine of hers has become somewhat predictable. The DSLR camera sitting on one of her filing cabinets is still powered up and ready to go, though.
Lena opens her eyes for a moment, briefly surveys the area, and shuts them back. She allows her muscles to relax, but not so much so that she's sleeping. Caffeine still surges through her veins.
And she'd never ever consider skipping her nightly cup of coffee.
A little over many seconds later...
Lena pokes her head out of her humble office and narrows her eyes. The other spaces rented out on this floor are occupied by varying small businesses that put the "small" in small business.
Since there's no place to fit a photocopy machine in her office, Lena has an arrangement with the dingy office a few paces away from her. Unlike hers, it has been done up with bead curtains and colorful, shiny things that catch the eye.
Mr. Troy Porter runs said office along with his wife of fifty years. Troy is an old African American man with frizzy gray hair and droopy eyes. He's slim and often dresses in khakis with the checkered shirt tucked in.
His business has something to do with the distribution and manufacturing of water purifiers- the industrial kind and not the fancy home and garden stuff.
Troy greets Lena, "Lena...here to chat or use my copy machine? Not that I don't mind, but you don't got no clients right now, so why not?" He turns his head down and peers at her through his thick-rimmed spectacles.
Lena shrugs, "Sure. How's your wife doing, Troy?"
Troy chuckles, "I'm lookin' forward to the next fiddy with that woman by my side."
While the printers at work are ten times fancier (laser!), Troy's aging inkjet printer will have to suffice. Moreover, Pluto Enterprises expressly forbids employees from utilizing company property for personal and/or recreational use. Even if it is an emergency, PE employees are prohibited from using company equipment.
Troy's office is also quite cozy and warm, which Lena doesn't mind.
He mixes a cup of tea with a bent spoon and asks, "So, how's the PI game these days?"
Lena dryly answers, "Still the same old, same old. Affairs take up almost all of my cabinet space."
"A damn shame, all that infidelity and such. 'Bout as bad as a malevolent foot fungus, I'd say."
Lena is not really one to pass moral judgment around like that. A job's a job and she gets paid (somewhat handsomely but not quite) to do what she does. The clients come in with their money and problems, the PI does her snooping, she gets paid, they usually leave (relieved or teary-eyed) and that's it.
Professionals are said to have standards, and Lena Adams is very much a professional. She also maintains some necessary distance (emotional or otherwise) from her work.
Troy goes on, "So, what was that you said when we first met? PI for the...?"
As if by reflex, Lena's lips form into a thin, barely noticeable smile, "A Private Investigator for the public good. That's...what I aspire to be. But I dunno, Troy. All I do is follow cheating spouses- alleged or otherwise- around with my DSLR, hide in bushes, and tail people. I don't do anything..."
Troy takes a sip of his tea and completes her thought, "More? Meaningful?"
"E--exactly!"
"Took the words outta your mouth, didn't I? Don't worry, you'll find something. One of these days, somebody's gonna come waltzing in through them doors and they're gonna need your help, girl. The question is..."
Lena leans closer and narrows her eyes a bit, "Hmm?"
Troy also leans closer, his wrinkled and weathered face contorting into a crooked grin, "Are you the right woman for the job? Will you be up to the task?"
Lena ponders over this, shrugs, and nods. Troy gives her an understanding smile and pats her on the back. He hobbles away to get another cup of tea for himself. He hollers out, "Anytime you need anything, just swing by. Hell, we practically neighbors."
Ms. Adams chuckles, shakes her head, and grabs the printouts from Troy's inkjet printer. Mostly legal stuff, licensing requirements, tax forms, and other such boring, dull paperwork that is all part and parcel of being a gumshoe in this city.
While perusing over these documents, Lena hears footsteps at the edge of the hallway. Her ears twitch as she lifts her head and sees a man in his early forties nervously standing outside.
He has a slightly bewildered expression on his face, his brow furrows, and he stammers, "Ah...Lena...Adams? PI?" The man speaks with a Southern twang that is hard to pinpoint.
"Yes, that would be me. And you are...?"
He takes off his red trucker cap, ruffles his greasy hair, and enters Lena's office. He sticks out his hand for Lena to shake. He's thin, wiry, and on the shorter side. His skin is pallid and there are really dark bags underneath his blue eyes.
Lena shakes his hand and motions for him to sit down. He does, placing his cap on his lap and rubbing the palms of his hands together. A nervous shiver runs down his spine as he looks around, his eyes scanning over the PI's small office.
The PI presents a calm, non-threatening, non-judgmental aura as she leans back into her own chair. She waits for him to make the necessary introduction and how he found out about her services.
The man stammers, "M--my name's Jake Woodward and I found out about you on uh...Craigslist."
"Ah. And how may I help you this evening, Mr. Woodward?"
"C--call me Jake, please. No need to be so formal. M--mind if I call you Lena?"
Lena shrugs, "Sure thing, whatever you're comfortable with. So, Jake, how may I help you this evening?"
Jake takes a deep breath; his hands incessantly tremble when he lets it out. He clasps them together and squeezes tightly. It is clear that this man is in a great deal of distress.
Lena side-eyes him, "Are you OK? Would you like me to get you a glass of water or coffee??"
Jake frantically shakes his head and politely declines. His pupils are dilated and his fingers twitch. He looks around her office one more time and averts his gaze. Lena patiently waits for him to continue speaking.
He gathers his composure and begins, "I'd like to hire you."
As a way of showing genuine interest, she leans forward and nods, "I'm all ears. What seems to be the problem, Jake?"
"My--my son, Tyler."
"OK? And is Tyler in some kind of trouble?"
Jake looks at Lena with his bloodshot eyes and says in a quivering voice, "No. He--he's dead."
Lena winces, "I'm sorry to hear that. How old was he?"
Jake pauses for a moment before he replies, "He was fourteen years old. It...it was a hit-and-run when he was walking home from school. MCPD treated it as an open-and-shut case."
Lena shakes her head, "That's terrible. What exactly happened?"
Jake scratches his chin, "The cops dropped the case so quickly. My wife was...devastated by how the authorities treated us like--like we were crooks or something. And I wasn't doing too good, myself. I'm a truck driver, so I'm out most weeks. We moved all the way from Tennessee to Magnolia City for my son's sake. But when...when he was killed, it really turned our--our whole world upside-down."
Lena's expression turns sympathetic, "I can imagine. How long ago was this, if you don't mind me asking?"
"A few weeks back. The MCPD treated us as suspects, insinuated that us country bumpkins from the South didn't raise our boy right, didn't teach him to look both ways before crossing--"
Tears freely flow down the bereaved father's cheeks. He chokes on his words, his lower lip quivers, and he clenches his jaw, "He--he...he was a very smart boy, Lena. Had a three-digit IQ, he did. That's why we moved here; a school in Tennessee couldn't provide him the kind of environment that he needed."
"I am truly sorry for your loss. Did the MCPD at least manage to track down the vehicle used in the hit-and-run?"
Jake shakes his head, "No, miss, they did not. And every second since our boy didn't come home from school, we--we were besides ourselves, my wife and I. And the hell that came after was...just beyond description. Cops kept questioning us, hounding us about what we know."
He adds, "My wife shrieked when she saw our boy in lying flat in that cold morgue. All banged up, bruised, broken, bloody and...gone. Autopsy said that he was run over and left on the street to die. Happened at night and on some road where them cameras don't have any coverage."
Lena feels that he has more to say.
The distressed man sniffs, "It gets worse. The authorities weren't just unhelpful- we're from Tennessee, so we have our fair share of that- they were hostile to us, Lena. My wife, she...couldn't handle it, could barely stand up straight after the cops left our house. They asked us all kinds of nasty, unpleasant questions and we tried our best to cooperate."
He stares at the rainbow pride flag on her desk, "She...was truly a sweet, sweet woman. I was sleeping- long day, y'know how it can be for truckers, heh. The power suddenly went off. It was late and I--I only got up cuz I heard some kinda electrical buzzing sound. But when I went to the bathroom..."
Jake found his wife in the fully filled (to the point of overflowing) bathtub, dead. She electrocuted herself with an appliance- hence the sudden power outage.
His lips tremble when he recalls, "Her--her eyes looked so glazed over. It's like the life itself had left them long before, Lena. Like--like she died even before she took such a drastic step to end things."
Lena clenches her jaw, "I'm really sorry to hear that. This is...oh my god, this is truly awful."
Jake softly mutters, "Thank you. I--I don't know if you have experience in investigating murders, but I wanna hire you to find out who did this. I'm not a vengeful or violent man. I just want answers, Lena. I just wanna find the bastard who ran my boy over and drove on without stopping to see what they'd done."
Jake places his sweaty palms on the table and gives her the most earnest stare that she's ever seen, "Please, help me find out what happened. I don't have much money- funeral expenses and all- but I WILL find a way to pay you."
Lena feels a tug on her heartstrings. Jake's tone of voice is full of emotion and there is something to it that she just can't deny or turn away from. The man is so beset by grief and loss that it shows through and through.
She takes a deep breath, "Alright, Jake, I'll take the case. And...I'm also waiving my usual fee."
Jake's disbelief shows clearly on his face, "No, Lena, I can't let you do that. I--I got a little, we could do installments, we could--"
Lena holds out her hand, palm facing the potential client, "Nuh-uh. I insist, please. And I have to admit something to you, it's only fair. This will be my first ever murder investigation. But I am a fully licensed, legit, and qualified professional PI in this city, and I've been doing this for quite some time now."
Jake nods and lets out a sigh of relief, "I understand. And...thank you for your honesty. So, how does this work, Lena? I tried gettin' the case files that the cops have, but for some reason, they're not interested in letting me see them."
Lena pats the table and says, "We'll start from the beginning, OK? By law, they should give you the case files. Them holding it back already sets off a few alarm bells- then again, it's the MCPD, so we can't exactly trust them to do the right thing here. I'll look into that first. In the meantime, I need to dig into the scene of the crime."
Jake gulps and coughs, "Anything you need. We tried gettin' a vigil set up near where Tyler was killed, but the permits still haven't come through...till now."
"I'm honestly not surprised by that. And tell me, how has your experience with the Magnolia City Police Department been?"
Jake sharply exhales, "About as bad and unpleasant as you'd expect. We told them everything- when Tyler was supposed to be back home, if he was running late, what he had for lunch, if he was involved in any kinda things that he shouldn't be involved in..."
Lena clears her throat, "I have to ask this- it's a part of my job- but do you think that there's anybody who might've had it out for your son and wanted to hurt him? A kid his age, someone else from school, a relation of his, his teachers, his classmates?"
Jake is visibly shaken when he replies, "I--I dunno. No! We've only been living here a few months, barely got the time to know anyone, let alone get into a fight or anything. The only kids that he had any interaction with were at his school. They were geniuses like him, but they were good company, good kids."
"I see. Did your son frequently walk home from school or did you drive him?"
Jake shrugs, "Walked, mostly. My wife drove him whenever she could. But he loved waking up early in the morning and...walking to school."
He wistfully smiles a little and puts in, "It's Magnolia City. Beautiful place to raise a smart boy. We wanted to get him his metro card so he could take the fancy subway trains and all that. He was looking forward to that...we all were."
Lena queries, "Do you happen to know the detective assigned to the case?"
Jake scratches his head, "Gram...uh...something. I dunno, Lena. It's been...hectic. Having to bury both my wife and son within such a short span of time really...took its toll on me. We're trying to keep Tyler's passing private; I don't want or need those vultures in the media getting wind of this."
Lena groans when she hears who the investigating detective might be, "Oh, him. Great. Yeah, you've got a point there, Jake. Once the media gets a whiff of something as troubling as the death of a child, they'll do their best to exploit this, spin this in all kinds of fucked up directions and..."
She trails off, not wanting to finish that sentence.
Jake nods along, "Mm-hmm. I just want my family to have some shred of dignity, y'know? So, you'll do it, then? F--free of charge? Are you sure, I mean--"
Lena equably says, "I'm doing it for free. After all, I'm a Private Investigator for the public good. And it's my first ever murder investigation, so charging you would be unfair to the both of us. You've...already suffered enough loss, Jake. I don't wanna burden you with any more unnecessary expenses."
Jake wipes his face, sniffles, and profusely thanks her again. He gets up and promises to send everything over to Lena as soon as he returns home- he doesn't have much in the way of info and details, but he says that he'll manage to get some more for her so that she can begin investigating.
He departs with the promise to send her some vital stuff by email. Lena's email ID is on the same Craigslist page that he found her through, so that should make things easier for the both of them.
Lena shakes her head and forces out a harsh sigh, "God. A kid. Sheesh. I better not screw this up..." She leans back into her chair, shuts her eyes, and spins around for a moment before standing up and walking over to the window.
She watches Jake put on his cap and walk away with a hunched posture. The warm summer wind wafts through and blows Lena's jet-black hair around.
Ms. Adams looks up at the dark sky. Her amber eyes linger on the higher buildings obscuring the stars. She purses her lips and decides to pay someone a quick visit.
She mumbles to herself, "Tyler from Tennessee. Tennessee Tyler. I'll have to draw up a contract, get the paperwork done, and do some more research. But I'm gonna need some advice on this one. My first ever murder investigation...not at all what I was expecting."
Lena stops and clicks her tongue, "And there I go, monologuing like a fucking noir detective. C'mon, break out of the mold. I'm better than this. I have to be if I wanna help Jake catch his son's killer."
She snaps her fingers and promptly sets out to get her coffee cup filled. After that, she locks up her office, heads downstairs, and out into the streets. Time to pay an old friend a visit!
Mere minutes later...
Lena finds herself standing in front of a condominium located in a more...quaint area. This particular condo is a lot smaller than the ones situated around it. It goes up for about a dozen floors, with four condos per floor. Not that it matters much, anyway. She's not here for the view.
Or to find a place to rent.
This is where her mentor- Michael Hayes- resides.
The eco-brutalist structure was completed a decade ago. The condos are located close enough to the waterfront to allow for some decent views of the city's skyline.
The PI jabs her index finger on the intercom button, "Michael. You in?"
His voice crackles out of the speaker, "Lena Adams. I'll buzz you up. This is a pleasant surprise!" His accent is familiarly Midwestern. He's had this tone to his voice since she first met him and was under his tutelage. And he's still the same man that she remembers after all these years.
Michael's joint is located on the second floor. Lena's energetic enough (all that coffee) to take the stairs- two at a time- up to his door. She knocks and the door opens almost immediately.
A man in his early sixties, with grayish, white-flecked, messy hair, a clean-shaven face, and a bright smile, welcomes her into his home. He's dressed in a red robe tied tightly around his waist.
Like the exterior of this condominium complex, the interior has a very Art Deco aesthetic to it. Michael's taste for the avant-garde and modern is apparent as his decor consists of old-world chic- the styles compliment each other well enough. The overall look is one that makes Lena feel...safe.
Michael sits on a long loveseat and languidly gestures for his former mentee to sit wherever she likes.
She takes off hear coat and sits directly opposite him on a single seater.
Michael relaxes and begins, "And to what do I owe the pleasure of this visit, hmm? A case bothering you and you need my advice?"
"Uh-huh. You could say that. Got a walk-in this evening- found me on Craigslist. Son got murdered, so I took the case- free of charge since it's only fair. My first ever murder investigation."
Michael is impressed, "Well, look at you. Finally breaking out of the rut of sordid affairs. Be honest with me, Lena. Did you really like following adulterous- allegedly or otherwise- spouses around, snapping pics on that DSLR of yours, and tailing them?"
She faintly chuckles, "Not really. But I have to make rent and pay my bills, you know? It's a job- like any other. And I do it well enough."
The soft and low lighting from the hanging lamp fixtures is soothing.
Lena adds, "It's not just any murder, you know? Kid got ran over. Cops came in, shrugged, moved on, and said that they're done with the investigation. Fuckers, tch. Dad's in a pretty bad shape. You know me, I don't like taking advantage of decent people in a time of need, so I offered my help for free."
Michael smiles a little, "That's what I've always liked about you. PI for the public good. It's a helluva tagline, haha! I think I might've heard something about what you're looking into. I think it was reported on a local radio station. Sad."
He gets up, pours himself a drink, and offers one to Lena. She politely declines. He sits back down with a slight groan and wiggles himself into a more comfortable position.
She goes on, "It is a sad case. A tragedy. I just hope that I don't let the client down. I have to find out who did this and ensure that some form of justice is served."
Michael takes a light sip of his scotch, "And you came here for advice. First time's always gonna be a bit rocky- you don't wanna bite off more than you can chew. OK, let's talk specifics. If you're comfortable with it."
Lena nods, "OK. You're a PI, you've been in this for a lot longer than I have. I get the gist of what I have to do when investigating a murder, but..."
"But you don't have the hands-on experience."
"Mm-hmm. All my hands-on experience is just affairs and kids asking me to find their crap- toys, rare collector's items, dolls, action figures, comic books, ugh...it's so...never mind. This is all new to me. And it's a pretty big case. Fucking it up isn't an option here."
Michael reassures her, "Don't worry, you won't. All you have to do is remember everything I ever taught you and every lesson I ever taught you. You've been studying, reading, practicing, and training- and I think that's proven to be quite effective."
He further asks, "How does your paperwork look? All good? Up to date? Ready to go?"
Lena nods and answers, "Yeah, pretty much."
Michael takes another small sip of his booze, "And how's the day job treating you?"
Lena couches her chin on her palm, "It's what I've been doing since graduation. It keeps me going, pays the bills, and seems alright. I'm good at it and Pluto Enterprises values my experience. The pay could be a little bit better, but that's nothing new."
He points at her, "Ask for a promotion, a raise, or both. You've been with 'em long enough to warrant something."
She shrugs a shoulder, "I'll try."
Mr. Hayes sets his glass aside and regards his guest, "I'm not a mind reader, Lena. Something's on your mind- that's why you came here. But unless you talk to me, I can't help you."
"Hmm. It's just that...I wanna help my client. I don't wanna let him down, accidentally pin the blame on the wrong people, or--or fuck anything else up."
"Mm-hmm. Don't worry, you won't. If my mentoring is any indication of what your talent is like, then I know for sure that you'll pull through." The reassuring grin on his face is the epitome of confidence.
Lena mirthlessly chuckles, "Easy for you to say. I'm the one who has to investigate, solve the case, and--and what's the worst-case scenario, huh? We're talking about a kid here, Michael. A dead kid. The police have already closed the case and walked away without bothering to learn a single fucking thing about who did this horrible, heinous, and unforgivable atrocity."
Michael mulls some things over before speaking, "I'm not disagreeing with you. Now, when it comes to murders like these..."
By the end of it all, Lena is given a decent boost of confidence in her ability to handle the case. Michael Hayes has a knack for knowing the right things to say- even if that means telling it to her unvarnished.
He advises her to dig into things, visit the scene of the crime, and THOROUGHLY go over the details that have been given with the finest of fine-tooth combs.
Mr. Hayes gulps down his second glass of booze and smacks his lips, "Ah. That's the stuff. So, there ya go. Y'know, even if you hadn't dropped by to get my advice, you'd have gone ahead and done everything that I just told you. I taught you everything I know about being a PI and unleashed you on the world."
Lena responds, "That's only because you yourself were the best. How's retirement treating you these days?"
The retired PI shrugs, "I still teach people like you the ins and outs of this...I don't wanna call it a game, Lena. I hate that word with a passion. And calling it a business is just as bad, just as crass. It's a calling, a--a duty, a...necessity. Especially in this joint!"
Since Lena hasn't seen him in a long time, she inquires, "Are you still getting people signing up to learn from you and all that? And are you still taking cases?"
Michael painfully winces when he shuffles his lower body a bit, "Yes. And no. Well, I still get referrals every now and then. As for actual cases, someone tried to hire me a few days ago, but I turned 'em down. I'm out of that particular line of work. Hey, maybe I'll send 'em your way, huh?"
"What's the case?"
"A string of grisly murders. You're not getting any more outta me, heh. No offense."
"None taken. And how's everything else going? How's the..." Lena vaguely gestures in the direction of his lower body.
Michael responds, "All the more reason for me to stop doing what I kinda liked doing. Retirement's for people in their seventies. If I hadn't gotten shot by those corporate goons, you bet your ass I'd have kept going."
"How is your health? How's the pain?"
"Eh, I manage. Painkillers and booze. Friend of mine recommended vodka and aspirin. Holy shit, it's like going on a trip...and I was born in the fifties and came of age in the seventies, so I know what a crazy trip looks like."
Lena chuckles, "Don't go overboard."
Michael was a damned good PI. But after getting embroiled in something to do with corporate malfeasance and white-collar crime, he got blasted in the gut and left for dead. The bullet went straight through and exited through the back.
It was through sheer luck that he survived- and even more miraculous that he didn't end up in a coma, a wheelchair, or anything of the sort. The medical bills were horrendous, and it took a good chunk of his savings to cover the expenses.
(American healthcare...wherein the large numbers on the paper are more than powerful to give a person a heart attack. For a supposedly "developed" nation to have such archaic and outdated systems is nothing short of a shameful, disgraceful travesty).
But in the end, Mr. Hayes got the bastards and in a rare case of justice being served, watched as they all were sentenced to life without any possibility of parole. Attempted murder, conspiracy to commit murder, financial-related crimes, and a myriad of other charges.
Nowadays, Michael spends his time mentoring, nurturing, and honing the skills of a new generation of gumshoes. Lena is just one of his many students.
The retired PI stands up with a groan and steadies himself. He gives Lena a hearty pat on the back, "Alright, off ya go. Solve the case, find the sicko who ran that poor kid over, and then you'll be able to rest easy. Don't worry, you'll do fine."
"I hope so."
"Any more hobbies, anything else occupying your time other than being a PI? You meet anyone yet? By the way, I think I might know a...few lucky ladies who'd be interested. Or are you still swiping on those app things? Hey, no judging from me, but you can't just be a PI and an office drone forever."
Lena blushes a little, "I--I have hobbies and other things, Michael. But these...ladies, are they friends of yours? Students of yours?"
"They're a bit older than you are, but you'd definitely hit it off. I could send you their contact info and all. Interested?"
Ms. Adams smirks, "I could be, yeah. I gotta head out now. You got everything that you need? Uh...just don't overdo the alcohol, hmm?"
Michael quips, "Yes, Dr. Adams."
She shakes her head at him, grabs her coat, puts it back on, and strides out after giving her mentor a hug.
The retired PI watches her leave and shuts the door behind him. He hobbles back to the loveseat and plops down into it with a painful groan. He picks up his glass and looks at it, swirls the booze around, and takes another sip.
After the eventful meeting and advice session, Lena drops by the same coffee shop from earlier in the morning. The hand-drawn sign reads: "GROUND BEANZ" in bold, friendly lettering.
Even at this hour of the dark night, a few customers sit and sip on their coffees and drink tea, the coffee beans and the smell of freshly baked goodies wafts through the air.
Many office-looking types have their MacBooks on the tables. They do some last-minute work or finish up some important assignments.
Lena is not one of them. She leaves her work at her...workplace. Gotta find a way to have that work-life balance somehow.
Ms. Adams is still an amateur when it comes to being a PI, but that was about to change in the coming days. For now, she sits at a single round table and rubs her face. Her fingers brush over her hair.
She is so very, very tired. And as usual, she orders a large cup of coffee and something to chow down on. The subway ride back home was quite an ordeal, so she needs to replenish her energy.
Lena devours what counts as her dinner, downs all of the coffee in a single go, pays the bill (no tip), and exits the place. She's glad that they're open so late.
But the employees of said place still have that weary look on their faces, their feet ache horribly, so...
On the journey back home, Lena walks by plenty of stoops and multi-storied houses that are practically glued together. The trees that cover the neighborhood are still blossoming.
Magnolia City has a population of about five-hundred thousand. Half a million, which is fairly decent.
Kudos where kudos is due- Magnolia City's planners cared more about people- human beings- and not profit margins or a false sense of aesthetics. Lena passes by plenty of bike lanes, wheelchair-friendly spots, and other nifty little things that make life bearable for the less fortunate, differently abled, and those who may have some or the other trouble getting around.
The metro also (usually) runs on time, so there's that, too.
Lena herself lives in a single-story house in a working-class suburb. It's medium-sized, has a nice porch, and is on a corner. A little farther away from the city center, but not too much so.
Sturdy trees and walkable paths make it a decent place to live in.
Lena goes up the steps, fishes her housekeys out of her coat pocket, and unlocks the front door. On the inside, the floors are made out of wood, the wall is painted white, and there are various potted plants throughout.
The granite kitchen island and the dining table are well-used, and the appliances are decent enough for her immediate needs. She flips a light switch on, bathing the entire house in bright, white, fluorescent lights.
The curtains are a dark shade of mauve. Lena's got a few framed photos of her family on the wall- mainly of her father, mother, and brother. Other than that, she has some pictures of her graduation ceremony. She went to Indiana State University.
Her shiny black graduation robes complimented her jet-black hair. But she had a bit of an acne situation back then. Her father and mother flank her, beaming with pride. Her brother ducked in front of her, wildly smiling and giving a double thumbs-up to the camera.
Lena has a nice and cozy bedroom, a well-used couch, a desk, a small but well-equipped workstation, a few shelves full of books, and a desktop computer.
The queen-sized bed is covered with a dark-red bedspread, a white duvet, and numerous fluffy pillows. Her closet contains an assortment of garments- both casual and professional.
While she slips out of her clothing, a call comes through on her mobile phone.
Jake's voice is on the other end of the line, all shaky and nervous, "M--Miss Adams? Lena? Hello?"
"I'm here, Jake. What can I do for you?"
"Well...are you busy? I just need a moment of your time. By the way, I sent whatever I could scrounge together to your uh...email address. Did you receive it?"
Lena sits on the edge of her bed, "I just got home, but I'm sure it'll be there. Don't worry."
Jake gulps and asks, "I know that this is short notice, but I was hoping to invite you to Tyler's wake. We--we're having it tomorrow. It's mostly a small thing- some family from Tennessee, a few close friends of Ty's, and some of his teachers. We wanted to keep it...small, simple, and quiet."
"Oh. I see. And what time is this?"
"Six in the evening. Given that you're gonna be investigating my son's murder, it only feels appropriate for me to extend this invitation to you, Lena. I genuinely hope that this isn't too short of a notice? I'm not trying to be pushy or anything, but I'd very much appreciate your presence."
She ponders over this for a moment and plainly answers, "Alright. I'll be there. Absolutely. I get off work at four forty-five. Text me the address and I'll be there. How long will the wake last?"
Jake mumbles a little, "It's uh...not for long. About an hour, hour-and-a-half, tops. We just wanna get together and remember Tyler, pay our respects, maybe even give you an...um...opportunity to talk to whoever might be able to offer you some information."
Lena nods, "That sounds like a great idea, but I don't wanna step over anyone's toes or turn Tyler's wake into some kind of interrogation session. I'll be there. Is there a dress code, by any chance?"
"Nuthin' like that, don't worry. I--I'll text you the address."
Lena runs a hand through her hair and ruffles it a bit, "Sure thing. Goodnight, Jake."
Jake sniffles and shakily replies, "G--goodnight, Lena. And thank you for--for taking this case on."
He hangs up and Lena is left with her thoughts. She turns on her computer and logs onto her PI-exclusive email account. Whatever Mr. Woodward sent over isn't much, but it's a good enough start.
For now, however, she is spent. The PI unclasps her bra, lets it drop onto the ground, and heads for a quick shower. The warm water washes away the accumulated stress, grime, and exhaustion.
She steps out, dries herself off, slips into her PJs, and flops down on her bed. Lena shuts her eyes, breathes out, and soon, gets lost in her dreams. While sleeping, not a single snore escapes her.
But that dream, that dream...comes for her again and again. Ms. Adams can't quite tell when it started, but it's been a part of her for so very, very long now.
In this dream, Lena is plunged into a bottomless ocean. She is perpetually sinking and cannot swim back up. The sky is blue, cloudy, and unreachable. There is no land and there is no way out. Before all the sounds muffle, the only noticeable sound is the splashing noise being made whenever something (or someone) pushes her into the water.
There are no boats, no planes, and no helicopters.
Oddly enough, she has no drowning sensation whatsoever. Lena just keeps sinking and sinking and sinking while the water rushes around her, waves splash and wash against her skin and even enter her open mouth and nostrils.
She is drowning but also not drowning. But she is sinking deeper and deeper and deeper...and then she wakes up.
The first thing that she sees is that she is in her bed, her duvet covers her up to the chin. Rays of the early-morning sun stream through her curtains and illuminate the room.
She turns her head and glances at her alarm clock. Lena blinks tightly, rubs her eyes, grunts softly, and gets out of bed.
It's time to do the same thing all over again! The warm water of the shower cascades down her naked body. She scrubs her skin with her favorite soap- some kind of flower-scented stuff- and shampoos her hair. She skips out on the conditioner.
After drying herself off, Lena dons a pair of pleated pants, a beige button-down shirt, and a pair of black pumps. As for makeup, it's simply some purple lipstick, concealer, eyeliner, and an extra layer of foundation.
Ms. Adams does her makeup every morning, just like she does her hair. Accessorizing involves some necklaces, bracelets, earrings, and a shiny brooch.
The employee of Pluto Enterprises grabs her stuff, locks up, and heads out. The rays of the sun are only beginning to warm things up. The streets are still a bit cold and covered in a thin layer of fog.
Lena swings by GROUND BEANZ for breakfast- bagels and two cups of coffee, one to have here and one to go. She takes a seat and reads through the morning news on her mobile phone.
The walk to Crystal Street Station is brief. Lena heaves a sigh of relief when the train arrives on time. The subway rattles and shakes a little, but that's sort of expected. There's a person with crutches sitting on the seat, and the rest of the train is mostly packed, so Lena grabs onto the pole.
When the train reaches its next stop, Ms. Adams exits and walks out onto the street.
Magnolia City is a Democrat stronghold through and through- it's been like that for as long as anyone can remember. It also has a functioning(ish) metro system which connects the whole, entire city together.
Such things are very rare in car-centric USA! Sadly, the underground, rapid transit subway infrastructure has been falling to disrepair these past few years, and the indifferent Democratic leadership refuses to allocate the necessary funds and resources to fix things up- it's not even that much!
(This is, of course, a major problem because there are some places- specifically, in the inner-city- where the metro is a lifeline to many. The lack of decent maintenance and timely repairs is a big part of the reason why the place is slowly destined for a total collapse).
Why can't we have nice, meaningful, and long-lasting things?
Lena does ask herself as much while walking into her place of work. She sharply exhales while sitting down in front of her work computer. For now, she puts those burning queries to the back of her mind and gets busy with her day job.
While eating her lunch (a veggie sandwich with a side of coleslaw), she goes through whatever information Jake had sent her in regard to the case.
The victim's father was a truck driver. Fourteen-year-old Tyler was walking home from school (he was taking extra classes) when he was struck by a motor vehicle (presumably a car) and left to bleed out on the sidewalk.
No cams were able to pick up on the vehicle's license plate- the police said that this was a hit-and-run and left it at that. The MCPD's Head Detective (who Lena is familiar with, unfortunately) said that the case was closed and that the culprit was likely to be some drifter or junkie.
The MCPD also has no further evidence, no other leads, no suspects, and no established motive.
Lena mutters to herself, "It's like they don't even care. Or they just don't wanna know anything. It's a fucking murder investigation- and I sure as hell going to dig deeper if they aren't gonna. Fuck those pricks. Fuck 'em and fuck their stupid, useless, and utterly incompetent department!"
(There's a reason why Lena Adams harbors such a hostility towards the coppers. Many reasons, actually).
The remainder of the afternoon goes by in a blur of paperwork, file management (both online and offline), grabbing printouts, and futzing around with spreadsheets.
A few people at the company have the good sense to actually ask for a promotion and a raise, but no dice, sadly. And there is a clause in Lena's contract which EXPRESSLY FORBIDS discussions (casual or otherwise) related to salary, compensation, raises, bonuses, incentives, etcetera amongst Pluto Enterprises employees.
It's all about the numbers to Pluto Enterprises...and whoever they ultimately answer to.
Oh, yeah, there's far more to Pluto Enterprises (PE) than meets the eye- a lot more. This particular subsidiary of a larger corporation (whose name shall be known soon) is in a very, very different field altogether.
A field not quite...of this world, one could say. But Lena, bless her beating heart, isn't privy to that.
Yet.
By four forty-six, Lena springs out of her supposedly "ergonomic" chair and heads out of the building. A chilly wind blows around her and makes her shiver a little. The sun shines down on the asphalt, casting a golden sheen over everything.
The sky is a deep and inviting shade of blue. White clouds streak the sky like cotton.
Lena doesn't own a car and doesn't intend to- the robust public transportation is more than sufficient for all of her mobility-related needs. Magnolia City is also quite walkable, and the important spaces aren't that far away from Lena's residence.
Moreover, there is an abundance of "third spaces" throughout this city- art galleries, libraries, museums, parks, botanical gardens, and many other places- that have become a part of her daily (and nightly) routine.
Ms. Adams has an hour to spare before Tyler's wake, so she mills about and stops by a flower shop. The old woman behind the counter- a kindly, grandmotherly type- greets her with a toothy smile.
She spends a few bucks on some flowers- white roses and pink carnations- and then heads to the wake. The trams on the streets are packed, the air is full of exhaust, and the stink of gasoline hangs heavy in the atmosphere.
Lena finds a seat, huddles a bit into her coat, and shuts her eyes while the vehicle rattles and shakes its way through the city. Within minutes, she's half a block away from Jake's home address.
"Well, here goes nothing."
Lena takes a deep breath, smooths out the wrinkles in her clothing, checks to see that her face looks OK, and heads up the steps leading to the porch.
She clasps the flowers tightly and rings the doorbell. The faint sound of conversing can be heard from within the duplex suburban house. It's a quiet neighborhood. Leaves gently rustle in the trees. A faint breeze blows.
The front door opens and an old man stands there, looking at Lena with an unreadable expression on his face. His Southern accent is harsh and unmistakable when he asks, "Can I help you?"
Lena replies, "I'm here for the wake, I'm uhm..."
Jake gently pushes the old feller aside and ushers her inside. All eyes immediately fall on Lena when she enters. She gives the flowers to the bereaved man, nods at the old man, and finds a seat in the corner.
Jake himself is dressed in a fine black suit, dark-blue tie, and shiny shoes. Most of the other people present are dressed somewhat identically. Some of the older men wear bolo ties.
The women wear wide-brimmed hats, ankle-length summer dresses with very muted color schemes, and gloves. The teens and younger kids are clad in jeans, button-up shirts, and denim jackets.
The living room is rather spacious and has been arranged for a wake. Tyler's portrait takes up a large portion of the wall. On the other side, a wide table has been spread out with a plethora of finger-foods and the like.
The walls of the house are adorned with trucker-associated things, photos of the family (both immediate and extended), knick-knacks all the way from Tennessee, and various maps and routes.
Jake gestures to them and explains, "My son had a thing for all things trucker and big rigs. I didn't want my boy following in my footsteps. He wanted to drive one of these babies and go on all kinds of trips, but he was destined fo--for so much more."
A few tears roll down the poor man's face; he sniffles and wipes them away with a tissue paper before scrunching it up with his balled-up fist.
He whispers, "I'm sorry, you don't wanna see this."
Lena reassures him, "It's OK. It's your home, it's your son's wake. Don't let anyone tell you how to grieve- we all do it differently. Just hang in there."
Jake dabs his face again, "Thanks. Please, help yourself to a--a drink and some food. Or just hang around, talk, and...yeah. I gotta go now."
Lena nods at him, "No problem."
Unfortunately for the PI, there is no coffee present. But there is a bowl of very red punch.
Miss Adams sighs and idly looks through what's been laid out. There is a platter of cheese cubes, a plate of crackers, a load of moon pies, and another of sliced meats.
Tyler's portrait shows him smiling widely. He had fair skin, curly hair, and bright green eyes. Behind him, a long-haul, eighteen-wheeler truck with its door wide open can be seen.
Poor kid had his whole life ahead of him and it was all brutally snuffed out by a speeding car. Lena resolves to find out who did this- and why. No matter what.
Ty's friends from school start to trickle in. They're mostly kids his age (or thereabouts). Some are dressed formally and this one kid even wears a bowtie, with his brown hair parted to the side.
Lena figures that she should at least talk to some of them to get a feel for things. She sits on the sofa, sips her punch, and tries to listen in on whatever conversation is being had.
Jake, however, takes the initiative to introduce his late son's friends to the PI.
Bowtie boy looks at Lena from head to toe, eyes narrowed in scrutiny. He straightforwardly asks her, "Who are you and what is your connection with Tyler?"
Lena flatly responds, "I'm the one investigating his murder. Where...are your parents?"
"I came by myself. I'm not five anymore- I'm practically a grown up."
Jake pats the boy's back, "Well, let's not get ahead of ourselves. You're a smart kid, but you're...still a kid. Go on, why don't you grab somethin' to eat and drink, hmm?"
He presses his lips into a thin smile and gives the boy another pat on the back. The latter nods and walks over to where the edibles and drinkables are placed.
"The other kids are the same way- high IQs. Tyler finally found a place where he belonged, where he was...understood. My wife and I weren't exactly the smartest of folk, but we made the choice to come over here and give our boy the best life we could. And that's what we did. Until...it all fell apart."
Lena sensitively asks, "What about your wife's wake? Why didn't you combine the two? Sorry if that came off as insensitive."
Mr. Woodward answers, "My...extended family believes that suicide is a sin. They're good people, but they can get awfully judgmental when it comes to stuff like this. I'll have my wife's wake when I'm ready, but I want this evening to be about Tyler."
"I see. I'm sorry to hear that, Jake."
Bowtie boy comes back with a cup of punch. Crumbs of food are spotted on the corners of his mouth. Lena has the good sense to ignore that lest he retort with a witty remark.
Jake leaves the two of them be.
Lena regards him with a guarded expression, "So, how well did you know Tyler Woodward?"
"What's your name, lady?"
"Lena Adams. What's yours?"
"Call me Stu."
The PI frowns, "Is that your real name or are you pulling my leg?"
Stu replies, "I'm not dumb enough to give a fake name to the chick investigating my best friend's murder. And yeah, I know it was murder. Hit-and-run. My parents try to shield me from this stuff, but I know what's going on."
"Then you must also know that the police didn't bother to do anything about it."
"Mm-hmm. No shocker there. The MCPD is, somehow, worse than the LAPD. And they make the Flimont City Police Department look like pros. My grandpa is from F-City, and he's always been pissed about it."
The other kids have their cellphones in their hand, and their fingers are busily tapping away.
Lena jerks her chin over to them, "Did any of you see Tyler before he was killed?"
Stu shakes his head, "Nuh-uh. I went home early- stomach bug. But Ty stayed for the special classes. I'm going to college before I turn eighteen, so those classes are meant to prep us up for the future."
"Wow. Sounds...intense. And academically speaking, how was Tyler? Good grades, attendance, behavior, all that stuff?"
"Lady, our school isn't for show. Tyler was so smart he could tell you the full names of all the Vice Presidents' wives off the top of his head. He was also really active in the student body and all. He even helped me in my run for class president- campaign slogans, leaflets, electoral strategies catered to specific age groups, that sorta thing."
Lena's eyebrows shoot up, "And do you geniuses ever get time for extracurriculars?"
Stu lightly scoffs at her, "We're not nerds getting wedgies. Even the jocks in our school can solve complex linear equations. Yeah, we had hobbies. We weren't a bunch of bespectacled, no-life smarties with our noses buried in big books all day long. We had stuff to do, stuff to occupy ourselves with."
"Like?"
Stu side-eyes her, "The usual stuff that teenagers occupy themselves with- whatever you did as a kid, I guess. Look, how is any of this relevant to catching Tyler's killer?"
Lena bluntly asks, "Did you and Tyler have a falling out of any kind?"
He truthfully answers, "No. We were best friends. Maybe not best friend numero uno, but we were pretty tight. I'd do anything for him, and he'd do anything for me. Anything legal, that is."
The PI further prods, "Do you know of anyone who might've had it out for him? An adult with a grudge? A teacher? Someone from your school's staff? A parent, perhaps? Did he have any enemies that you're aware of?"
"Nah. You're looking in all the wrong places, lady. At my school, the only time we go far is when someone messes up and does something really stupid. Like that one kid who was suspended last month for playing some really yikes stuff on the PA system. Tyler saw who did it and dutifully reported it to the relevant parties."
Lena rubs her hands together, "Did said parties ever retaliate or threaten retaliation?"
Stu shakes his head, "Nope. He didn't just SEE who did it. He figured out how they hacked into the school's PA system, too. You should've seen the detailed report he typed up in the computer lab. The teachers talked to the kids and told them to knock it off, or else..."
Lena regards the young lad and folds her arms, "So, if I'm not looking for someone who knew Tyler, then who exactly should I be looking for?"
He mulls over it for a moment, "Well, you're the PI. Uh...it seems random. Maybe whoever hit Ty was, like, really blackout drunk?"
"Hmm. Maybe. Thanks for your help, Stu."
Stu nods, does a slight bow, and saunters away.
Lena's amber eyes follow him, "He seems alright. Smart. Lemme know how the therapy goes in a few years' time. Heh."
Some of Tyler's teachers are present. Jake makes the necessary introductions. Lena nods back at some of them and decides to talk to the math teacher. Jon is British, pale, has a short beard that's been neatly trimmed, well into his thirties, wears a dark-orange, sleeveless sweater vest over a white dress shirt, and khaki pants.
Lena begins by asking, "How well did you know the...uh...victim?"
Jon plainly answers, "He was a pupil of mine, Ms. Adams."
"Call me Lena. How was he in class? Did he ever have any issues? Academic? Personal? Did he skip a lot of classes?"
The Brit chuckles a little, "Tyler- or Ty as some were inclined to call him- was always very smart. His grades in school were top-notch and on par with the rest of his peers. He was a bright and friendly lad. He was never late to class, never missed a single day, and rarely had issues with the other pupils."
Lena narrows an eye and scrutinizes him, "Huh. Right. And how long have you been his math teacher for?"
Jon nervously chuckles and tugs on his collar, "Ah, so we're going down that path, eh? Very well, I have nothing to hide, miss--err--Lena. I came to this city five years ago. All my papers are in order, I assure you."
"And where are you originally from?"
Jon chuckles again, "If the accent hasn't already given it away, I hail from England; the city of Gloughchester, to be precise. In many ways, that place reminds me of Magnolia City. But let's not get too carried away, heh."
Lena queries, "What made you leave?"
"The school I used to work at...wasn't quite up to snuff. And the walls starting to crumble apart certainly played a role in my decision, as well. I received what I believed to be a decent offer and...took it. Err...accepted it, I suppose. The rest is history."
"Hmm. And do you know of anybody who might've wanted to hurt Tyler? Anyone who may have had it out for him, harbored a grudge, or maybe even knew what his walking route back home was?"
The Brit scratches his chin, "As I've said, Tyler was a bright and happy lad. I do not mean to overstep my bounds, but you are barking up the wrong trees. No disrespect intended, of course. If I were you, I'd...actually, never mind. I don't know the first thing about being a PI, sorry."
Lena folds her arms, "No, no, I insist. What is it?"
Jon shuffles his feet a little, "Well, you see, I'd...look at the tire tracks of the motor vehicle that struck him down and go from there. They'd be able to tell you much more than I could. Assuming that...wait, how does one match tire tracks to vehicles?"
Lena flatly responds, "It's a difficult process, but they've probably been washed away by now. Thanks for your help, Jon."
"Anytime, detective."
Now, onto the relatives. They're an...interesting lot. Bonnet caps, bolo ties, big, booming, twangy voices, and a whole lotta salt and pepper.
Jake, for whatever reason, only picks certain relatives to introduce to Lena. There are uncles, aunties, cousins, cousins twice removed, great-grandparents, and more. But all of them are...well, nice enough, she supposes.
One of the frail and older ladies calls Lena an "oriental". Yikes. Lena stifles a grimace and quickly shuffles away. Jake swoops in and explains that that particular relative has dementia and was born in a very, very different time.
Lena admits, "Believe me, I've been called far worse by people who haven't had any dementia."
"Well, she didn't mean anything malicious by it, but I apologize on her behalf, nonetheless." Jake does his best to be a good host, hold it all together, and stay strong for the sake of his family and the ones who've come all the way from Tennessee to pay their respects.
Lena shakes the old lady's outstretched, wrinkly hand, tightly smiles, and walks away.
The other relatives don't seem to know much, nor do they display any signs of wanting to hurt, let alone kill, Tyler. Many of them are genuinely quite aghast at how sudden the murder was and how quickly the MCPD closed the case.
There's a lady who is in her mid-twenties. Her blush is so very bright and her smile so very wide. She is, however, dressed in a pair of jeans, a tight white t-shirt, and a printed shrug jacket. Her curly black hair looks like it's been washed recently, and her brown eyes sparkle.
Tina looks around and deposits some of the finger foods into her very large purse. Lena's face scrunches up in mild disapproval when she notices that.
The PI walks up to Tina and asks, "Did you know the victim?"
The young woman turns to Lena and greets her with a cheerful voice, "Uh...yeah, sorta." Her New Jersey accent is quite thick and pronounced.
"Sorta?"
"Yeah. Tyler helped me get rid of all the viruses on my computer." She says it like "Computah".
"Uh-huh. And you're his uncle's...fifth wife, is that right?"
Tina nods, "I am. Lucky number five, eh?"
Lena dryly responds, "I guess so. What do you do for a living, Tina?"
Tina gleefully answers, "I'm a makeup artist...for the stars!"
Lena thinks to herself, "Yeah, the cheesy soap opera ones, if your own crappy makeup skills are anything to go by."
Tina continues, "I'm working on a show called...actually, I'm under NDA. But I get to work with all sortsa celebrities, make 'em look like a million bucks, and stuff."
"Uh-huh, I'm sure you do. Where's your husband?"
"Hospital. His bum leg's acting up again. That's what he gets for riding his motorcycle around town, thinkin' that he's twenty all over again. But I still love him."
Lena sighs a bit, "Let's focus on Tyler, please."
