Tina nods fervently, "Yeah, yeah, sure. What do ya wanna know? You know, ya remind me of all these stars I work with."
Lena thinks to herself, "Oh, great. Here we go again."
She musters up a smile and plays along, "Do I?"
Tina vaguely gestures towards Lena's face, "You're a PI, right? You're almost there with the cliched PI style. All ya need is an inner monologue, a hat, a whole pack of cigars, and a booze habit! Hahaha!"
Lena isn't smiling now, "Yeah, ha-ha. Very funny. Focus. Tyler. Do you know anybody who'd wanna hurt your uhm...nephew, I guess. What about your husband, were he and Tyler close?"
Tina shrugs, "Well, we all got along like a big, happy family. My husband wouldn't hurt a fly. Sure, he's got a few money problems, but he and Ty got along decently. And after my husband's sister...offed herself, things haven't been the same around here."
Lena quirks an eyebrow, "Oh? Now, we're finally headed somewhere. Go on, tell me more."
Tina gesticulates, "Shouldn't ya be writing this stuff down in a notepad? That's what they do on all the TV shows involving a gumshoe."
Lena taps on the side of her head, "Don't need to. I've got it all in here."
"An eidetic memory??"
"Something like that, yeah. So, what did you mean when you said that things haven't been the same around here?"
Tina sighs heavily, "Oh, nothing much. Just that, like, I don't think we've been the same since then. I mean, My husband's sister was absolutely devastated when...ya know. She lost a lotta weight, looked so awful, and barely said a word to us when we dropped by to visit her. She wasn't always like that. Losing a kid does that to you."
"It does."
Tina looks around, "I gotta head home. Truth be told, I'm still an outsider to the family. New Jersey and Tennessee, am I right?" She says it like "Nooh Joisey".
Lena plainly replies, "I guess so. Before you go, is there anything else you can tell me that might help in my investigation? Anything at all, no matter how small or insignificant it may seem. Please, don't hold back."
Tina brings her index finger up to her chin and thinks. Her eyebrows furrow a bit, and Lena wonders if she's doing it all for show or if she really is trying to give her some kind of useful information.
The PI clears her throat and casts a sidelong glance at the makeup artist.
Tina finally says, "I don't think someone from the family did it. Nah, if they wanted to hurt Tyler, they'd probably have done it...subtler."
Lena frowns, "Subtler? Like?"
"Poison? B&E? Snatch him on his way from school? Definitely not driving into him. This doesn't uh...seem premeditated to me."
Lena asks herself, "Just how many crappy detective TV shows does she watch in a day? Holy shit."
Tina continues, "The cops weren't too hot on solving this one, after all. No shocker there. Maybe you should broaden your horizons, eh? Look for someone not at all related to Ty."
"Such as?"
"Dunno, you're the broad investigating the case, ain't ya?"
Lena nods and sighs, "Thanks, Tina. That'll be all."
Tina cheerily waves goodbye and loudly announces her exit as she stumbles out the door.
Lena glances over at the deceased's father. He presses his lips into a thin line and pensively lowers his head, doing his absolute best to present a composed and dignified front.
But Jake's demeanor towards certain relatives is a lot colder and a lot less forgiving. But he hesitates to let them know, instead nodding along to whatever they say and keeping his composure maintained.
He looks up at Lena, gives her a slight, almost imperceptible nod, and turns back to his guests.
Miss Adams stays till the very end of the wake. A few words of remembrance were said and everyone who was able to make it was given a warm and heartfelt goodbye.
A Pastor led the final prayer. People started to slowly leave the house, but Jake wasn't quite ready to call it a day. There's a lot of food still remaining. He slowly walks to the kitchen and starts packing them away in plastic containers. Tears stream down his face as he does so.
Lena comes up behind him, puts a hand on his shoulder, and gently says, "Thank you for inviting me, Jake. I was able to learn a lot about your son and his friends, extended family, and teachers. This should, hopefully, help in solving the case."
Jake has his back turned to her. He sniffles, "I appreciate the sentiment. It means a lot to have someone with your...expertise on the case. And thank you for doin' it pro bono."
"It's no worry. I'll find whoever did this. I--I promise."
Jake doesn't turn around. He just continues to put the leftovers in the containers, "I'll hold you to your word. Would you like some gravy to...take home?"
Lena shakes her head, "I'm fine, thanks. Goodnight, Jake. We'll stay in touch and I'll keep you apprised on my investigation."
He slowly nods and replies, "Thanks. G'night! Get home safe."
"Hmm. Bye."
And with that, she exits the house and starts making her way down the steps. The sky above is nearly pitch black. A few stars twinkle here and there, and the air is cool and crisp.
Lena Adams spends the remainder of the night caffeinating herself and poring over what information she has in regard to the deceased child. This is a case that she intends to solve, no matter how long or difficult it may prove to be.
She occasionally stands up to stretch her cramping legs. And after a few hours of this, she closes up her office and heads back home. During the train ride, she has a hard time sleeping.
Chapter Two: Totally NOT A Batcave
Later in the night...
Under an Irish pub named "O'Rory's Tavern," a cavernous maw is hidden deep beneath the ground and only accessible through a set of secure doors from the smoky pub's backroom. A narrow set of metallic stairs lead down into a secret space, and at the bottom of the steps is another secure door.
(One can't be too secure these nights!)
The metallic steps carry the echo of rapidly descending footsteps. The sound reverberates and bounces off the damp walls. The door at the bottom is shoved open by a broad shoulder.
A well-dressed man with deathly pale skin, cropped auburn hair, a smooth face, and three piercing, green eyes stomps in. The eye on his forehead glows a bright and unnatural shade of ivy green.
He tugs on the lapels of his suit and forces a deep breath through lungs that ceased functioning decades ago. The breathing is just out of habit, if nothing else.
Dampness pervades the underground space. There are pipes on the wall, dim lighting, a few humming freezers, a row of metal cots (for emergencies), a bunch of storage shelves, a workbench, and other assorted bits and bobs that may (or may not) be construed as junk.
The man kicks a crumpled can aside. His sharp and angular features twist in disgust as he notices the mess. His three green eyes dart all over the room.
A hiss of displeasure escapes his cold lips, "Goddamnit. Nossies made a mess. Again. Hmph. I've gotta talk to Cedric about this."
Sheriff (Kindred Sheriff, that is) Marshall Fairfield of Clan Salubri (three-eyed vampires) paces around the area and grumbles while cleaning up the mess that some of the local Nosferatu had made- they mainly used this space as a temporary resting/experimentation spot.
The Prince of this Domain, for his part, didn't mind such things- and the Nossies can be a persuasive bunch!
Sheriff Marshall grumbles some more while piling the empty beer cans, scrunched up blood bags, and other trash into a garbage bag. The plastic crinkles as the frustrated Sheriff tosses whatever he can into the bag. He grabs the bag and flings it halfway across the area.
"This is a fucking Masquerade breach waiting to happen. I'm gonna have to have a stern sit-down with those Nossies. And soon. Who leaves blood bags lying around the goddamn floor like this? That's not how things are supposed to be done!"
"And yet that's how the Crawlers in this Domain conduct themselves, darling," a feminine voice dripping with syrupy honey-sweetness replies.
A woman in an elegant, form-hugging dress saunters down the steps. She is of average build and height, pale as a sheet, has a round face free of any major blemishes, and black hair that cascades down to her lower back.
Her three brown eyes fixate upon the Sheriff.
He sighs, "Rosalind. So good of you to show up. Why don't you help me clean up the mess, hmm?"
Seneschal Rosalind Meyer (or Meyers to certain folks) waves her hand and sashays her way towards a worktable. She picks up the nearest thing to her (which happens to be a piece of paper) and daintily holds it in her cold fingers.
Seneschal Rosalind flicks the paper aside and smiles sweetly, "It is what it is, Marshall. The others should soon be here. Hopefully. There's...a bit to discuss and go over tonight. And how was your evening, Sheriff?" Her tone is genuine, gentle, and filled with care.
Sheriff Marshall rubs the bridge of his nose and answers, "Busy maintaining order in a city with half a million souls going to and fro. How 'bout you?"
The Salubri Seneschal languidly waves her hand about, "Oh, you know. The usual. Wedding prep, planning, and the like."
Sheriff Marshall mumbles, "It's been going on for half a century. Just stick the ring around his finger, say 'I do,' and give him a sloppy kiss on the lips. End of story."
"It's not that simple, Marshall. You- of all people- ought to know that. Come, now. Help me lay out the chairs, would you?"
"Hmm. Sure."
The two of them get around to diligently unfolding a bunch of metal chairs and setting them up in a way that they are now facing towards a slightly elevated platform. A few of them have duct tape covering the spots where the padding is coming undone.
While they work, Sheriff Marshall asks her, "So, uh...how are your Blood Dolls doing? Any fuss? Anyone getting any bright ideas?"
Seneschal Rosalind's pale face tics, "They know what they've gotten themselves into, Marshall. Don't worry, if they...try to pull a runner, my first call would be to...you. Satisfied?"
The other Salubri shrugs a shoulder, "I don't like hurting people unless it's absolutely necessary. And even then, I still don't like it. I already got the gist of how your arrangements work, Rosalind, but I have to ask--"
"Are they happy? Are they fulfilled? How intact is the Masquerade?"
Sheriff Marshall sighs heavily, "Took the words straight outta my mouth. Like always. Yeah, are--are they happy with you biting into them on a nightly basis?"
She plainly declares, "It's an entirely consensual feeding arrangement. We both stand to benefit in the long run, and they're perfectly safe and secure. And if it's worth anything, I do make a hearty meal for them. It's only just, isn't it?"
"I guess so. I hear someone."
A dapper man in his late thirties with very dark skin, full lips, luscious dreadlocks, and trimmed facial hair comes down the stairs. His clothes are immaculate and very well-tailored- a purple suit jacket, off-white slacks, and snakeskin shoes.
Some beads are tied around his icy hands, and his three dark eyes are somewhat glassy, but they quickly snap into focus when he looks at the Seneschal. A smile asserts itself across his face, and his teeth are all very white and very straight.
Seneschal Rosalind puts her hands on her chest and smiles back with just as much intensity. Her pupils dilate.
Prince Cedric Montgomery of Clan Salubri (again) approaches his Seneschal and kisses her on the lips. Just a brief peck. The two of them embrace each other and sway from side to side, longingly staring into each other's many eyes.
The Sheriff stands aside and lowers his head reverentially, "Prince Cedric. How have things been?"
Prince Cedric's deep voice almost echoes throughout this underground not-a-Batcave, "Well enough, Marshall. I heard that some Nosferatu had been...havin' a sleepover down here? Well, damn, I know that times are tough, but them Nossies are loaded."
Seneschal Rosalind nudges her fiancé (of fifty years!) with her elbow, "Oh, come now. Let's not paint in such broad strokes, honey. While the Primogen and his Whip may be...loaded in their own right, we still have plenty of Crawlers in this Domain who very much live on the margins. We mustn't forget that."
The Prince takes his Seneschal's hand into his, "I'm not saying it like that, baby. I'm just saying that them Sewer Rats gotta take better care of each other- like they do in other Domains! Warrens and shit. How come we still don't got none of that over here, hmm? Makes ya wonder, don't it?"
Sheriff Marshall chuckles, "It sure does, boss. It sure does."
Prince Cedric paces around and coolly asks, "So, how many we expecting for tonight's meetup? How's the security lookin'? Marshall?"
"All quiet, chief. Masquerade breaches are at an all-time low, everyone plays their role, stays in their lane, and...they get along with each other. Well enough for our liking, I guess." There is a hint of uncertainty in the Sheriff's voice.
Prince Cedric nods firmly, "That's good to hear! Hey, Rosalind, you decide on a wedding venue yet? And I do want them white flowers."
Seneschal Rosalind sighs, "Honestly? I'd prefer lilies. How 'bout we meet halfway through, hmm?"
"We still don't got a venue yet..."
Sheriff Marshall mutters, "Just do it in a church already."
The two lovers overhear that and chuckle amongst themselves. Sheriff Marshall joins along, but only a little. And the three of them go way back, so there's a lot of history between them.
The Prince tugs on his cuffs and sits down on a chair set up for him on the elevated platform. His Seneschal is seated to his left, while his Sheriff is seated to his right.
Now, they patiently wait for the other Kindred to file in.
A horrendously deformed, albeit well-dressed, man hobbles down the steps. His ashy, boil-ridden skin is stretched tightly over his elongated, gaunt, and twisted face. Bits of frizzy hair stick out from his scalp, and his mouth is wide open, revealing deeply rotten teeth and a chipped fang.
But his three-piece suit is immaculate.
Seneschal Rosalind addresses him, "Laurence! Come, come. Did anyone spot you?"
Laurence Lancaster of Clan Nosferatu glowers at everybody with his bloodshot, bulging eyes, "Of course not. I've been doing this shit for years on end." His voice is gruff, hoarse, and gravelly.
The Sheriff dryly says, "It's not 'this shit', Laurence. It's important! And where's Otto? Knowing him, he'd probably take our alternative entrance." He does a thumb-back gesture to a loose grate leading down, deeper into the cavern.
Laurence answers, "He ain't comin'. Sent me in his place. As Whip to the Primogen, I got the right to be here. I'll relay whatever the hell we waste our time talking 'bout to him, he'll sign off on it, and everything'll be copacetic."
The Prince sighs deeply and shrugs, "If you say so. And how has your week been, hmm?"
Attempts to inject cheer and be polite are met with foul grunts and fouler glares. Laurence folds his arms and sinks deeper into his seat. His sulking is obvious, and the others choose to ignore him for the most part.
(He's got myriads of issues).
His Embrace was a decade ago. He originally hails from Flimont City and is of the infamous Lancaster family (moneyed assholes, basically). One would think that being Embraced into such a Clan would've humbled him, given him a fresh perspective on matters, but it did quite the opposite.
His Embrace was a little too sudden and the process too rushed, and it did a helluva number on him. He couldn't go back to his crazy, overbearing father and as a result, his company- Lancaster-SYS- completely collapsed into many pieces and was sold off for pennies on the dollar. Parts, practically!
Laurence had no choice but to remain in Magnolia City ever since those events transpired. He's got three adult children (all sons from different women) who don't really bother keeping in touch. And none of them have really cared to find out what happened to daddy dearest.
Even then, Laurence is still a wealthy man in his own right. His shrewd business acumen, his callous ruthlessness, and his complete lack of mercy and human decency have kept him afloat and allowed him to live a very, VERY comfortable existence.
Heavy, nearly obnoxious footsteps descend down the metal stairs at a very rapid pace. The kind of footsteps one would expect from a person with very sturdy legs and very little patience.
A well-built, muscular man in his indeterminate thirties cracks his knuckles and walks over to the closest unoccupied chair. He sits down with a huff and grunts. His alabaster skin is slightly scarred, and he's wearing a blue blazer and pleated pants.
His two green eyes glare at everyone around him. His expression is stern, brooding, and very, very focused. His blonde hair is neatly combed to the side, and his ears are plugged with wired earphones.
Prince Cedric stares at him and calls out, "Amos Greenberg!"
Amos clicks his tongue in frustration and pulls out one of his earphones, "What?"
The Prince sighs and addresses him, "How you doing?"
"Fine." Amos shoves the earphone back into his ear, folds one leg over the other, and shifts about a little in his seat. He throws his head back and shuts his eyes. His pale face is smooth, but there are a few spots here and there.
Amos Greenberg is, by all accounts, an Assamite (Clan Banu Haqim), but say that to his face and he'll punch you dead- those muscles aren't just for show!
He identifies as a "Bnei HaShem". They are a bunch of Israel-loving, Jewish supremacist Assamites who vehemently and ferociously reject the heritage of their Blood in favor of some made up, ahistorical (one might even say anti-historical) Zionist bullshit. Typical.
There is a small, but problematic, Bnei HaShem presence in Magnolia City. As such, the actual Banu Haqim are a bit cautious about him and his associates and are attempting to keep an eye on him. A very close eye.
And many real Assamites have been put on the back foot by Amos's many attempts to destroy them all and set up shop in this Domain. All of this is occurring behind Prince Cedric's back.
Amos has been trying to ingratiate himself into the Prince's Court, but thus far, it hasn't worked out as well as he hoped it would.
The Prince asks him, "Amos, I got a question for you, boy."
Amos sighs heavily and takes out his earphone, some really loud music faintly bleeds out of the device, "What?!"
Sheriff Marshall does not like this False Assamite. He grits his teeth and demands, "First of all, show some goddamn respect and take those fucking things off. The Prince wants to have a conversation with you- the least you could do is take off those goddamn things and HEAR HIM OUT!"
Amos's voice is calm and even, "I'll do that when HE asks me nicely, Cyclops." And in goes the earphone again.
The False Assamite defiantly crosses his arms and raises his chin, glaring daggers back at the trio seated at the elevated platform.
Seneschal Rosalind's gaze darkens, "Well, that went about as well as expected." She politely smiles at Amos and averts her gaze when he looks back at her with a sour expression.
Seneschal Rosalind murmurs to herself, "He's a funny one."
The Sheriff growls, "That's it. I'm taking his head off--"
Prince Cedric places his hand on the other man's knee and urges him, "No, you ain't. Marshall, we already talked about this. The...Bnei HaShem haven't done a thing that can be interpreted as a violation of the Camarilla's Traditions. We don't got just cause to do away with them."
Sheriff Marshall snarls, "They're shady. I know it, I just know it, but I--I can't seem to do a damn thing about it!"
Seneschal Rosalind's voice is very soft, "It's alright, Sheriff. If they are...shady, then they'll slip up. And we'll be there to put them in their place."
The Sheriff's voice is exasperated, "But I--I can't seem to find a single goddamn violation that they've committed against the Traditions or any other law that exists! I can't reasonably pin a single fucking thing against them!"
He grimly adds, "But all those recent disappearances and murders aren't a coincidence. It all started when muscle man over there made his presence known to us."
Prince Cedric sighs, "Now you're just jumpin' to wild conclusions, Marshall. Sure, he was lurking around without presenting himself to me in accordance with the Fifth Tradition of Hospitality, but it don't mean that he was goin' around killin' folks and makin' people disappear."
Seneschal Rosalind put in, "Maybe he has a point, honey. There are coincidences, sure, yes. But perhaps he's right and there is a connection that we should seriously be looking into."
Prince Cedric narrows his eyes and stares at the smug Assassin, "I dunno, Ros. It feels like we're grasping at straws here. Lookin' around for a boogeyman under a bed, so to speak. I dunno. But...we should keep a closer eye on him and his people, hmm?"
Sheriff Marshall stifles a groan, "That's, like, the least we should be doing. And why have the actual Assamites stopped showing up to these meetings, hmm? I--I mean, Amos isn't even anybody's fucking Whip. Why is he even here?"
Seneschal Rosalind shrugs, "Well, to be fair, Marshall, these meetings are open to any and all of Caine's Childer. Amos has just as much of a right to be here as anybody else does. Even Ghouls are more than welcome to attend these meetings, you know."
The Sheriff replies, "Yeah, but Ghouls aren't that big of a threat to the Masquerade compared to some asshole who looks like he spends all of his time at the gym and nowhere else. I don't like him. I just don't. And it's not because he's Jewish, it's because he's an asshole!"
The False Assamite snorts. It's like he heard a part of the Sheriff's outburst and found it amusing.
Seneschal Rosalind's gaze is withering, "Well, you've made that abundantly clear to us on numerous occasions in the past. Let's just do what we can to keep the peace, watch over things with keen eyes, and...don't let Amos get to you, Marshall."
The Salubri Sheriff balls his hands into fists and mutters, "Whatever. S--sure. Sorry, Seneschal. I shouldn't have disrespected you l--like that."
Prince Cedric puts his hand on the knee of his fellow Peacemaker, "It's fine, Marshall. We get it; it ain't easy being Sheriff. But we do our best to make each other's Unlives- and duties to this Court- as easy and painless as possible."
The Sheriff nods, "Sorry. But I'm not dropping it. Something stinks and we better be getting to the bottom of it."
Prince Cedric leans to the side and covers the side of his mouth with his hand. He lowers his voice to a whisper, "If we plan on doing any investigating, then we better do it off-the-books, you dig? He'll see us comin' from a mile away. Me and Ros got something that we wanna run by you after this meetup ends. If you're interested."
Sheriff Marshall nods, "I am. Y--you're the Prince, after all."
Prince Cedric pulls away and clicks his tongue, "It ain't like that, Marshall. All our voices matter equally. Ain't that right, baby?"
Seneschal Rosalind beams and looks into her lover's three eyes, "Yes, indeed. The Second Tradition of the Domain implies- strongly- that a Prince's word is law, but all voices are valued equally. That's just how we do things around here."
Prince Cedric takes her hands in his and reassures, "And that's how we always will, baby." He kisses the top of her hands, and she lightly giggles in response.
A very brief and uncomfortable silence follows, but nobody has anything else to add. Laurence sulks while Amos bobs his head to the music. The other three are still engaged in their own little discussions.
After a few moments, Prince Cedric checks his watch and shakes his head, "If that Italian don't show up in the next minute, we're starting. He can get the particulars of this meeting from whoever he so pleases. Is a little punctuality too much to ask for these nights? We got some serious stuff to talk about!"
Laurence's gurgling grunt is enough to confirm his displeasure with this.
Amos clicks his tongue and grumbles, "C'mon, c'mon, start already. I got places to be, important shit to do."
Sheriff Marshall snarls, "YOU got places to be, important shit to do, huh, well how about--"
Seneschal Rosalind quickly intervenes, putting on the best possible smile she can muster, "Now, now, Marshall. We'll give our Italian friend a few more minutes. After all, the night is still young, isn't it?" A forced chuckle escapes her lips.
Amos's dead eyes dart over to her. They stare at each other for a few seconds before Prince Cedric puts his hand on his girlfriend's knee.
Gentle footsteps can be heard from the metal stairs. Everybody looks over. Everybody except Amos, who has his eyes shut and earphones plugged in.
A woman in her forties strides in. She is pale-skinned and somewhat thin. Her bobbed, blonde hair is neatly done, and her lips are painted a deep shade of red. A silky white shirt and a black skirt adorn her frame.
She profusely apologizes for being late, her Italian accent flaring at certain points.
"Scusi (ITA: Excuse me). I am so sorry for being late, Prince. Am--am I late? Where are the others? Only this many?"
Francesca Livroni is the Whip to the Primogen of the Hecata Clan, the Clan of Death.
She sets her Gucci purse on the free seat next to her, straightens herself out, and bows her head reverently. Then, she takes her seat in an elegant and poised manner.
The Necromancer looks around the room, takes note of Amos, and frowns slightly.
Laurence mumbles, "They sent the wrong Italian. Heh-heh."
Francesca retorts, "Mr. Lancaster, you are like me. Whip to the Primogen of our respective Clans. Where is Otto, anyway? Still brooding in his mansion?"
Laurence counters, "And where the hell is your Vincenzo, huh? That old Graverobber still trying to play around with shit he got no business interfering in?"
Francesca scowls, "He is busy." She refuses to fall for Laurence's cheap barbs.
"'Busy'. Uh-huh, sure..." And he huffs, grunts, and ignores Ms. Livroni.
Seneschal Rosalind claps her hands together, the sound echoing throughout this subterranean cavern, "Kindred of the Court! Members of the Council! Could I please have your attention."
She fixes the muscular not-Assamite with a somewhat severe look, and he grunts as a sign of acquiescence. The earplugs come off and he nods to her.
The Seneschal continues, "Thank you. Before we begin these proceedings, let us take a moment to reflect on these past few weeks, hmm? Just a short and quick moment, if you'd all be so kind."
Amos shrugs and says nothing.
Laurence lowers his head and clasps his scarred, deformed, and pus-ridden hands together. His breathing is heavy and labored. He shuts his eyes and mumbles something that nobody else can quite pick up on.
Francesca contemplatively stares at her surroundings. Her gaze becomes unfocused. Her fingers idly twitch and her lips slowly part. She absentmindedly strokes her left hand, which is concealed by a thick leather glove.
Her hazel eyes linger on the natural rock formations on the walls and the ceiling.
Seneschal Rosalind waits for everyone to settle down before continuing, "Good."
She looks over her shoulder. Prince Cedric nods back, and she returns her gaze to the rest of the assembly. She promptly declares, "First and foremost, let us remember to always do our best to maintain our Humanity and strive to be the best versions of ourselves that we can possibly be."
She waits for a beat before continuing, "Next, let us remember those who have, unfortunately, perished. We are...looking into the circumstances surrounding their untimely demises. That's all that we can share with you at this moment. Please, Francesca and Laurence...make sure that your respective Primogens get this information."
The Hecata vampire nods and begins to rapidly jot some things down in a thick leather journal of some kind.
The Nosferatu Kindred merely shrugs, "I'll give Otto the abridged version later on when I go and see him."
Seneschal Rosalind slowly replies, "Of course, whatever's comfortable with you, Mr. Lancaster. And Ms. Livroni, please make sure that your...book doesn't fall in the wrong hands, hmm? It'd be a darn shame if it did."
Francesca equably answers, "Sì (ITA: Yes). It is always with me and never leaves my side. I understand the gravity of this situation, Seneschal Rosalind. I will do my best to ensure the integrity of the Masquerade. Always."
Seneschal Rosalind looks back at the Prince, and he nods. He stands up and relieves her, "OK, now onto the matters at hand. There's been some shit goin' down in this city, and it's bad enough that we're starting to get concerned."
The Salubri Seneschal sits herself back down and keenly watches her boyfriend pace around.
His dreadlocks sway around when he walks about, "Somebody is murdering our people, and we're not entirely sure why. So far, most of the victims have been Assamites...Ghouls and Kindred alike." He looks at Amos.
Amos softly chuckles, "What? We don't know jack-squat about the perpetrator or perpetrators, right? The fuck you gazing at me for with those three eyes of yours, huh? Do I look like I had anything to do with that? You know how heated our disagreements with those fakers got, but...I wouldn't stoop THAT low. Heh, it's not my style."
He elaborately throws his arms up, "Oh, c'mon! Besides, what about all those other murders, huh? From what I heard, it's connected to the port and the docks."
Sheriff Marshall strokes his chin, "Huh. Right. What about those grisly killings? Cedric? I--I mean, Prince Cedric, shouldn't we also be concerned about that? Port authorities are dropping dead like flies, left and right."
He recounts, "Dockworkers, supervisors, cargo handlers, vehicle operators, office accountants, clerks...even managers have been found around town with their throats cut or their chests split open. Some of them were still warm when the cops found 'em."
He glares at Amos balefully, "And all of the murders happened at night- I read the police reports..."
The False Assamite side-eyes the Salubri Sheriff, "I don't quite like what you're implying, Salubrian." His tone is quite menacing and very hostile.
Sheriff Marshall lowers his head in shame when Prince Cedric cuts in, "Marshall, can it. Now. Amos, we are terribly sorry for that. Tensions, as you can imagine, are quite high right now."
Amos's dead eyes narrow and his brows furrow, "Yeah, so you say. I don't come here to be accused, OK? My people have a long history of being blamed for all of society's woes. We've been put in the mud before, and we won't put up with it anymore. We're done being the perfect victim for the goyim to ogle and gawk at."
Sheesh, yikes.
Prince Cedric grimaces slightly, "OK, let's not even go down that road. We ain't here to be accusin' no one of anything. Do you, by any chance, happen to know anything that could be of use to us, Mr. Greenberg?"
Amos lifts his chin, "How so? Useful in what way, exactly?"
The three-eyed Prince says, "Any of your...friends work in the docks or around the ports? Do you...have contacts there? Anybody who might've seen something that can help shed some light on this nightmare? Innocent folks are getting hurt, dying, and losing their livelihoods. It's not right. It ain't right."
Amos callously shrugs, his gaze is intense and sharp, "Yes, it's all very tragic and all, but people- mortals, especially- die. From what I see on the news, crime's been going up lately. Heh, no surprise there. I've had to change the locks on some of my--"
Seneschal Rosalind counters, "I don't know what 'news' you've been watching, but crime in this city has actually declined over the years- it's even said to be at an all-time low! Maybe you ought to check your sources before confidently citing them as fact. Be very cautious before making such bold declarations."
However, she does concede, "But I will admit that the sudden spike in violence is a bit disturbing, yes. But we are creatures, primarily, of the long run. Just something for us all to keep in mind."
Amos snorts, "OK, whatever. Crime's up, crime's down. Makes no difference to us Kindred, right? Just as long as we got our warm bodies to chomp into. Speaking of which, is anyone here Hungry?"
Seneschal Rosalind's patience is quickly running out, "Mr. Greenberg. We extend these invites to you as a gesture of good faith. While we are acutely aware of your disagreements with the...other Assamites, this is no time for petty infighting. Please, try and conduct yourself a little better."
Amos sighs, "Yeah, well, I'm Hungry--"
Francesca tells him, "Then, you should've fed before coming here? Or did you expect to be given something- or someone- to slake your eternal thirst?"
Amos snickers, "Now that you mentioned it...do we get subsidized meals down here? Hmm? Hmm?" His eyebrows wiggle up and down.
Prince Cedric shakes his head, "No, we do not. But in the future, I STRONGLY recommend that you keep the Beast at bay before showing up to these meetings. We don't want- or need- anybody Frenzying around like a damn fool."
He gestures to Sheriff Marshall in order to reinforce his point. The Sheriff is a tad bit tense, but he nods and puts in, "Yep. I'd hate to have to put down a Kindred lost in the Wassail."
Amos scoffs, "What's the worst that could happen? Wassail is only if you go too damn long without feeding. You Frenzy, you Frenzy. Just pick up the pieces, get rid of the evidence, and move on."
Seneschal Rosalind visibly grimaces at how indifferent Amos is acting, "No, it's not as simple as that, Mr. Greenberg."
The False Assamite snickers, "Oh, yeah, you'd know, wouldn't you--"
Prince Cedric butts in and points a finger at Amos, "ENOUGH! Don't even think about goin' there! I strongly recommend that you drop this RIGHT NOW!"
Laurence dryly says, "What? Is Rosalind any better than us cuz she only bites into...willing vessels? Consensual feedings and shit, tch. But we're all Beasts."
Francesca stays silent, opting not to say anything or offer her own two cents on the matter.
A deathly silence follows for a moment. Seneschal Rosalind can't bring herself to look at anyone, and she's wringing her hands. Her jaw is clenched, and her brows are furrowed. Her fellow Salubri are just as uncomfortable as her.
Amos adjusts the patterned yarmulke on his head and casually asks, "So, uh...is that it for tonight? I got places to be."
Sheriff Marshall shakes his head and defers to the Prince, who is heaving with barely concealed rage. He calms himself down, forces a deep breath through lifeless lungs, and blinks his three eyes.
Prince Cedric straightens his back and lifts his chin, "We're done when I say we're done. Sit your asses down and don't move until I'm done talkin'. Understood?"
Everyone nods.
The Prince proceeds, "It ain't a full house tonight, but it's acceptable. Whips, please ensure that your respective Primogens get the message about this meetup. I...understand why they ain't present, but in the future, I'd strongly advise that they make the time, move their schedules around, and show up."
Laurence mutters, "Yeah, sure thing."
Francesca firmly nods and quietly jots down some notes, "OK. I will be sure to remind Vincenzo."
Prince Cedric stares at Amos, "And YOU wouldn't happen to know what happened to the Banu Haqim Primogen, would you?"
The Bnei Hashem dismissively waves his hand and scoffs, "He probably got Beckoned away? Disappeared? Went for a really long fucking walk? Gone with the wind? Who gives a shit. I don't."
Sheriff Marshall sighs, "Dr. George Fateh informed us that the Assamite Primogen hasn't been seen...in a while. And I'd trust the good doctor...any night over--" He stops himself from saying more, but he makes sure to give Amos a very pointed look.
The False Assamite does not return it. All he does is smirk fiendishly.
Seneschal Rosalind narrows her three eyes and calls out to the Hecata, "Miss Livroni, that's a nice purse you've got over there. When we last saw each other, I don't believe you had it with you."
The Italian vampire looks at the purse and says, "Oh, this? It is...I found it. Sì, it is...something I...err...located. It is a long story, and I do not know if I would be allowed to share it with you, Seneschal. Scusa (ITA: Sorry)."
Laurence murmurs, "It's just a fucking purse. Expensive looking, too. Why you withholding information, huh?"
Francesca replies, "I would not be able to answer that, Crawler. I--I do not feel fully comfortable just yet. Maybe once I receive a better answer from--never mind. Scusa. It is a lovely night, isn't it?"
She's changing the subject abruptly. Classic. But it's just a Gucci bag, why is she so evasive about it?
Prince Cedric loudly clears his throat, "Gal talk after the meeting. Right now, finding the Banu Haqim Primogen is my number one priority. Everything else- for the time being, at least- goes on the backburner until he's located. Francesca?"
"Yes, Prince Cedric?" Francesca asks, sitting up straighter and giving the Salubri her undivided attention.
"I'd like a word with you in private. After the meeting. You still do that...thing, right? You know what I'm saying, right? That...thing?"
She nods and equably replies, "Sì. I would be more than glad to discuss such matters with you after we are done with tonight's meetup."
Prince Cedric looks over his shoulder, "Marshall? Where is Dr. George, anyway?"
The Sheriff shrugs, "The doc hasn't been seen in...a while. It's not like him to abandon us like that. Not since he and muscle man over there got into an argument over...y'know?"
Amos boldly asserts, "There is no such thing as a Palestinian- never has been, never will be. Simple as. If I hurt that little twerp's feelings, then that ain't my problem. I'm gonna continue being myself, and if he doesn't like that, too fucking bad."
Seneschal Rosalind softly says, "But Shakespeare's writings mention Palestine. And that was way before the establishment of the State of Israel in the forties."
The Bnei Hashem shakes his head, "I don't care. Look, I didn't come all this way to be put under a fucking microscope, alright? I'm here because I'm ready, willing, and able to play ball with you people. But so far, it looks like it's just me doing all the work, making overtures of goodwill and glad tidings to you, and I'm not liking the cold shoulder. Just saying."
Prince Cedric sighs, "Hm. True, you have been making efforts, Mr. Greenberg. And I'm grateful for that. I see that. I appreciate that, too. Respect goes a long, long way in my city."
Seneschal Rosalind clears her throat.
The Prince corrects himself immediately, "Our city. Sorry."
He rubs the base of his neck and mulls some things over before speaking up, "OK, now that that's outta the way. What else is there for you to know? Hmm? Marshall?"
"The power-sharing agreement with those Anarchs down South."
"Ah, right. That. Mm-hmm. We've talked about it before, but I dunno if any of you have heard it before tonight? Amos? Fran? Laurence?"
Shaking heads and shrugs all around.
The Salubri Sheriff sighs, "I've been working with a few of the Anarchs down in Las Estrellas. It isn't...THAT far away from our own Domain. Hell, the Anarchs practically own the Southwestern United States. As such, we strongly believe that it's in our best interests to NOT fight them but rather to have an understanding. We've come to an..."
Seneschal Rosalind takes over, "An agreement. Of sorts. We're hardly in the finalizing stages. Needless to say, YOUR input throughout this process would mean the world to us. Baron Zintoro is not an easy man to deal with. And yes, that is his name. No, don't ask how or why, it's just a...name."
Prince Cedric resumes, "We're trying to convince the other Anarchs to follow suit, but it's a long, long, looong way off. We're still figuring out the details, and we're being very cautious about it. Very."
The Necromancer asks, "What Clan does this Baron Zintoro belong to?"
Sheriff Marshall answers, "Officially? Caitiff. But there are rumors swirling about that he's a Malkavian, although nobody- myself included- has been able to independently verify those claims. So, Caitiff it is. He's your typical, run-of-the-mill Anarch Baron, and the city of Las Estrellas is his Barony."
Laurence growls, "Las Estrellas. That's where all those casinos are, right? Do those Anarchs down there have their fingers in any other pies apart from the biggest one?"
The Sheriff replies, "Not that we know of, no. But like you said, they're pretty big into the whole gambling schtick. They've got more than their fair share of influence."
Amos snickers, "I actually have a bit of money to throw around before the tax people come knocking. Huh, I might swing by when I get some time. They won't try to bite me, right?"
Prince Cedric shakes his head, "If you do decide to go, then you'll be under our protection, Amos. You have my word. Now, I don't endorse gambling or casinos or any shit like that, but I ain't gonna stop other people from doing whatever they want with their money. If you're lucky enough to have it, then use it wisely and enjoy it, I guess."
Seneschal Rosalind shakes her head and sighs a little, "I find the whole thing quite distasteful, but like...Prince Cedric said, it's your money. And yes, we'll be sure to extend our protection to you, Mr. Greenberg. This whole deal with the Anarchs is...fragile."
She stares at the ground and adds, "But we need alliances. Now more so than ever. Even if said alliances are unusual and...a bit weird. It's not as though we're going to see eye-to-eye on everything, but...we have to be able to co-exist. Right?"
Francesca gives a non-committal shrug and quickly jots down a few more points. Her hazel eyes scan the rest of the room before she looks back down and begins furiously scribbling.
Laurence makes a few gurgling noises and wiggles around.
Amos folds his arms and asks, "And what exactly do the Anarchs get out of this itsy-bitsy agreement, hmm? I'm curious, if nothing else."
Prince Cedric tells him, "That's...still a work-in-progress. Right now, we're not quite ready to disclose the finer details. Once we have them figured out, then we'll announce it. Formally."
Sheriff Marshall speaks up, "The port murders are still...concerning. If any of your people happen to know anything, please, don't hold back. Come to us. We can protect you. Innocent people are dying."
Francesca sincerely nods, "I will see how the Hecata Clan can contribute to this matter, Sheriff Marshall. Our...resources are at your full disposal."
The Salubri Prince of this Domain smiles widely, "Now, that's what I like to hear. Fran, make sure you stay back- we are gonna need to have ourselves a private chat after the meetup. And I'm talkin' about important stuff; real important stuff."
Laurence can't help but ask, "How come I'm not invited?"
Seneschal Rosalind fixes him with a sympathetic look, "I know how...inquisitive you Nosferatu can get, but this is simply a private matter between us, Miss Livroni, and the Clan of Death as a whole. Please, try to understand."
Amos lowers his voice, "She's telling you to keep your snout outta this one, Laurence."
The Nossie picks up on that remark and says elects to say nothing further. He clasps his deformed hands together and hangs his head.
Francesca taps her left hand- her gloved hand- on her thick leather journal and smiles politely.
Seneschal Rosalind puts on a smile and declares, "Well, then. Cedric, honey? Anything else to add or shall we conclude this meeting? I'm sure that our...friends have other places to be, people to meet."
The remainder of this Batcave meetup is quick and goes off without too much of a hitch, hiccup, or kerfuffle.
Whoever must leave, does so. Amos doesn't bother with saying a proper goodbye; he just shoves his earphones back on and storms off. He's obviously in a deeply foul mood.
Laurence hobbles away, but through the loose grate leading down, deeper into the cavern. Using his preternatural strength, he effortlessly tears the thang off, leaving the opening large enough for his twisted body to pass through.
The moneyed Sewer Rat grumbles about getting his suit dirty while making a phone call, "Yeah. It's me. Bring the car around. I'll be waiting. Mm-hmm."
Sheriff Marshall's eyebrows go up, "Wow."
Seneschal Rosalind smirks, "Indeed."
Francesca patiently waits. She holds onto her Gucci bag and fidgets with the threads along the edges of her black skirt. She gulps and looks around the cavernous chamber, "I have another engagement, but they can wait for me."
In time, the four vampires get to talking about...certain matters. Fran, for her part, intently and keenly listens to what the other three have to say. The Hecata Whip even has a few things to say, but she mostly lets the others do the talking.
Their conversation is a very serious one. They discuss many, many things. A great deal of it is rather...Morbid.
Elsewhere...
Amos is in his sedan, simply cruising along a (mostly) empty stretch of road. His pale face is scrunched up in a deep frown, and his jaw is clenched. He slows down when he comes across a random group of people lolling about.
He eyes them with an intensity unlike any other and growls, "I am so Hungry, so very Hungry. Let's see what's on the menu tonight..."
The False Assamite stops his car, turns off the ignition, and steps out. The night is quiet. Very, very quiet. But sunrise is nearly around the corner, so he better make this quick.
And quiet. Preferably. But Amos Greenberg hasn't been too shy about leaving exsanguinated bodies in his wake. And he doesn't show a great deal of remorse about his many victims, either.
Back to Lena...
The PI eventually falls asleep. It was quite the struggle at first but sleep she does. And a dream, a dream of endless water comes for her. Yet again, she is cast into the bottomless ocean. The water fills her lungs, and she's pulled down by its irresistible force.
Yet, there is no drowning sensation. None whatsoever. She is...fine. In fact, she's perfectly fine. In the waking world, her chest rises and falls while she rests. Her breathing is soft and gentle. Nay a snore escapes from her lips.
The sun is now up. The Kindred in this Domain are now being pulled into the clutches of the day-sleep. In time, Lena Adams will be dragged into their dark vortex.
Until then, the mortal PI remains none-the-wiser about the existence of vampires...and the others that go bump in the night.
Today is simply another day at work. Lena kinda resents the fact that even Saturdays are workdays for her. But alas, money is money, and she needs every last penny. Not just for herself, but for her family as well.
Her father's medical bills are nothing to sneeze at. And her mother's own salary is hardly enough for them to get by, even if it's just the two of them.
After getting showered and dressed, Lena grabs the newspaper and flicks it open. Her amber eyes scan over the headlines, and she mumbles a few things under her breath. She turns a few pages and her face lights up a little.
Magnolia City has recently been voted the top five most LGBTQ-friendly cities in the Northwestern United States! Absolutely great for a person like Lena.
But the cost of living has been going up these past few months. Lena looks at her recent utility bills and already feels a small headache coming on- nothing copious amounts of coffee won't be able to fix!
(The headaches, not the bills).
Lena pointedly asks no one in particular, "Here in America, why is it so frowned upon to live with your parents or--or extended family members? Auntie Esther would've loved to have me stay with her, right?"
She chuckles a little, "Heh. Maybe. OK, off to work now. At least Sundays are my day off. Lemme see if I can get a date to top this tiring week off."
The young woman gets herself together and heads to the front door.
Lena, for her part, wasn't initially willing to part ways with her parents and brother. The first few weeks were quite difficult. But soon enough, she grew to accept the fact that she was going to have to live on her own.
Still, a part of her does feel...lonely. And being so far away from home (or what counts as "home") does not make it any easier for her to cope at times.
Oh, the many times the Private Investigator had to fight back the tears!
She swings by GROUND BEANZ, grabs some quick grub, a very large cup of coffee, and consumes them without too great of a fuss. She buys another small cup of coffee, refuses to tip, and walks out.
But she does feel somewhat bad for those tired, baggy-eyed baristas who have to fulfill all sortsa crazy and wacky orders. Some soccer mom came in and showed the young barista something on her phone- likely some trendy, fancy drink- and expected the barista to whip it up immediately.
The plights of a sub-minimum wage employee in the hellscape that is the service and hospitality industry.
Crystal Street Station looks...the same. But there are parts of it which are starting to show their age. A couple of the tracks have even become rusted. Some of the grimy grime on the walls has a certain tint to it.
So far, these issues seem fixable and won't cost the city much. It's just a question of whether the powers-that-be (tm) deem it worth fixing.
While waiting for the choo-choo train to arrive, Lena tosses her small cup into the colored bin and leans against a railing. She's not quite in a hurry, not yet anyway.
She's got time, so she pulls out her phone and opens the dating app titled: "FemmeConnect". A few notifications pop up, indicating that certain ladies have shown interest in the PI who works for Pluto Enterprises by day.
A part of her wants to open her messages and see who's interested in chatting her up. The train isn't anywhere close, so...Lena knows that she has time to spare, and it's not like her to be all shy and bashful about initiating a conversation.
A message pops up in the app, and it's from a person named Elise. No profile picture, but her bio does mention that works in a salon as a hairdresser, makeup artist, and nail technician.
Elise's brief (and generic) message reads: Hey, so I was wondering if you'd like to go for a walk and maybe grab something? You seem like a beautiful woman, Lena."
Lena bites her lower lip and types back: "Elise. Cute name. And sure, why not? It's a nice day out. How about this evening?"
Miss Adams wasn't expecting Elise to be online, but the reply she gets is instant, and a smile creeps onto her face.
It reads: "Can't, love, sorry. Busy @ work. Salons, you know how they are. Especially on weekends! We're practically booked! But we should schedule something. You seem like an interesting woman. I would very much like to stay in touch with you."
While Lena thinks of what to type back, she asks herself, "I wonder what Yvonne would think of her. Oh, well..."
She slowly types back: "That sounds wonderful. BTW, if you want more interest in your profile, add a picture- doesn't matter if it's grainy or a few years older. Otherwise, gals on here will think you're trying to pull a fast one on them. And you might lose future dates. Or get banned from the app altogether."
Miss Adams sends a wink and a kiss emoji. Hmm, spicy!
Elise responds with a string of heart and smiling face emojis. Cute!
The train hisses to a stop, and the passengers either alight or board.
Lena is the latter. She boards the train and takes a seat near the center. A scruffy man wearing fingerless gloves is fast asleep on the other side, his snoring filling the otherwise quiet train car. A small red cup sits in his lap. The quarters inside jingle when he shifts a little.
A mother with two young children is seated a few paces away from him. She grimaces slightly, making sure to keep her kids away from the man's scarred feet. A young man with curly hair sits near the window and plays some music from his earphones.
Lena sighs, fishes into her bag, and pulls out a few coins. She slowly rises up and tosses them into the cup, "Here, I hope it helps you out, buddy."
Snores and sniffs are the only replies she gets.
When the train arrives at the next station, the people disembark and board. The doors close, and the train speeds off, continuing its journey north.
Lena walks into the Pluto Enterprises office and greets everyone. Not a single person is too bothered about her being right on time, like always. She dutifully clocks in, takes off her duffel coat and drapes it over the back of her chair.
She turns on her computer and gets...started. And to think that her Saturdays are gonna start and end the same way, every week, for however long this job lasts.
Lena is a little bit sad that she's going to be spending her such a lovely day in this office, but hey, it's not that bad of a space. Aesthetically speaking, of course.
But in Magnolia City, there are far better ways to spend one's Saturdays. The sun is out in full force, beaming and shining on the mortals, and the sky is clear. It's a good day, a great day, and a day well spent.
At least that's what Lena is hoping. After a few hours of non-stop staring at the screen and doing...spreadsheet stuff, Lena's phone vibrates. It's a message from one of her close friends.
It reads: "Yo, Leens, gurl. U free 2day? Me and Tobes planning 2 grab sum grub @ this Greek place. Gyros and all. U down?"
Lena smiles a bit. Her fingers glide over the virtual keyboard: "I'll take an early lunchbreak. Still at work. Don't worry, I'll be there, Eddie. How's the garage doing? How's Toby keeping? I've been meaning to contact him, but work- both PE and PI- have had me busy lately."
Eddie texts back: "Toby's doing fine. Taking his...boy meds or juice or whatever they're called. We should catch up. And catching up over Gyros is always a good thing, right? Heh. be there Soon. Don't leave us hanging. But we won't ever start munching without you."
Lena's smile grows wider, and she replies: "OK, see you in a few hours. Text me the address to this Greek place."
Her friend does so and she stares at the address, nods firmly, and returns to work. It's not a long wait until the time for Lena's lunchbreak comes up. She gulps down the last bits of her seventh cup of coffee and tosses the paper cup into the appropriate recycling bin.
Lena checks her reflection in the mirror and wipes off the droplets of coffee on her lips.
Well, it's time for some Gyros and catching up with friends!
The Greek restaurant in question has a rustic vibe to it- dark wooden tables and chairs, glass windows with an excellent view of an abstract art museum a few feet away, and the faint scent of lamb meat is in the air.
The bells chime when Lena walks into the restaurant. There are quite a few people present in this establishment. While it lacks in an air-conditioning system, the patrons aren't complaining about it. But there are an adequate number of oscillating fans mounted on the ceilings to make up for it.
Lena sees two men seated at a square table, they're both on their phones and chuckling at something or the other. From their backs alone, she recognizes them.
She quietly circles around them and slides into an empty chair. Eddie and Toby both look up from their phones and smile at her.
They both smile and greet their friend. It's been a long time since they last got together. Adult responsibilities and shit- bills, rent, mortgages, taxes, vehicle insurance, life insurance, debt repayments- yada yada yada- keep getting in the way.
Eddie Rivera-Torres works as a mechanic (cars, bikes, all kinds of machine-beauties) in a local garage. He has caramel skin, an uneven hairline, and is well into his thirties. A bad attempt at a ducktail beard completes his look- last time, he had a chinstrap beard. It's currently gone.
He hails from Puerto Rico, but has spent a significant portion of his adult life in the US of A.
"Yo, yo, yo, Leens! Wazzup, gurl!" Eddie greets her, and his smile grows even wider when she leans over and gives him a big hug.
Toby Fetscher works in the same garage alongside Eddie- they've been pals long before Lena herself showed up. He has very fair and freckled skin, a big nose, curly auburn hair, and wears a blue, checkered shirt. Like Eddie, his build is somewhat wiry.
He is a transgender man who is still very much in the process of not only transitioning but also figuring out the whole "manhood" thing. He's at that awkward, uncomfortable, and extremely crucial stage of life, so to speak.
Toby's friends- mainly Lena and Eddie- have supported him throughout his journey. He's come a very long way since then.
Lena takes Toby's hands in hers and smiles warmly, "Hi, Toby. I missed you. Both of you."
Eddie chuckles, "So, Gyros, right?"
He looks at the menu card and exclaims, "They got so many here! Hey, look, they even have an option to make your own. Tobes, you might dig that, hmm?"
Toby concedes with a chuckle of his own, his voice is quite low and husky, "Heh, I would. I'm pretty hungry, Eddie. That last oil change took a lot longer than it was supposed to."
Eddies smiles back at him, "It's the pay and benefits, right? So, Lena, how have things been with you, chica? How's the week treated you? We got ourselves a lotta catching up to do."
Toby puts in, "We--we do. And I also have some stuff for you. If...you're OK with taking them home?"
Lena has an inkling of what they are, "Sure thing. Yeah, we should. But let's make our orders before we do the whole catching up thing, huh?"
The trio summon a waiter- a dashing young man with a tan, windswept hair, and a very long Greek name- and order their respective dishes.
The PI orders a fish gyro; Eddie goes for two vegan gyros with extra pomegranates; lastly, Toby asks for a large beef gyro but without the mayo. A complementary plate of salad and dips is also delivered to the table.
Toby breaks the ice with a question, "So...detective Lena Adams. Any new cases or is it just the same old, same old? Or are you currently on somebody's trail right now?"
Lena shakes her head, "Nah. Well, I do have...something that isn't the usual, but I can't quite...tell either of you about it. This one's way more sensitive than some alleged steamy affair with an intern at a prestigious law firm."
She rubs her face and adds with a groan, "And this whole damn week has been exhausting."
(The PI isn't too comfy with sharing the deets of her new case- and rightly so!)
Eddie pops a carrot into his mouth, "You got a new case."
"I got a new case," Lena vaguely confirms with a nod of her head.
Toby pokes the cucumber slices with his fork, grimaces, and decides not to have it, "What about your actual job? How's that been coming along? You know, I still tell myself that your job at Pluto Enterprises has absolutely nothing to do with the solar system, hahaha!"
Lena laughs along, "Yeah. Heh. It's the usual song-and-dance routine. Anyway, enough about me. How's the garage doing? How's it treating the two of you?"
Toby shifts around, "Eh. Maybe there's more variety compared to your office job. Eddie, you're way better at this than I am. Tell her about the low-lows."
Eddie snickers, "He means the lowriders. Haha! Yeah, we got a lowrider to fix up. Client's from Flimont City- y'know, that crazy place in the Midwest with non-existent public transport and all? The dude's got money to throw around for his baby, so he just left the ride here with us and told us to make sure that it's as good as new."
Toby interjects, "'Even better than new'. His words. And when I shook his hand, it...was kinda cold. Weird."
Eddie nudges Toby with his elbow, "Ah, don't jinx us. We need the business, Tobes. We gotta finish it up, polish the car, and deliver it to him on time. And I smell lunch coming our way."
He rubs his palms together and expectantly stares at the plates of food being set in front of them. When the waiter's arm brushes near Toby's shoulder, the latter inadvertently flinches and lets out a small whimper.
Lena sympathetically winces, as does Eddie. The waiter's eyes widen in shock. Toby goes silent and freezes in place. His jaw goes slack.
Eddie is quick to reassure the waiter, "Hey, hey, not your fault, buddy. Long day. It's fine, we got it. Don't worry, we won't sue you or anything. Just...don't talk tell anybody about this, yeah?"
The waiter nods and shuffles away, sparing a look over his shoulder as he does so.
Toby fights back the tears and swallows hard. He's trembling a little bit. Eddie reaches over and gently pats his friend on the shoulder. Lena reaches forward and cups Toby's hands in hers.
They both recognize the fear in Toby's gray eyes. He is, after all, a victim of intimate partner violence (IPV). Beneath most of his long-sleeved shirts are scars of the various beatings and strangulations he's endured at the hands of his former partner.
Lena gently shushes him, "Shh, shh. It's fine. Those days are long behind you. You don't have to worry about it anymore, Toby. We're here."
Eddie nods along, "That puta is long gone now. C'mon, let's dig in, alright? You want a small piece of one of my gyros?"
Toby blinks and shakes his head, "I--I'm fine. Thanks, you two. Uh, yeah, let's eat." He lowers his head and stares at his meal for a few seconds longer than usual, his teary eyes lingering on the pita bread and the contents therein.
He picks up his knife and fork, grips them a bit too tightly, and takes a few breaths. The mechanic then looks at the abstract sculpture in front of that abstract museum, but the faraway, thousand-yard stare is quite evident on his face.
He tries to focus on something else- anything else.
Eddie chews his food and offers, "We love you and we care for you, man. No matter what, right, Lena?"
"Always."
They spend a few moments, silently eating. Halfway through the meal, Toby gets up and goes to fetch some items from the car which he and Eddie arrived in. His hands have stopped trembling.
He sits down, takes a long, long drink, and looks out of the window. A large plastic bag full of clothes rests on the empty chair next to Lena.
Eddie looks at the bag and asks, "What's with all this? Oh. Right." He goes back to munching on his food.
Lena looks at the bag, too. Her eyebrows shoot up a little, "Wow. Are you sure? Hey, I mean if you wanna give more to charity, shelters, and thrift stores, then I'm OK with it. This is...a lot."
Toby shakes his head, "I already gave most of it away, don't worry. Go on, take a look inside. I--I do hope it's to your liking, though..." The shyness in his tone is fairly evident.
Lena nods and pulls the bag over to her. She wipes her hands with a tissue and takes a peek inside. It's mostly t-shirts, blouses, sweaters, and coats from the time when Toby was still known as Celeste.
The gumshoe pulls out a random black t-shirt that's on the tighter side of things. She squints at the words scrawled on it and reads out, "'Mostly Void. Partially Stars.' Huh. Any idea what that means?"
Toby pokes a tomato apart and shrugs, "Dunno. Sorry. I even forgot who and when I got it from. But I do remember the where, though. Uh...New Jersey. Rahway. Yeah, there. I...wow, that feels like a really long time ago, huh."
Eddie slightly groans, "Aww, c'mon! Don't remind me of gettin' old, Tobes. I know that we all gotta do it eventually. Someday. But not today. Not anytime soon, y'know? I don't wanna dwell on it."
Lena shoves the t-shirt back inside the plastic bag and sets it aside, "I've actually been to Rahway for a sabbatical. Honestly, it was nothing special."
Eddie asks her, "Would you go again? Judging by how baggy your eyes are, you could use a nice, long break."
Lena scoffs, "To Rahway? Nah, not really. Believe it or not, but I'm actually quite comfortable here in Magnolia City. And I have my commitments, too; I can't just up and leave whenever the first whimsical urge comes to me. Plus, I don't really have anyone to go on those trips with, anyway."
Toby incredulously asks, "Still no special lady in your life?"
"Nope," Lena shakes her head, "And I'm OK with that."
(In truth, she is quite lonely in that aspect. She craves for a lady's long-term companionship, the sensuous touch of another woman, the warmth of another fine vixen's bosom. But she doesn't wanna admit that- at least not right now!)
She changes the subject, "Thanks for the clothes, Toby. It saves up on a few trips to the stores."
(Lena usually shops at the thrift stores, but she's not gonna openly admit that anytime soon. A majority of her clothes are from those kinds of places, actually. Not that she's complaining! They're perfectly good and wearable. And durable after a great many tumble-washes, too!)
The mechanic scratches the back of his neck and gives her a thumbs-up, "Sure thing. Always glad to help a close friend out. Right, Ed?"
Ed has some dipping sauce dripping down his chin. He quickly wipes it off with his palm, leaving streaks of it smeared all over. He smiles, "I'm glad you got a whole new wardrobe, Leens. Heh-heh." He awkwardly reaches out for a tissue and dabs at the remaining stains on his face.
The conversation eventually segues into hobbies and extracurriculars.
Ed declares, "So, I was thinking of taking up a few pottery classes at Magnolia City Community College. Leens? Interested?"
"Nope."
Toby shrugs and shyly says, "I'm actually OK with what I've got right now- working on cars is fulfilling in its own way. I'm not that artistic or anything, I don't think so, anyways. I'm pretty sure that I'd make a mess if--if I tried the pottery thing, uh...Eddie."
The other mechanic rubs his friend's back and encourages him, "Aww, c'mon. Don't say that. If it ain't for you, then it ain't for you. But I'm signing myself up. Next batch starts on...Wednesday, I think. I gotta check the schedules, though."
Lena munches on her gyro and asks, "Eddie, this is a bit of a strange question. But over the past few weeks, did a car with a dent- maybe a bit of blood- come by your garage? It's...for something that I'm looking into. Did you or Toby see anything like that? It's...important."
Eddie abruptly shifts in his seat and uncomfortably scratches his chin. He shakes his head from side to side, "Um...no. Tobes?"
"Not that I recall. Nope. If we did, though, we'd have instantly reported it to the authorities. I dunno how useful that'd be- we all know how useless they are in this city- but...we would've remembered something like that pulling up to the garage. Ed? The uh...surgeries over these past few weeks have been taxing."
The PI nods, "It's fine. Ed? Anything? Anything at all?"
Eddie gulps some water down, pours himself another glass from the pitcher, and shakes his head again. He sighs and runs his fingers through his hair, "Nope. Like Tobes said: we'd have reported it. But we didn't see...a thing outta the ordinary. No dents, definitely no blood. Just carburetors, overdue oil changes, and sparkplugs, y'know? Hey, I've had my share of oddball customers, but this? Nah."
Toby shakes his head, "Yeah, he's right, Lena. These past few weeks have been decidedly mundane. Heh." He chuckles nervously, and the laugh sounds somewhat forced.
Lena's brows knit together in deep thought, and her fingers drum on the table, "You're sure?"
Eddie's face tics and he casually shrugs, "Mm-hmm. Now, c'mon, don't glower at us like that- you have a habit of doing that. We're your friends, Leens. We love you, well not that kinda love, of course. But uh...you get what I'm saying, right? Tobes?"
Toby softly smiles and doesn't say much else except nod along to Eddie's words. His friend has always been able to cheer him up. And that usually goes in both directions.
Lena's tense expression softens, but only a little. Yet, there is still that scrutinizing glare in her amber eyes, and her fingers continue to drum.
Eddie excuses himself and shuffles off to the bathroom. Toby averts his gaze and stares longingly at the abstract sculpture in front of that aforementioned museum.
He muses, "What...does it signify? What is it supposed to be about? All that bent and twisted metal. Huh. MC cubed is offering a few classes on the subject. Maybe? Should I?"
("MC cubed" is what some people call Magnolia City Community College, well because of the three Cs in its name.)
Lena regards Toby with a somewhat sympathetic look, "How's everything going? You haven't seen any suspicious people in the garage, have you? Dents, blood--"
Tobes clicks his tongue, "C'mon, Lena. I work with Ed in the garage day in and day out. Sometimes we even do double-digit shifts. I'd know if there was a car that came in with a dent or a fresh bloodstain. And Eddie would sure as hell notice."
He turns his attention to her and sighs, "Unlike your nice office job, being a mechanic is a very physical, hands-on kind of thing. It's not all about computers and the Internet, y'know? And after I'm done, I get to go back to my rent-controlled apartment, watch a few minutes of TV, try and get some sleep, get up, and get ready to do it all over again. That's the life."
Lena dismissively states, "I get it- it's not easy. And for the record, my job isn't just about computers and the Internet, OK?"
Toby looks away and lowers his voice, "Can we please not do this now? Look, we had a nice lunch, caught up, and got to hang out with each other. Let's not end it on a sour note? Please?"
Lena waves her hand, "Sure. Whatever. Yeah. I--I should probably head out. Oh, Eddie's coming. Hmph."
Ed chuckles heartily, totally oblivious to the tension between the two of his friends. He sits down and grins from ear to ear. But his grin slowly fades away when he sees the all-too-familiar scowl on Lena's face.
He winces and pulls out his wallet. He snaps his fingers and asks for the check, even going so far as to offer to pay for everyone's meals. Usually, the three of them pay for their own meal, but Ed insists that today's lunch is on him.
"Uh, this one's on me. Don't worry."
He does not want to be a part of whatever awkwardness is happening between either of them.
Toby shakily asks, "Are you sure? But didn't you say that your grandparents had--"
Eddie interjects, "Ah, it's taken care of. I got myself a lil' extra spending money. Just wanted to treat y'all. And, yeah, my grandparents are doing good. Thanks for asking."
(He has been living with his grandparents ever since the 2008 financial crisis. He had to move back in with them after they lost everything- including their old house. Now, they all live in a place that, while not terrible, is starting to get a bit cramped.)
Ed smugly chuckles and counts out his extra dollar bills; he even leaves a very generous tip for the waiter. Now, Lena has known him for years now. He's always iffy about tipping, only doing so out of guilt or so the staff won't give him the stink eye.
Lena can't help but silently wonder to herself, "Where exactly did he get that extra cash from? He always says that times are tough- which they are! But...how?"
Eddie notices Lena side-eyeing him, so he subtly shakes his head. He knows that look all too damn well. In time, the trio says their goodbyes, and the friends split up.
Lena grabs the bag of clothing and saunters back to her office.
Eddie walks up to that abstract sculpture and strokes his chin while inspecting it from various angles. Toby shoves his hands in his pockets and looks around, seeing all the college kids idly relaxing on the ground, the trees, and even the fountain in the far distance. A few birds are flying over.
A few of them land on a bench. The unoccupied ones, that is.
Eddie calls out, "Hey, take a pic of me in front of this thing. Tobes!"
Toby pulls out his cracked smartphone, taps his thumb on the camera app, and squints.
(He doesn't have a nice camera, but the phone does the job in a pinch.)
Eddie takes a single knee and flashes double peace signs with both of his hands. His cap is on backwards and his expression is fairly laidback.
Toby lowers his phone, taps a few more things, and looks at the photo. Ed comes up and nods approvingly.
"Cool! Hey, get that cracked glass fixed sometime soon, hmm? C'mon, let's head back to the garage. So, do you think I'd be a good fit for pottery classes? I've always wanted to try my jazz hands at something new."
"I think you'll like it. How much is it, anyway?"
"Eh, I got the cash for it."
Toby lowers his head and slows down a bit, "Y--you're not withholding anything from Lena, right? I--I mean, you were acting kinda funny and weird when she asked about the dents and the blood."
Ed waves his hand dismissively, "C'mon, Tobes. You know how Lena Adams can be sometimes."
"But if we saw something like that, then we--we'd absolutely report it, ri--right?"
Eddie fixes his friend with a dull gaze, "What're you getting at? Look, we're cool. I didn't see nothing. You didn't see nothing, either."
Toby pointedly asks, "Then how did you get the extra money, Ed??"
"What, a man can't spend a little extra cash on his friends?"
Toby nervously rubs the base of his neck and picks up the pace, noticing how briskly Eddie is walking. He sighs and tries again, "Are--are you doing something...you shouldn't be doing?"
Eddie adjusts the cap on his head and sighs heavily, "What?! No! I just got some extra money to throw around, that's all. Don't read sooooo much into it. Hey, maybe all those boy meds that you're on have a--a...uh...side effect?"
Woops.
Toby glares at Ed and stops in his tracks. He stares at his friend.
Eddie realizes his mistake and scratches the back of his head, "I--I didn't mean it like that. You know I've always supported you throughout not only your transition journey but also your healing process after the whole...abuse thing."
Toby holds up a finger, his voice faltering, "J--just don't. Please. And yes, I know how supportive you and Lena have been. E--especially you, Ed. But--"
The mechanic lets out a strained chuckle and continues, "But it does get to a point where it feels a bit condescending at times. I'm not trying to be an ungrateful asshat or anything. If you don't wanna talk about where or how you got the money, then that's...fine, I guess. I don't wanna pry."
Eddie puts his arm around Toby's shoulder and walks with him, "Look, it's fine. We all got our own secrets, hmm? And it's not like Leens is Miss Perfect or something. I mean, you know how...dismissive she can be at times."
Toby's jaw clenches, "Yeah. She can be pretty dismissive and come off as rude. I remember how she let loose on that cosplayer from last year's comic con. Whoof! Sheesh, she just blurted it out to the poor girl."
Eddie shakes his head, "Heh, yeah. Lena absolutely hates that nerd stuff and all those comic book game conventions and whatnot. And she makes no secret of it, either. Damn, I still remember that cosplayer chick was on the brink of tears after our PI friend tore a strip outta her. Good thing no one recorded the fucking thing. That could've been a lawsuit waiting to happen."
Toby sucks some air through his teeth, "It could have. But hey, we're all adults and mature enough to know how to handle ourselves. Lena's still...a great person; she means well. And it's not like either of us are flawless. We all make mistakes and have...weird quirky things that sorta define us but also don't."
"Mm-hmm. I get what you're saying, Tobes. C'mon. So, how'd the last surgery go. I'm not prying, I don't wanna make this weird, and I'm not trying to make you uncomfortable on purpose- I just wanna know how you're doing. It's been a while since your last one, right?"
"Hm. Yeah. Mind if I take off early today? I got an appointment with a voice coach. You know, to help me get rid of the last remnants of the whole...squeaky--"
"I know, I know. Yeah, go on. Ed's always got your back. Always."
"T--thank you. You have no idea how--how much this means to me, Eddie..."
The rest of Lena's afternoon goes by in a blur of spreadsheets, work, numbers and letters on a computer screen, tons of photocopies, lots of coffee being guzzled down, and some additional paperwork.
By the time work ends, she's tired. She rubs the bridge of her nose, stands up, and walks out of her workplace without saying goodbye or even bothering to announce her immediate departure.
Her co-workers either remain glued to their screens or have made their own exits hours earlier.
The sun is setting, and the air is getting cooler. Small mercies. By the time Lena gets to her PI office, the sky above is in a shade of blue-black, and the streetlights have already flickered on.
She steps into her office, opens the window, and flops down on her chair with a long and drawn-out groan-sigh. The PI spins on her chair a few times, takes off the red scrunchie holding her ponytail in place, shakes her head around, and lets it all cascade down to her shoulders.
Lena reaches out for the mug sitting on her desk and inspects it with a fairly bland expression etched onto her face. Her eyes dart away, and her nose wrinkles.
Time for some coffee! After she gets herself a steaming cup, she sits down and begins going over the relevant information pertaining to Tyler's case.
She sips her coffee, reads a few documents, and writes down a few notes. Her bleary-eyed stare goes from page to page, the tiredness being staved off by the constant intakes of caffeine.
Soon, the coffee runs out, and Lena is still stuck with her current task. This is gonna go on for hours. Such is the life of a snoop in Magnolia City! She does break up the monotony, however, by occasionally checking messages on FemmeConnect, hoping for something good.
Elise has shown great interest, as have a few other chicks. Lena immediately discounts anyone younger than her, anyone who has vaguely nerd-adjacent stuff in their pictures, or those who have pictures of their pets instead of their actual faces.
She also grunts in derision when video games, anime, and/or comic books are listed as hobbies or interests on the profiles of the other prospective matches. Lena has nothing but contempt for such things.
(The gumshoe even goes so far as to block those women's profiles.)
Lena's attention is drawn away from her phone by the sound of a nearby door opening and closing. Must be one of the neighboring small bidness owners locking up for the night and heading home. Lucky them.
Elsewhere...
Laurence Lancaster, Neonate of Clan Nosferatu stands in front of a massive mansion made of sandstone. His hunched-over form is dressed in a dark gray suit and teal tie. A scarf goes around his hideously deformed neck.
He hobbles up to the front door with a grunt and brings his twisted hand to the doorbell. After a few rings, the door is promptly opened by a tall man dressed in a somewhat similar manner to Laurence.
The expressionless butler bows his head, steps aside, and wordlessly ushers the Nosferatu inside.
The butler's thin lips part as a tiny gasp escapes from them. His expression remains unchanged, however, and he ushers the Nossie down a hall and into the foyer.
On the inside, the tiled floor is pristine, polished to a smooth, shiny finish. The wooden wall paneling appears new, and the ceiling is adorned with high chandeliers whose diamonds and other precious stones glitter and gleam.
Curved dual staircases ascend up to a first-floor landing. The railings are made of dark wood, as are the bannisters.
This place oozes old world luxe vibes in every nook and cranny.
