And yet, a great many modern conveniences have been baked in- hidden surveillance cameras in angles no one would ever think to look at, multiple exits and escape routes, and the ability to bring the shutters down should the need call for it.
Laurence waits in the big-ass sitting room. He stumbles onto a leather sofa and throws his head back. Normally, the owner of this Haven wouldn't permit such things, but Laurence and said owner are on speaking terms, so he's allowed his eccentricities.
After a few minutes, a woman walks in, carrying two empty wine glasses on them. She silently sets them on the glass table, gulps fearfully, and quickly takes her leave.
Laurence's grubby fingers reach out, and he inspects the wine glass with a toothy smile, "Otto sure knows how to entertain a guest."
A deep and menacing laugh echoes from behind him. It bounces off the walls and enters Laurence's eardrums. He turns around, but there is nobody there. Another bout of laughter soon follows.
Laurence's fingers tense and quiver.
An unusually tall and bald man with pointy ears, a twisted, pimply face, and blood-red eyes casually walks into the room. He is adorned in a long black coat that barely touches his knees, slim-fitting pants, and polished shoes.
His cracked lips part, revealing chipped yellow teeth, some of which are missing. His big ears twitch, and his dead eyes slowly roam over to the wine glasses.
Laurence sighs, "Otto..."
The Nosferatu Primogen of Magnolia City replies, "Laurence Lancaster." Without saying anymore, he sets his walking stick aside and swiftly sits on the opposite sofa.
The two vampires regard each other, the silence between them deafening.
Otto Weinbauer's grin is more eerie than welcoming, "How was last night's meeting, my friend?"
Laurence shrugs, "We spoke. Not many were there- just that Italian chick, that Jewish guy, and me. And how can we forget--"
"The Council of Nine Eyes. Hmph. But Cedric our Prince enables us to have a good thing here. So, don't knock it. Anything of note? Skip the boring parts, please. These nights, I find myself having less and less patience for the affairs of the Court."
Laurence grunts, "You never had any patience."
Otto counters, "Hah! You're one to talk. I knew you since you were a fucking Fledgling. Remember those initial nights, when you were kicking, screaming, scratching, biting, and all of that? You were in denial of what she did to you, but you came around...eventually. And how very convenient of your Sire to take her leave after turning you. Heh."
"I--I'm a different person now."
"No, you're not. Stop lying to me- and to yourself. If there's nothing of note that happened at last night's meeting, then I'm not interested. Like I always say: why knock it? Prince Three Eyes enables us Nossies, gives us a wide berth to Embrace, feed, do our thing, and all that jazz."
Laurence languidly gestures to the empty glasses, "Is that shit just for show or are you planning to share some?"
Otto's mouth twists into a smirk, "It's freshly squeezed. Patience. What's a few wasted minutes to creatures of eternity, hmm? Any news on my Banu Haqim counterpart yet? Have they found him? I've sent my feelers out in every conceivable direction."
Laurence leans forward, "And?"
"Tch. And nothing. And that makes me very disappointed. You know how it is, don't you?"
The other Crawler nods, "Mm-hmm. Cryptophilia. We just gotta know shit. It doesn't matter. We just...gotta know it. No matter who- or what- stands in our way. And we sure as heck ain't in the sharing business- not unless it's for a bigger, better secret."
Otto laughs, "Our Clan's Compulsion is a wonderful way to excuse otherwise inexcusable...actions, wouldn't you agree?"
"Sure. And what are you doing these nights? Still pretending to be into fancy wines and shit?"
"Well, I am forever fifty-five. So, I am caught in a permanent, never-ending flux of being in and out of a midlife crisis. Heh. Besides, being a sommelier is a great cover. For all sorts of things."
"Mm-hmm. And the name kinda fits. Wein...bauer. What was it last decade?"
Otto dismissively waves his hand, "Weinbauer is the name I'm going with for this decade, possibly longer. And you're right, it fits neatly- like a glove- with the sommelier cover. It's just perfect, right?"
Laurence's nose twitches, "I--I guess so."
"What's with the sour look on your ugly face, huh? C'mon, spit it out, Lancaster. And for fuck's sake, get a name change already. You can certainly afford it- I don't see why you still stubbornly hold onto it like it means anything anymore. Ditch it. Unless you're trying to get us all turned to piles of ashes--"
Laurence twitches and emphatically insists, "I'm NOT! I'll work on my ID when I feel like it, not when you tell me to. And, c'mon, where's the blood? I'm tired of staring at two empty wine glasses!"
Otto shakes his head, "Impatient just like you were during the very first night of your transformation. Ah, some people. You know the deal, Laurence. The blood? When it's time. Don't rush the process. It's delicate."
"Is that the same thing you tell all your sommelier friends?"
"Hah! Unlike you, I can actually pass for something resembling a not-ugly human being and mingle among polite society. You, on the other hand, got the fuck-you end of our Clan's Repulsiveness. And that's just great and all, but it does come with its fair share of challenges, hmm?"
Laurence mumbles, "Don't remind me of it."
Otto waggles a bent and crooked finger, "But see, that's just the thing..." He trails off when the same woman from before shakily returns with a tall wine bottle. She sets it down, avoids looking at either man, and promptly exits the room.
Otto sniffs at the air, "Hm. Earthy with a hit of fear and anxiety. Very interesting."
Laurence reaches out, but Otto swats his hand away, "Ah, ah, ah. Manners. My Haven- my mansion- my rules. Keep that Beast of yours in check. Seriously. Do I do that when I'm in your digs, huh? Do I?"
Mr. Lancaster forcefully sighs and shakes his head, "No. You don't. But I'm Hungry! And it's getting increasingly harder to procure vessels ever since what happened."
"Heh, yeah. RIP. Oh, well, at least I still have my ways..." Otto trails off, opens the wine bottle, and pours out two generous glasses of deep red liquid. Its thickness reminds Laurence of tar for some reason.
The vampires raise their glasses and drink their respective sanguine portions.
Otto smacks his lips, lets out a content sigh, and throws his misshapen head back. His neck is completely twisted, and his clammy skin is unnaturally tight, so much so that the veins beneath are visible.
Laurence averts his gaze and feels the roaring Beast within him calming down, seemingly satiated by the recent treat.
Otto rubs his stomach, "Aaaah...that hits all the sore spots."
"You were saying something before we got interrupted. I don't like interruptions or half-ass conversations. Out with it. Now."
Otto grins fiendishly, "Well, look at you. It looks like the blood gave you some Dutch courage, huh? Is this how you always treated your employees back at Lancaster-SYS? Hey, don't get me wrong, I respect the way you do things- I really do. But...I'm not them. I'm not some fucking drone that you can push around like a toy or a piece of furniture."
Laurence shakes his head, "I know. Sorry."
"There's your second mistake. Apologizing. It weakens you, makes you like some of those other sad sacks who bear our Clan's name in other Domains- all huddled together like rats in their sewers. Stupid Warrens. Just nothing more than a bunch of wannabe communists playing house. Pfft."
Laurence shrugs, "I don't disagree. We should've destroyed those places, right?"
Otto states, "Not a half bad idea, all things considered. But the Warrens have become so entrenched in the minds and culture of our Clan that destroying them outright would mean more problems than solutions. And there's already enough trouble going on as is."
Laurence murmurs, "The Second Inquisition..."
Otto clicks his tongue, "Gah! Those fanatics aren't shit. Neither is the Sabbat. And the Anarchs are jokers, but they're our clowns if we play our cards right."
"And the others?"
"What others? They barely register as blips on the radar. Now, can we please stop getting sidetracked and focus on the topic at hand, huh?"
Laurence counters, "What IS the topic at hand?"
"I'm getting to it. Sheesh. I know that you're only a decade into this, but you've still got a long, long way to go, Laurence. Let me speak. So, as I was saying: I'm getting to it."
Mr. Lancaster sighs heavily and silently gestures for his Primogen to continue.
Otto sets his empty glass aside and obliges, "You see, we're both men of extraordinary money, aren't we? Right? I mean, you haven't already gone and blown off your hundreds of millions on some useless, stupid shit, have you? Hey, don't look at me like that, I'm just teasing. Hell, I could blow off half my riches, and I'd still be rich enough to last another few decades."
Laurence nods along, "So could I. Is there a point to all this or just another bored attempt at measuring dicks?"
"Hahahahahaha! Ever the humorous one. And yes, there is a point. You see, it's not a secret that I have been making certain...investments lately. I'm not revealing any more than that- and don't you dare try to figure it out. Find your own niche and exploit it till it's drained dry. That's how we get ahead, right?"
"Uh-huh. Even when I was a mortal, I've never been squeamish about doing what it takes to not only be successful but to also stay that way for the longest possible amount of time. You're not that different."
The Nossie Primogen confirms, "I'm not that different. Anyways, our money can...buy us a hell lotta things, can't it? Women, cars, property, shares in the next big thing, watches, clothes, suits, cravats, scarves, vacations, and the like."
"Right. And?"
"But our money can also...nullify our deficiencies. And what is our most prominent one to date, my friend? Now, before you open your trap and answer me, I want you to think really long and hard about the answer. What can our money do more than paper over?"
Laurence squints and takes time to come up with a satisfactory answer, "Our ugliness. But that isn't the question. It's about finding the right people to help put us together differently. We're Kindred- we can't just walk into some fancy plastic surgeon's office and demand a full facial reconstruction."
Otto chuckles, "You're right, absolutely right, my friend. We can't just waltz in. But that's beside the point which I'm trying to make. Money...can buy the beauty which we lack. It can buy us a hell lot more than that, too, but we're getting off track. It's not about buying what we want but about buying what we need."
"We've managed so far. Hell, you're almost reaching the Ancilla mark. And you got by just fine without having any procedures done. Or--or have you?"
"No, I have not. But it's possible. Just look at...her. Look how she got away from us. You know who I'm talking about, right? She thinks that she's all high and mighty. Bitch wants nothing to do with her own Blood, then that's her problem. Don't come crawling to me when the SI is breaking in the door and asking for your head, because I'm not gonna do a single fucking thing."
Laurence puts in, "And I ain't lifting a finger for her. She really thinks that she's better than us. But she's really beautiful. Hard to actually believe- at times- that she is one of us, isn't it? Well, she is. And if there's anything that's worth all the time, effort, and money, it's that."
Otto snorts, "Mm-hmm. Funny thing is...I've recently come across the contact information of some very talented Tzimisce chirurgeons. Top of their field and they have a specialty in Nossie beautification. I don't know if they're the same people who sewed that bitch up, nor do I care. But if they can fix my face and body, they can certainly do the same for you."
Laurence clasps his hands together and asks the most obvious question, "How much is it?"
"Tch. It's not the money that you should be worried about- both of us could easily afford it ten times over. No, it's what they ask of you in return that's the concern."
"And what's that?"
Otto raises his chin, "Secrets. Those fucking half-aliens want what we've amassed over the long nights. I have...a decent chunk to trade. But even that's not enough for them."
Laurence nods, "And then there's the fact that we'd be telling who-knows-what to a bunch of Tzimisce. Have you even vetted these people? Background checks--"
"I am far from a goddamn amateur at this. All I know is that they're beyond Sects- they have no interest in the bickering. They're not Inconnu, Autarkis, Unbound, or Independent, either. And believe me, I checked. They're just a bunch of freelancers offering their...Flesh-Shaping services to certain Kindred- needy or otherwise."
The Nossie Whip remarks, "Sounds shady. Can't believe I'm saying this, but...do you know how I can get in touch with them?"
Otto snickers, "Not even two decades into this and you've already grown tired of your mug, huh? Hey, I don't blame you. What do I get out of this? What, did you really think that I'd just hand such delicate information over for free? That's just plain insulting."
Laurence sighs deeply, "What do you want in return? My--my Childe says he's got something, but it's either shit that you already know or something that doesn't mean a thing. Hell, for all I know, it could be redundant and you know it already!"
Otto smiles widely, "I could! And how is that Childe of yours, anyway?"
"Fine. We don't talk much- a part of him resents me for Embracing him into this...mess. But he's fine. He has self-preservation instincts. We just aren't on the same page about certain things."
Otto shifts his weight around, "Yeah, reminds me of some of my Childer- the ones who made it past a week, anyway. Fuck all of them, honestly. I only keep tabs on them out of...spite, I guess. That's usually the dynamic between Sire and Childe in our Clan- spite, resentment, and the like. It's just how it is."
Laurence mumbles, "And fear, too. Now, what do want in return for the information I need?"
Otto leans forward, "I want access to your Childe's secrets. If not yours, then his will have to do."
"Why? As it stands, you already know too much."
"You're right. I do. But it's not enough. It's NEVER enough! Sure, I have secrets in all of my goddamn pockets- not just the back ones, heh. Hell, I have this state's fucking District Attorney by the balls."
Laurence, who is visibly impressed by the comment, asks, "And how did you manage to pull that off?"
Otto plainly answers, "I used that Asian PI chick- she's got a knack for uncovering affairs. Great Yelp reviews, too. Once I had the information on my lap, the rest was as easy as apple pie. Now, if your Childe has some other juicy info on the side, then you should be more than happy to give me his."
Laurence shakes his head, "I don't know if he'd willingly give it to me, Otto. We aren't on speaking terms. And he's been avoiding me lately, hanging around with those new Banu Haqim--"
Otto corrects him, "Bnei HaShem. Let's have a little respect for our neighbors, huh? But, yeah, you're right- that's something I've heard, too. Hey, he may be your Childe, but he's gotta take the training wheels off and figure out his own way forward. I mean, it's been what, a few years since you Embraced him?"
"Something like that. And if I were to get you something good..."
"Yes, yes, I'll put you in-touch with those Stokers and you can work out some kind of a plan to look all...regular again. But I'm still trying to figure out which of my secretive secrets to part with. I mean, a few of them are really juicy! I even have fucking pictures of how juicy they are! And I'm talking top-fucking-quality ones. High-def."
The other Nosferatu nods, "OK..."
The Nossie Primogen proudly states, "You know, a good chunk of my repository of secrets wouldn't have been possible without that Private Investigator. She's quite good at following people around with her DSLR camera, hiding in bushes, and taking pictures of them at their most vulnerable and weakest moment. Or their most pathetic ones, hahaha!"
Laurence lowers his head, "I know who you're talking about. But she mostly does affairs, doesn't she?"
"Yep. But that alone is enough to have some really powerful and influential people wrapped around your leprous pinky finger, heh."
"Is she a threat to the Masquerade?"
"Nah. Lena...Adams is good at what she does. She's our asset and she doesn't even know it. I mean, there are a lot of people who unwittingly do our bidding in this city. It's better that way. That distance gives us ironclad deniability and all of that jazz. Plus, we have the Council of Nine Eyes on our side."
Laurence isn't so sure, "Are we sure? Because I do feel that Cedric, Rosalind, and Marshall are still a bit iffy about us. Not like it matters, either way, but--hey, if we had someone deeper in the Council, maybe we can set them up to be a mole or something for us?"
Otto remarks, "Ballsy. Risky. But I kinda like where this is going. OK, OK, you've got me captivated. Any candidates? We need someone malleable. But we also need someone who isn't too young- so Fledglings are off the table. Anyone else?"
"I'm thinking along the lines of the Jew. Amos Greenberg. We're already pretty tight with him."
"Oh, him. Yeah, I...don't know. After he and Dr. George Fateh had their heated argument, I'm not so sure about the guy anymore. He's got a chip on his shoulder, and I don't need another potential loose cannon running around, screwing things up in this Domain for the rest of us."
Otto leans closer and intently stares at his guest, "You do know the kind of trouble that loose cannons tend to invite, right? Now, are we good? I've given you a lot to chew on. Or so I hope."
The Whip offers, "What about that Toreador? She seems like a perfect fit for this kinda thing. She isn't exactly tight with the Salubri, but she knows how they operate. I'd say that's the only plus side about her."
Otto groans, "Oh, great. Her. And yet...that doesn't seem like half a bad idea. Then again, why knock it? I mean, Cedric, Rosalind, and Marshall have been good to our Clan. Do we really wanna risk that by putting a mole among them?"
Laurence replies, "I'm not saying we should go all out on that, but I'd say it's something to consider for the future- there's no telling how long that wide berth is gonna last for. And if we gotta use that Toreador to our advantage, then so be it."
Otto shrugs, "Eh, I'll think about it. But I'd sooner walk out into the sun with a cactus rammed up my ass than get that ditzy Toreador Elder wrapped up in all our schemes and shit. I know her type. She'll try to play it nice and pretend that she's not a bloodsucker, but she's a fucking shark, Laurence."
"Is she, though? Really? She seems kinda sweet."
"You do not know her as well as I do. Trust me when I say that she has waaaaay more tricks up her sleeve than either of us could ever imagine. Now, go on. Get outta here. My duties as a sommelier summon me. I got a few late-night guests to meet and entertain."
Laurence chuckles and queries, "The breathing variety?"
"The breathing AND moneyed variety. They wanted a tour of the vineyard, maybe some grape-stomping under the moon, and some good conversation."
"And do you deliver?"
Otto merely shrugs and maintains something resembling a smug look as way of a reply.
Laurence stands up with a grunt and hobbles towards the exit. He stops in his tracks and asks, "If I were to get you something good, how do we decide on a fair price?"
"Fair is subjective. Always has been with the Clan of the Hidden. Swing by sometimes and I'll give you a tour of the vineyard. And maybe I'll look like a really pretty lady while doing it. Who knows? Heh-heh."
The vineyard behind Otto's Haven is expansive, sprawling, and has an assortment of grapevines lining its many walls and paths. Exploited laborers from deprived nations are brought in and forced to work their asses off, growing grapes, tending to the land, and even being coerced into giving up a few pints of their blood as part of their employment contract.
They do not have much say in the matter. If anyone raises objections, Otto's crooked fangs and chipped claws make quick (albeit very messy) work of silencing them.
Permanently.
And then he has his vast network take care of erasing the poor victim from existence. And they do a disturbingly thorough job of removing all traces of the unfortunate person- both online and offline.
The grounds are kept immaculate. Even at this ungodly hour of the night, workers (borderline slaves) are seen picking grapes from the vines, filling baskets with ripe fruits, and carting the said produce over to the winery's silo.
But Otto and his people always make sure to "clean up" and present some sort of legitimate (totally non-coercive/non-exploitative) business operation when mortals (or Kindred with something resembling a conscience) show up.
After all, a "good reputation" is priceless, especially in the pretentious wine business! And Otto's rep, officially, is sparkling like a diamond.
All part and parcel of the way things are. The Nosferatu Primogen hardly came up with these twisted ideas himself; he's just fulfilling the only logical endpoint of a capitalist system in protracted decline- maximizing profit no matter what.
"Money talks," Otto tells himself while watching Laurence's car roll out of the driveway, its beaming headlights casting a faint glow onto the ground.
In time, the two Nosferatu will find a way to strike a deal. Or they might end up in a deadlock. But both of them hope to get what they want, which isn't necessarily a handsome face.
It's secrets. Always has been. Their Compulsion of Cryptophilia is as strong as their Hunger for blood- it consumes them. And they don't quite like sharing the hush-hush whispers and what have you.
Otto, for his part, has unearthed a ton of secrets in his relatively long Unlife. Some of that, of course, is best left buried. And Laurence Lancaster's secrets are just as intriguing, if not more so. As are the secrets possessed by Mr. Lancaster's Childe.
And how much of it is Lena unwittingly tangled up in?
Speaking of...
The gumshoe is fast asleep. She's got a date tomorrow- Elise made herself free and wants to meet for dinner. A great way to cap off a week that has indubitably been tiring, long, and rather stressful.
But hey, a girl's gotta eat. Especially in a city where the prices have slowly been increasing- the wages have utterly failed to keep pace with the rising cost of living.
Chapter Three: Sun's Out, Buns Out!
Well...not literally, of course. That's asking for a public indecency charge to be slapped onto those bare...buns. But there are a few known nudist colonies in this city. The hot sun beams down, and it's a warm day out!
This is slowly shaping up to be a beautiful Sunday morning. The sun casts its golden-orange glow over Magnolia City. The sky above is light blue, dotted here and there with a few puffy clouds. People go about their daily routines in relative contentment, with only the odd honking of horns being heard every now and then.
This is the one day which Lena doesn't have to wake up early. She relaxes on her bed, and her hair is as messy and tangled as ever. She blinks the sleep away from her eyes, rubs the crusty crust out, and groans as she forces herself up into an upright position.
The PI reaches out for her nightstand and snatches her touchscreen phone. She unlocks it and immediately taps on the FemmeConnect icon.
The most recent message from Elise reads: "Goodnight. I'll be seeing you tomorrow. I genuinely do look forward to it. Don't be late, but I'll understand if something unavoidable happens."
Lena bites her lower lip and types back: "And a good morning to you. Still no profile picture? Anyways, you seem like a decent match. Can't wait to see you. Decided on the venue yet?"
After a few minutes, Elise comes online and responds: "Oh, good. I'm glad you decided to meet up with me. There's this nice Italian restaurant near where I currently reside. You're not allergic or anything, right?"
"Nope. Italian sounds great. And what time are we looking at?"
"Seven?"
Lena's smile is replaced with an unenthused pout. That's a lot earlier than she would've preferred to meet up with a potential date: "Sounds a bit earlier than usual, doesn't it?"
Elise replies: "Well, yes. But I have work tomorrow. And I absolutely loathe Mondays, as I'm sure you do. Seven-thirty?"
"Yeah, that sounds great. Send me the address of this place and I'll see you there. Bye."
Elise replies: "Ciao." She follows it up with a few heart emojis.
Lena snickers and puts her phone back on charge. After that, she shuffles out of bed and heads to the bathroom.
After freshening up and doing her morning routine, the PI opens the closet and fishes out her usual outfit- a pair of faded slacks, a random button-down shirt, and a duffel coat to go over the whole ensemble.
Makeup is light and minimal- after all, the fun starts in the evening and Miss Adams intends to look absolutely gorgeous for tonight! For now, she ties her jet-black hair into a topknot ponytail, applies some sunscreen to her skin, and makes herself ready to start the day.
After putting on some red lipstick, she heads to the kitchen to put a small meal together. The coffeemaker hums as Lena chops some fruits and drops them into a bowl. A mug is quickly placed under the spigot, and the delicious, bitter drink is poured into it.
Lena peels a banana and adeptly chops it into pieces before adding it into the bowl with her other fruits- strawberries, kiwis, and blueberries. She makes ANOTHER cup of coffee and gets ready to dig in!
Nom, nom, nom, nom, nom. Slurp, slurp.
Lena puts the dishes away and stretches herself out with a groan, her muscles and joints popping and cracking. It's going to be a long, busy, and eventful day for the PI- and it's not just because of the amount of work that awaits her.
Today, she intends to visit the scene of the crime; the place where Tyler Woodward was run over and left to die. She wants to try and retrace his steps, see how he got there, and how the vehicle in question was able to hit him and speed off without anybody seeing a single thing.
It is slowly shaping up to be a puzzling case. She calls the father of the deceased child and asks him to meet her at her office so they can formally draw up a contract, finalize the details, and then be on their way.
It'll be a long and sunny day.
Many ships dock at the city's numerous ports, their hulls gleaming in the sun's golden light. Cargo is either loaded or unloaded, workers sweat and haul, and security guards patrol the vast warehouses, giving a threatening stare to any strangers that attempt to approach the premises.
A while later...
Drawing up the contract, going through the formalities, and ensuring that both parties are in agreement was easy enough. Jake, for his part, brought a friend of the family along to ensure that this would be a fair and legal exchange.
In time, Lena Adams finds herself standing near a sidewalk. The area around her is quite deserted- most of the shops and businesses look like they've been boarded up for years on end. The few homeless tents nearby look recent and are devoid of any and all human activity.
But there are a few possessions strewn about. An unhoused person snores loudly while a cardboard cutout next to them reads: "WILL WORK 4 FOOD. 2 jobs not nearly enough. 3rd one would be 'preciated."

Gary strokes his thick mustache and evenly tells her, "Mm-hmm. Let's not repeat that again, hmm? Believe it or not, but you're a very valued employee here at Pluto Enterprises."
Lena scoffs under her breath, "Sure feels like it. Actually, I wanted to talk with you about something."
Gary leans against a wall and placidly enquires, "And what would that be, Lena?"
"Just something that's been on my mind, that's all."
"I'll see when my schedule is clear. If it is, I'll slot you in. Most I can spare is seven to ten minutes."
Lena's eyebrows go up, "Wow. Sure, that's more than enough time. Thanks. Now, if you don't mind."
Gary fervently nods and wheedles outta her sight, "Oh, oh, of course. Don't wanna overstay my welcome. Haha!"
(He's not bad people, but it's clear that he unquestioningly carries out the orders of his corporate betters- just a few weeks ago, he had to let a few of the long-term PE talent go because "streamlining" and "efficiency" and a whole buncha other corporate buzzwords.)
Ironically enough, PE reported a rather favorable quarter in its earnings forecast the very next day. But that's neither here nor there, surely!
Lena sips on a small cup of coffee. While doing so, her brows knit together in a frown. A bunch of random documents have found themselves in her work email account- she surmises that one of the newbies must've misfiled them or some such.
"What the hell??" Lena guzzles down the rest of the coffee and sets the paper cup aside. She sits up straighter and starts the mind-numbingly monotonous task of trying to figure out who sent these over.
However, her facial expression shifts when her amber eyes skim through the documents. Her pulse begins to race, and her heart rate starts to accelerate.
Lena reads out what she's seeing, "'On The Beach'. 'Incident Learning Process'. 'OBEs'. 'PLEs'. Gauze? Gauze?! The fuck is all of this?? What am I--I even reading here?"
Lena takes a closer look at the sender of the files. Unknown sender. Now, this just got hella suspicious (and intriguing). And hella scary, to boot. She cranes her neck and sees her boss in a very important meeting of some kind.
Company guidelines clearly stipulate that when such suspicious events occur, the whole thing should be reported straight to Mr. Stuttgart himself. Not IT, not the head of IT, not to anyone else, not to the other department heads.
Straight to Mr. Stuttgart. But he's in a meeting. He's sitting back in his fancy leather chair, and his fingertips are steepled together, his head is bowed, and his brow is furrowed. Looks like an intense budget planning session.
Or a great timewaster. Dime a dozen of those types of meetings in such corporate environs. Lena herself has been subject to quite a few.
She grunts and shakes her head, trying her hardest not to think about it. She eventually comes to the conclusion that this is just some type of innocuous error. Could it be a benign prank? A dumb practical joke designed to...what, exactly?
Lena goes over whatever came her way again. She can make neither head nor tails of it. For the time being, she figures that the best thing she can do is leave it all be.
The more she reads this stuff, the more her face scrunches into a confused scowl. Lena eventually decides to stop reading the mysterious documents and focus instead on the immediate tasks at hand- the tasks that her salary is dependent upon completing.
The real-world activities she needs to do, not the hokey pokey, mysterious shit. But...but a small part cannot help but wonder just what all of this entails.
While lingering near the watercooler, Lena mutters to herself, "'Spectres'. Since when did this company have anything to do with...Spectres? And what the hell is--"
She is snapped out of her hazy thoughts by an unexpected hand clapping her shoulder.
Gary. He has an inscrutable look etched onto his otherwise immaculately groomed mug. Armed security guards flank his sides. Lena looks at them, then down at her attire.
Gary lowers his voice, "Let's not make a scene in the middle of the day, Lena. Just come with me nice and calm-like, OK? I'm sure that there's a logical explanation for--for all of this."
The guards lift their chins and intensely stare at the PI, who wobbles on the spot. Her eyebrows go up, and her eyes widen. The others stop what they're in the middle of doing and keenly observe the proceedings.
"Gary?! W-what the--what are you talking about??"
"Please, Lena. Don't make this any harder than it's already getting. Come with us and we'll get it all sorted out, OK?"
Lena gulps and accedes to his request, "OK. OK. Look, I dunno what's going on, but..."
Gary ushers her into his office and locks the door. The armed guards stand outside, their chests are puffed out, and their grim expressions unchanging.
The bossman presses a button on a remote and the frosted glass turns fully opaque. Oh, shit's gettin' real!
Lena looks around her boss's office- not her first time in here. He gets his own aircon, a mahogany desk, the biggest ergonomic chair that she has ever seen, a freaking Apple iMac, shit-tons of awards for everything from mentorship to leadership, and a buncha other knickknacks.
(And that's not even including the lucrative stock options and bonuses that Gary is entitled to! And he counts as middle class!)
Meanwhile, people like Lena are practically living hand-to-mouth.
Gary sits down with a heavy sigh, "It has recently come to my attention that you have been the recipient of some...confidential information. Some sensitive documents. Very, very sensitive."
Lena licks her lips and nervously nods, "I swear that--that I had absolutely nothing to do--"
Gary lifts a stern finger and continues, "Please, don't speak until I finish saying what I have to say. After I'm done, you can say your piece."
She lowers her head and glumly stares at the floor.
The bossman brings his fingers together in a steeple, rests his elbows on his desk, and regards her with a measured look, "Miss Adams. Lena. You had no business seeing those documents...but you did. Those weren't meant for you. You should've deleted them the moment you saw the header."
Lena throws her hands up and complains, "I was going to, but I--"
Gary tuts, "Ah, ah, ah. Let me finish. Please. I've already ordered IT to perform a full, remote wipe of your computer. I'm sorry, but we at Pluto Enterprises take safety and confidentiality of our data very, very seriously."
Lena stiffens. The words get caught in her throat.
When her boss senses her silent acquiescence, he goes on, "All you had to do was come to me, Lena. Instead, you went ahead and, for whatever reason, decided to open the email and peruse the contents. Why? To put it plainly, what you did was extremely irresponsible."
"But--but you were busy, Mr. Stuttgart," Lena softly murmurs.
Gary shakes his head, "That's hardly an excuse. If it's an emergency- which it absolutely was- then you'd have had my permission to break the door down, pull a fire alarm, or scream to get my attention. But you did nothing of the sort."
"I--I didn't know."
"Exactly. You didn't know. Now, here's the hard part--"
Lena gasps, "You're not gonna lay me off for THIS?!"
Gary frowns and visibly bristles, "No. Please, don't be so overdramatic. We're not going to fire you. Don't worry."
Lena breathes out an audible sigh of relief. The sweat glistening on her forehead says it all. She clears her throat and belatedly apologizes, "Oh, s--sorry."
(Lena Akira stays in her job because of the medical and dental insurance coverage that she receives from being employed by Pluto Enterprises. She's had it like that ever since she started interning with them- straight outta college. If she leaves, she loses ALL of it. Simple as.)
Such is the ugly reality of the capitalist system, which is quite akin to a toxic, abusive relationship. And as such, people like Lena have no other choice but to sell their labor power in exchange for a meager paycheck.
Many in this world have no choice because they NEED the dosh to pay the bills and finance their basic needs- food, shelter, clothing, transportation, sleep, stability, etcetera.
It doesn't have to be this way...
Gary shuffles some loose paperwork and plainly states, "It's fine. We have no intention of letting you go. You've been with us...for years. You're practically a member of the Pluto family. Besides, the costs of recruiting and training somebody to replace you would be...well, substantial."
Lena faintly smiles, "OK...I'm sorry for--for whatever I did."
Gary evenly responds, "No problem. Don't do it again. Not that we're expecting something like this to happen ever again. But in this line of work, you just never know. Even if you think you're taking precautions, it can always go wrong. There's a million-and-one ways for things to go sideways."
Lena's face falls, and she lowers her gaze to the floor.
The bossman queries, "What exactly did you see in those documents, anyway? I'm sorry, but I have to ask."
"Just a bunch of stuff that I didn't understand, Gary. I mean, I tried making sense of it, but..."
Gary interjects, "We wouldn't recommend trying to make sense out of that. As far as we're concerned, it's just a bunch of mumbo-jumbo."
Lena asks, "Was it some kind of a prank?"
Gray briefly chuckles, "Maybe." His tone goes from stern to mildly evasive.
Lena clearly picks up on his change of demeanor, too. She tries her luck anyway, "How about an explanation?"
Gary straightens his posture and vehemently tells her, "You don't need any other explanation. You're in the wrong here, not the company. And I don't have to remind you of the clauses in your contract- no discussing these matters with coworkers, family, loved ones, lovers, friends, or basically anyone other than the department heads and directors of this company."
Lena blows a raspberry, "Mm-hmm. I've been here long enough to know how things are done. I used to bring coffee on a cart to a room full of investors, remember those days?"
Gary smiles and nods, "Yep. You were just getting started with us. Your first job straight outta, what was it..."
Lena's smile fades, "Indiana State University."
Gary's smile also vanishes, and he starts to look a tad uncomfortable. He uses his thumb to scratch his mustache, "The years certainly went by. Look at you now. Look at how far you've come. Gone are those wheel carts and in came a bunch of new, important responsibilities. Lots of responsibility."
"Oh, sure. Yay me. Since I have your attention now..."
Gary leans closer and stares at her intently, "Yes?"
"What the hell is an OBE or a PLE? What 'Beach' are we On?"
"I'm not gonna dignify that with an answer...or any answer, for that matter. Don't ever bring it up again- that's my last warning to you, Lena. Certain...things are out there, but we'd strongly encourage you not to think about them. Mankind wasn't meant to know everything."
Lena is in the habit of not taking orders too well, even when she doesn't have a whole lot of choice in the matter. So, instead of meekly accepting her boss's caveat, she presses on, "Honestly, it didn't sound like gibberish to me. Something's going on here and as an employee of PE, I have a right to know what's really--"
Gary explodes, "Enough!! Not another word of this!"
He calms down, "Please, Lena. You have no idea what--what--never mind."
Lena narrows an eye, "Since I still have your attention, why don't we talk about a raise, huh? Hell, you yourself said that I'm family, right? And if I know anything about family, it's that they have each other's backs, don't they?"
Gary sucks some air in through his teeth. He clears his throat, looks to the side, and ponders something.
Lena doesn't take her amber eyes off of her boss. He feels the pressure of her gaze and writhes with discomfort.
The bossman concedes, "An increase in salary is something that--that I...uh...can't personally authorize. All final decisions are at the behest of--of the CEO, who reports directly to the Board. If you really wanna push for a bump in pay, I can refer the matter over to HR."
Lena dryly tells him, "You better get started on it, then. I've worked here for a significant portion of my adult life, Gary, and I've gotten, like, coupons and shitty-ass gifts and cards. Nothing substantial. And don't even get me started on last year's crappy mugs and t-shirts."
"Alright, alright. I'll see what I can do. But you better not use this as an attempt to blackmail or--or strong-arm us. This isn't how things should be done, and we're not going to reward bad behavior."
Lena frowns, "What bad behavior? It's not my damn fault that a bunch of word salad documents found their way into my inbox, is it?"
Gary lamely concedes, "I suppose that you're correct; it isn't entirely your fault, yes. But that doesn't necessarily give you the right to get yourself involved in stuff that's none of your concern. And it sure as hell doesn't give you a right to extort or threaten your employer."
Lena scoffs, "Jesus. I'm not threatening anybody. All I'm asking for is a raise- it's reasonable! I mean, have you seen the rising prices? As it is, way more than half of my paycheck goes straight into paying the rent. And the rent's already gone up, too! Which means that I'm gonna have to shell out more and more!"
"I am truly sorry to hear that. We do offer courses on financial management and fiscal responsibility. You--"
"Fuck that shit. I want a raise. Not a lecture, not platitudes, not one more gift card. Not an umpteenth coupon. Money. Raise. And if it helps you sleep better at night, then I've already forgotten about...about all of that strange stuff."
Gary sighs, "I'll try getting that raise thing done. Most I can do is put in a--a good word for you and let my own bosses decide if you do merit a raise or not. Don't get your hopes up, though. Times are tough all around."
Lena groans, "Oh, spare me the sob story. I'm not stupid, this company is raking it in like nobody's fucking business."
Gary merely answers, "Mm-hmm. I suppose that may be the case. Now, if you don't mind. You and I both have work to do. The day's far from over." He makes something resembling a shooing gesture at her.
Lena picks herself up, bids a curt farewell, and heads back to her chair. She plops down on it and is greeted by a string of notifications popping up in her email account.
Her work computer has already been remotely wiped. An extreme measure, all things considered. It takes her a few minutes to get everything set up again. She has to manually change some settings, re-check for Windows Updates, and once the whole thing is back in shape, her work resumes.
Lena Adams is left with even more questions than she started out with.
The answers might (or might not) make themselves known to her in due time.
During lunchtime, Lena munches on a chunky burrito and thinks about matters NOT related to Pluto Enterprises. Namely, her cases as a Private Investigator for the public good.
Oh, yeah. She's got cases (plural) to tackle. Tyler's murder isn't the only thing on her plate, and an investigation does not develop linearly. She's got a few potential cheating spouses to tail.
As for Tyler's case, she figures that she'll have to broaden her search a bit. There were no cameras in the immediate vicinity where Tyler was killed. But somebody farther away must've seen the car which ran him over.
Michael (her mentor) would often tell her, "When there's nothing in the immediate radius of the crime scene, you gotta expand your horizons. If there's nothing within two miles of the corpse, you better take a longer look- five miles away. And if you can't see anything within a hundred miles or so, then the only thing you can do is to ask the spirits if they saw anything."
She had no idea what that last part meant. Most likely an attempt at a joke, if anything.
But anyway, Lena reckons that she'll have to expand her search. There has to be a cam (whether from some dingy bodega, an ATM, a pawn shop, etc.) that should've seen the car speeding away. If she's lucky, then she'll be able to spot a dent, traces of blood, and most importantly...a license plate.
Lena tosses the burrito wrapper into a nearby trash can and languidly leans back in her chair. While the food digests, she shuts her eyes and briefly recalls last night's sensuous activities. A sultry smile adorns her face.
Remembering the passionate session makes her inner thighs twitchy.
Afternoon soon gives way to evening. Lena is now in her PI office. The sun begins its grandiose descent beneath the horizon. She goes over the files pertaining to Tyler, trying to see if there's something that she's still not seeing.
Maybe a clue or a crumb of some kind?
Elsewhere in this Domain...
Francesca Livroni of Clan Hecata stirs from her day-sleep. Flashes of traumatic past memories involving fire, shouting, and strong words invade her mind and cause her to violently shudder. Her skin crawls.
She pops awake. No breath emerges from her nostrils or mouth. Not that it was ever going to. And it hasn't for a long, long time.
The Whip to the Hecata Primogen resides in a simple duplex house in a quiet neighborhood where the houses are evenly spaced out. About two blocks from her own dwelling is a small grocery store selling everything under the sun.
Not that a vampire like her needs the edibles, but they have good deals on other stuff- cleaning solutions, lightbulbs, toothpaste, gardening tools, and the list goes on and on.
The soft brown carpeting runs up against her cold and bare feet when she slips out of her double bed. Francesca flings the curtains wide open and watches the moon behind the cottony clouds. It's an exceptionally bright, starry night.
The Necromancer has a long night ahead of her. But, like any other self-respecting Corpse with a shred of decency, she heads straight to the bathroom. Vampires might not have need for the toilet, owing to how changed their physiologies become after the Embrace, but there are other reasons why one would visit a restroom.
Francesca stares at her reflection in the mirror. The tiled walls look pristine. She analyzes her features- the deathly pale skin, the lifeless hazel eyes, the remnants of dark circles underneath them becoming quite visible again, the pinhole-sized pupils, and the messy blonde hair that's all over the place.
She shakes her head and gets ready to bathe. While she brushes her teeth, she enters her walk-in closet and swiftly selects an outfit. After a vigorous teeth-brushing, she spits the white toothpaste out and mulls over the possibilities.
Fran thinks about the past- a lot. She remembers how she and many of her fellow Graverobbers were brought over here to Magnolia City, USA all the way from Italy. This was around the time that Benito Amilcare Andrea Mussolini had seized power.
The Italian Hecata- the ones who hadn't gleefully aligned themselves with the fascist scum, anyway- saw the proverbial writing on the wall and decided that it was high time for them to relocate. Or, at the very least, escape to a safer place.
(Not an easy thing to do when one is a cold, walking corpse incapable of being out in the sun for even a second!)
He...brought them here to Magnolia City. But not necessarily out of the magnanimity of his shriveled heart.
While under the shower, Francesca shudders. Not just because of the water. Rather, the reason for her shudders are thoughts of...him. Her Clan's Elder, the man who had used his own immense wealth, power, and connections to bring a shipload of his fellow Hecata to safer shores.
Francesca holds her naked body tightly, a few tears of blood dropping down the side of her cheeks. They eventually intermingle with the shower water and get washed away. The many (mixed) thoughts swish, swirl, and ferment inside her mind.
His words were stern...as were the many lessons that he had taught her. Francesca looks at her left hand. Burn marks and other severe injuries from the past mar her dead flesh. It's so grotesque that the very sight of it makes her sick- even while knowing full that she can't really "get sick" anymore.
Francesca's left hand is still usable- all her fingers are present and twirl as intended- but the burns make her skin look as Repulsive as that of a Nosferatu's. The blemishes, cracks, and scars are a never-ending reminder of the importance of certain things.
Family being the most important of them all. At least for the Hecata Clan.
Francesca wraps a towel around her body and heads out. She wipes her face and lets the memory play through her mind. As ghastly and traumatic as it is, the eternal nature of her existence demands that she keeps an ironclad focus on her duties...to the Family above all else.
The Family-Stiff (Nickname for the Hecata Clan) rubs her left hand with her right one. She winces each time she feels the roughness and deformities of her scarred flesh. When the memories begin to subside a bit, she snaps back to reality and reminds herself that...he is no longer here.
(The hand never healed after what was done. He made sure of it.)
As a matter of fact, he has not been seen for decades on end. And yet, not a night goes by when the other Hecata (especially her Primogen) are not reminded of their Elder's many...lessons. Lessons in the importance of sticking together and not rocking the boat.
Lessons in the necessity of Family, of the Blood, and of the world beyond. A reminder, too. A reminder of the Promises.
Particularly, the Promise of 1528. And he always knew about the five-hundred-year time limit. Granted, 2028 is still a long way off, but he was already making certain preparations. Even back in the last century!
These nights, most Hecata (Francesca included) sorta wait around and go about their Unlives as usual. When 2028 comes, then they'll see. They will hopefully know what to do when that night emerges from the shadows. Until then, one step at a time.
Francesca puts on a silky red shirt with the topmost buttons undone- this reveals a small bit of her cleavage- Ooh La La! She wears an above-knee-length gray skirt and a pair of heels. For accessories, she slips her leather glove onto her left hand (reasons), puts on some hoop earrings, and wraps some shiny bracelets around her wrists.
As for makeup, she settles in for some delightfully seductive and dark eye shadow, a touch of mauve lipstick, and some rouge. She applies some concealer to, well, conceal the deathly paleness of her skin- can't be too careful these nights!
She grabs her (recently acquired) Gucci purse and dons a pair of sunglasses (for the cool factor and nothing else). Her blonde, bobbed hair is a bit all over the place, but she's managed to dry and style it into something that looks decent enough for tonight's occasions.
Francesca heads downstairs and enters her study. She leaves the door open and sets herself behind the ornate, cherry mahogany desk. She pulls out a stack of letters and decides to go through them again. As of late, she's had this pang to go over her old missives.
She cracks a tiny smile at some of the steamy (at least by last century's standards) love letters. While she goes through the plethora of correspondence, the doorbell rings.
Food's here!
Her dead eyes flit to another letter, this one anything but flowery. The name on it has been partially scratched out- even after all these decades, the vicious claw marks are still discernible. She couldn't have forgotten about him even if she wanted to.
"Giordano," she says with a slight growl. She doesn't get any angrier than she already is, but hearing the name itself is enough to make her feel a stinging pain. And not just in her left hand.
The memories of his hideousness are somewhat fresh. And yet, he brought them all over here and (perhaps) saved their Unlives from the fascists.
Even now, his words echo and reverberate throughout her mind, 'I could've left you for Il Duce, but I brought you here. My Blood. My Clan. Never forget, Francesca. Family above all else. Even the Sects. Even the Eternal Struggle."
Francesca shudders and gets up to get the door. The second ring of her bell sounded more impatient than the first. This time, a man with a mohawk and tons of piercings stands in front of her.
He looks to be in his twenties. His tattooed hands are clasped together, and an expectant look is written on his face. He looks like the kinda guy who likes...Pain.
Francesca sighs and lets him inside her Haven. He knows what this arrangement entails. Hell, she surmises that this guy probably LIVES for the kind of Pain that she's about to inflict on him.
He didn't bring anything with him. He IS the food for the nocturnal predator of the dark nights.
The mortal looks around her Haven, "Sweet digs. Lotta Italians on this block, heh. Lemme guess, you're way older than a lot of the nonnas on this block?"
Francesca side-eyes him, "What is it to you?"
Mohawk man shrugs, "Hey, it's nothing to me. I'm curious, is all. And it wouldn't be my first time getting...bit by one of you people. Does it always hurt when Italian vampires bite into people or am I just some rare breed of masochist or something?"
Francesca ushers him to the living room. Her bulky touchscreen phone is charging, there's no TV present, and a few couches are scattered about. She sits down and has the mortal sit across from her.
He taps the side of his neck with two fingers, "Well, go on. Do your thing. Unlike others, I actually live for this sorta shit. Not the pain, mind you. But the euphoric rush, the explosion of ecstatic sensation and overwhelming pleasure that I feel when you..."
Francesca holds her cold hand out, "You're not the first person I've fed on who has an...inclination towards this sort of activity. Normally, I would have a much better selection, but times are tough, and--"
The mortal interrupts her, "Yeah, yeah, yeah. And word on the street is that you usually cook a mean spaghetti and meatball meal afterward, so I came in prepared."
A wee while later...
Mohawk man barely winces. The Kiss was Painful as all heck, but he's developed a sort of tolerance towards it- or rather, he enjoys it. Francesca returns with a plate of hot food.
He's got a hungry look in his eyes. He gleefully accepts her offering and digs in. Within minutes, he wolfs it all down and slurps the sauce noisily, "Ah, not the best I ever had, but it's definitely up there."
Francesca plainly nods, feeling her own Hunger being sated, "Anything else before you leave? And remember, do not tell anybody about this...arrangement."
"Yes, nonna. Do you wanna tell me to avoid strangers in big white vans, too?"
Fran shakes her head, "How many of you are there?"
He grimaces at the query, "Huh?! The fuck's that supposed to mean?"
"Your kind. The kind who, for some reason, gain pleasure from such...Painful bites. Is it a...fetish of some kind? Can you not feel any Pain?"
"Wow, you are super old, hahaha! What's wrong with having a fetish? And I sure as hell could FEEL your bite, lady. Hey, don't get me wrong, I enjoyed it as--as much as you did."
"Hmm. Very well. Please, be careful."
Mohawk man feels his head spinning. When he attempts to stand up, he falls back and catches himself. His eyes have an unfocused glossiness to them, "Whoa. Shit, I...feel woozy. Mind if I lounge here for a few?"
Francesca shrugs, "By all means. It is the...least that I can do for you."
He asks her, "You got any video games around here? You do know what those are, right? And uh...where's your TV, lady? No, seriously, I've been to some of your friend's digs and they're IN the current century. But you? Oh, shit. Something's--"
The mortal clutches the side of his head, "Grrr. Woozy. Wow. Y--you didn't accidentally take too much, right?"
Francesca equably states, "I took only what was required. I can make you a...drink if you would like?"
"What, like, a post-getting-bit-by-Italian-vampires hangover cure? Is it green, white, red, and gloopy?"
Francesca chuckles, "I was thinking something along the lines of warm water."
"Warm? H--Honey, a vamp's definition of warmth is not the same as mine. Seriously, you were colder than the Arctic Circle when you were touching me! But I gotta ask..."
Fran sits back down, "Hmm. Ask."
"What's with the Italian accent? Are you new to this country or something? Wait, how do vampires do the whole immigration thing?"
"I have been here for decades. More need not be said because I do not know you that well. And...there are certain rules that our kind must uphold."
The bitten mortal rubs his neck and snarks, "Yeah, like...don't talk to the food. Oh, wait! 'Thou shalt not talkest to thine food'."
This elicits a soft chuckle from the immortal lady, "Hmm. Funny. Wait here for a few minutes. Once you are feeling better, you may leave. I will clear your plate and bring you some warm water."
"Gotcha!"
Once the food (tm) takes his leave, Francesca shuts the door and places her head against the painted wood. She's had a long Unlife so far. Usually, she goes hunting. But her Clan finds all these sorts of people (scene kids, so-called "freaks", those on the margins of society, etc.) and has some sort of revolving door system going on.
Except the mortals never know who, where, and what will be next in line. All they know is that the bites of these particular vampires hurt. And they really do fuckin' hurt!
Normally, the Kiss is supposed to be this quasi-euphoric thang, but the Clan of Death are somewhat incapable of providing a more ecstatic or pleasing experience.
('Tis what it is!)
Miss Livroni grabs her Gucci bag, locks the front door, and saunters towards the bus stop. She feels a little bad about feeding off the mortal, but eh. It's either such arrangements or the worse(er) alternatives that her Clan has resorted to in the past.
The first thing on her agenda is to swing by the Haven of her Primogen- Vincenzo. He lives in a high-rise apartment in the inner city, and even at night, the sprawling urban jungle is a colorful and vibrant sight.
Vincenzo's second floor apartment is a tad cramped. The lights cast a sickly yellow hue, and the humidity is pretty high. But he's situated here because the energies of the dead are higher in this part of the Domain.
And since Vincenzo is a Graverobber, this is definitely the most appropriate place for him to be. Even his duties as Primogen come second to his unerring devotion to...Family. And not just any lowercase-f family.
But The Family. The ones not bound by the usual mortal ties.
Vincenzo is tall and disturbingly thin. He seems to be in his fifties and has gaunt and hollow features. His pallid skin is riddled with acne and mottled with discolorations, and his oily hair is slick, short, and black.
His teeth are yellow and slightly crooked. His Italian accent is as thick as Francesca's.
He greets her with outstretched arms, "Francesca, cara (ITA: dear)! Come, come!"
The Whip returns his hug and politely kisses him on the cheeks. He returns the gesture, as is the custom.
Vincenzo ushers her inside his dimly lit Haven and offers her a seat. To call it messy would be an insult to messy. Scattered tomes, ancient looking documents, and crumbling old newspapers are strewn all over the place, and the amount of dust in here is amazing.
A few spiders have made their homes here, too. As have other creepy-crawlies.
Even the Primogen's outfit looks raggedy- an old shirt overlaid with a sleeveless sweater vest, faded slacks, and some flimsy-looking shoes.
Francesca sits on the cleanest possible seat that she can find and gracefully folds her legs, "How are you tonight?"
Vincenzo joyously answers, "Bene! Bene! (ITA: Good! Good!) And you?"
Fran shrugs, "The mortal you sent my way was...satisfactory."
Vincenzo lips curl up into an unsettling smirk, "That is good! These, eh...how do you call them? Ah, yes. These punks are not all so bad. Their...uh, spirit is strong. Hmm, yes, yes. Their spirit. Hmm. I have communed with many punks...many indeed!"
Francesca smiles softly, "Thank you for your help. I never got the chance to properly thank you the other night."
He swats a fly buzzing near his nose, "Oh, please! Do not mention it! It was only the least I can do for such an esteemed member of...our...Clan. Hm, yes. Clan."
"Not Family?"
Vincenzo winces and involuntarily spasms when that word escapes Francesca's lips.
He rubs the side of his greasy cheek, "Clan. That other word...it has lost all meaning to me on these nights, cara. Giordano...he was not a good man. I should not speak like this, but I am grateful that he...disappeared. In decades, we have not seen him; and maybe that is for--for the best."
Francesca tightly holds her gloved hand and nods along, "Sì. He...was not a good man. But if it were not for him, we would still be in Italy. And the fascists would've found us and--and--and destroyed us."
Vincenzo fixes her with a baleful, pursed-lipped glare, "Hmm, true, true. Do you ever think of the old country?"
"Sometimes. You?"
"Many times, haha! Sì, sì. Giordano, he...saved us. But it does not change what he did to us- all of us. The ways that he went about ensuring that the, hmm...Clan was united and loyal. The methods he took to ensure such loyalty and unity, I--"
He gulps, "I--if only we could've freed ourselves sooner. But he has not been seen in decades and for that, I thank the spirits. Maybe the spirits did it, you know? How do you say it? Disappeared him?"
Francesca mirthlessly chuckles, "I would like to think so, too. But Giordano was a powerful Elder. His command over the spirits was superb and they always obeyed his every will. He would not have gone so easily."
Vincenzo scoffs and flicks a small roach off his shoulder, "Tch. Then in that case, I hope he went...kicking and screaming! I hope that the spirits swarmed him and broke him like the...cazzo that he--he always was."
Francesca forces a few breaths through her lifeless lungs. The smell in here is rancid and stale. She winces and rubs her cold neck with her gloved hand, "Hmm. Let us speak of something else."
Vincenzo shakes his head, "You worry that he will return?"
"Will he?"
"Our Blood is immune to the Beckoning. But it wouldn't be like him to suddenly disappear. And all of his Ghouls were mercilessly slain...I communed with their spirits, and they all say the same thing."
It wouldn't be the first time that Francesca is hearing all this, so she dully repeats, "They all saw a very fast and little man wielding a sword. Hm."
The Hecata Primogen lifts his chin and stares at the cawing raven on his grimy windowsill. Its beady raven eyes stare back at his dead eyes with a certain awareness. An intelligence.
It flaps its wings and caws again.
Vincenzo makes a harsh shooing gesture and the bird flies off, "I thank whoever that small man is. Or was. He did our Clan a service by disposing of that monster. By removing him, he allowed us the opening that we needed."
The Hecata Whip points out, "Aren't we also monsters?"
Vincenzo counters, "Hmph, yes, perhaps. But not as bad as he was. We are monsters only out of necessity. Giordano was a monster because he--because he liked it. He revelled in it, that old fool. But now, all is right with the world. Anyway, cara, what brings you here?"
"I wanted to say hello. And thank you."
"You already did. And how is everything else? Work, home, the...other stuff?"
Francesca flatly reports, "Work is normal. And quite flexible. Grazie (ITA: Thank you) for--for helping me get this job."
Vincenzo clicks his tongue, "Ah, it was nothing, cara. After all those long, long years of you toiling in that restaurant, I was happy to finally lend a hand to you. What else is new?"
Fran shifts around on her seat, "Same old. Same old."
"Have you found a lover yet?"
"No."
"A Ghoul, at least?"
"No."
Vincenzo shakes his head from side to side, "You must at least have a...friend, cara. A companion. Keeping up your Humanity is vital, especially for creatures as...Morbid as us."
Francesca avers, "I don't want a Ghoul. Well, not yet. And as for a lover, well..." She trails off and glances around her fellow Hecata's Haven. Gloomy and messy, but not awful.
Vincenzo follows her gaze and suggests, "Heh, you think my place is...terrible?"
"No. I understand why you have chosen to live in such a space."
Vincenzo clarifies, "Chosen to? It is necessity. It brings me closer to the dead with whom I regularly commune on these nights, cara. You could easily pierce the Shroud yourself if you tried to. And I know that you have."
She chuckles, "Sì. I...have."
"And how was it?"
Francesca shows off her Gucci bag, "She led me to it. She even told me of her many...stashes located all across this Domain- clothes, jewelry, some money. I will give most of it off and keep some to myself."
Vincenzo muses, "Ah, Francesca...always so generous! A good quality to have, yes."
"How long before you have to move again?"
The Primogen clicks his tongue derisively, "A few more months. Even you see it, don't you? The Decay all around...me. This Haven was not like this when I first moved in."
The Whip lowers her head pensively, "I know. I know. Are you managing fine?"
Vincenzo shrugs, "The dead guide me. They are my path- my purpose. I know of some houses that are unoccupied by the waking but are nonetheless full of restless souls. I will continue to commune with them and work out some kind of deal. Bargain, maybe?"
"Hmm. I should go now."
"Yes, yes. Be well, cara. Do what must be done for the good of the Fa--err--Clan. Your Humanity is...precious. Yes, it is the most precious thing that you have."
"Sì. Sì. Grazie. Addio (ITA: Goodbye)."
He remains seated and waves her goodbye. He then turns his attention back to the grimy windowsill. The raven is back and this time, there's some blood on its beak.
It caws at him as if to beckon his attention.
Francesca makes her way down the steps and back out into the street. She pulls out her smartphone and checks her messages. Nothing of note. Her next destination is a simple subway ride away.
Ten minutes later, she's standing outside a park. Her footwear clacks on the cobblestone path. She walks down to the end, turns left, and steps onto some grass. A row of quaint little houses is in front of her. The lights in the windows are off, save for one house.
Francesca's expression softens as she slowly approaches the house. She knocks on the front door and is let in by a muscular young man wearing some kind of nurse's uniform. He ushers her inside, knowing full well who she is and why she's here.
She greets the caretaker politely, "Hello, Jeremy. How is he keeping?"
Jeremy wipes his hands with a towel and replies, "Sal's...keeping fine, Ms. Livroni."
Francesca adjusts her shirt's collar and strides into the bedroom. On the bed is a very, very old man. A few tubes and sensors are attached to his arms. Machines beep and blink in tandem with the man's heartbeat and other vitals.
His face is wrinkly and most of his once-luscious hair has fallen off. His skin is sagging and his body has withered away from old age and overwork. In short, the long years have not been kind to Sal.
Not at all.
Francesca takes the seat next to him and delicately sits down. She gingerly runs her cold right hand on the mortal's forehead. He stirs awake, his eyes slowly opening to reveal two glassy green eyes. They stare unblinking into nothingness.
Fran waves in front of his face to get his attention. Sal sits up, blinks, and takes her hand. He softly whispers, "Fran?? Is that you?" His voice is hoarse and frail. His grip is weak.
"It is me, Sal."
Back then, Francesca used to work as front-of-house staff at none other than the Sorriso di Napoli (Smile of Naples) restaurant. Yep, the very same one that recently got redone, renovated, and renamed to Ristorante Cielo Blu (Cielo Blu Restaurant).
Sal was a waiter back then- and a whole lot younger, too. While Francesca's immortality carried her to this new century, Sal's fragile mortality aged and eroded him. Regardless, he is one of her oldest and dearest friends.
But the dementia that he currently suffers from has turned him into an old shell of his bubbly, witty, and charming self. The past and present all collide and smush together in his mind. A brain that could once retain entire orders from customers under extreme pressure is now reduced to this.
But even now, he's still able to recognize Fran. At least when he's able to. Right now, when he sees her, his face lights up in recognition and happiness.
For her part, Francesca has made sure to maintain contact with him, visiting whenever possible.
Sal props himself up by his elbows with a harsh groan, "Fran? Fran? Is that you?" The disbelief in his tone is unmistakable, as is his awe.
"It is me, Sal. How are you? Keeping fine? Feeling alright?"
Sal grunts, "It pains all--all over, Fran. Wow, you look...amazing. Or are my eyes tricking me?"
Fran chuckles and turns her head aside, "It is the low lighting in here. I have come to check up on you."
"Oh, that? That's--that's nothing, really! Hey, is that place on Elm Street still around? Think you could swing by and grab my...uh...that Jerome guy told me it's gone, but I--I don't trust him."
Francesca gently corrects, "His name is Jeremy, Sal. He is your caretaker- he only has your best interest in mind."
Sal mumbles, "And who are you again?"
Francesca's dead heart gets stung when those words leave Sal's lips. Her unblinking gaze lingers on her old friend and co-worker.
He gulps and repeats himself, "Who are you? Are--are you with the Watchtower? You t--tell those JW fucks that I'm out! OUT! I'm donating my blood to those folks and that's that!"
Francesca calms his down, "No, no. I am...it is me. Do--do you not remember? Me?"
Sal's expression softens as realization slowly dawns on him. The wrinkles in his face deepen, "F--Francesca? Whoa. Shit...hey! We gotta get to work now! C'mon, we better move or we'll miss the bus--" He actually tries to get up out of his bed, but she quickly places her cold hand on his bare shoulder.
She calmly pushes him back down and fixes him with a sympathetic look. He looks up at her with a slack jaw, the words failing to form in his mouth. He shrinks back down into his pillows and gets comfortable.
Francesca plays along, "Tonight is your...holiday, Sal."
"Oh, right. Right. You're still going to work, right?"
"Yes. Of course."
Sal muses, "Being front-of-the-house is a...lot of fun, isn't it? But not nearly as fun as what I do, heh."
"It is very enjoyable, I suppose."
Sal gesticulates and fondly recalls, "You know, last night...I took an order. We had these beatniks come in, real slick types. Didn't even need a notepad. I got it all up in here." He smirks and taps the side of his head with his bony index finger.
Francesca nods, "Yes. And you also took the orders from that African American couple sitting on the far table."
Sal repeats, "'One spaghetti for me and one penne pasta for the lady. Hold the olives.'"
Francesca chuckles, "Indeed--"
The dementia-stricken mortal cuts her off and excitedly says, "Did you know that--that Ladonna Hughes is coming to our joint next week? You know, the...the...singer. Beautiful voice, like an angel. I'm gonna ask for her autograph, Fran. I'm gonna do it."
She knows all too well to whom he is referring. She is also painfully aware of Ladonna's eventual fate- it was not a pretty one, to put it mildly. But of course, Sal will never be privy to the truth.
The two of them spend some time reminiscing about the good ol' nights. Sal bounces from anecdote to half-recalled memory narrated with great glee and enthusiasm. But then the conversation drifts towards other waters- darker, sadder, and less happy memories.
The eighties were hardly what one could call delightful. Austerity, cuts, and layoffs hit the restaurant business (and broader service industry) pretty hard back then. Sal got let go in a massive restructuring effort. Fran left due to the fact that some of her co-workers may have caught on to her apparent lack of aging.
After a few more tender minutes, Francesca bids her Touchstone goodbye, softly kisses him on the forehead, and leaves the room. He falls asleep soon afterwards, the fatigue finally catching up to his old bones and weary mind.
The Hecata vampire hands Jeremy some money and tells him to ensure that Sal lives out his days with some degree of dignity and comfort.
He pockets the money and solemnly nods, "His condition isn't improving. I've worked with dementia patients before, so I'm pretty used to this, but..." He trails off and clears his throat.
Francesca faintly sighs, "I understand. How long do you think he has left?"
"I'm not a doctor, miss. I just take care of the man's daily needs and...other stuff. I'll do my best for him, you have my word."
She gives him a pat on the shoulder, tightly smiles, and takes her leave. While walking away, tears of blood roll down her cheeks. She quickly wipes them off and hurries down the block, not wishing to draw attention to herself.
Since Francesca has time, she decides to swing by her old place of work and see how it has been redone, remodeled, and spruced up for the modern era. She first started working there shortly after arriving to the United States of America.
Giordano had set her up with a fake identity and provided her with enough financial assistance to keep afloat for a good while. He'd also assist in helping her swap out IDs whenever the heat got too high. She's been using different names and aliases for the past few decades, too.
But she will always be Francesca Livroni of the Clan of Death.
It goes without saying, but Giordano's assistance wasn't done out of boundless benevolence. He was an Elder of fairly low Humanity, and lower scruples. And he extended his "assistance" to any and all of his fellow Hecata, whether they wished to accept it or not.
A great many of them were refugees in a foreign land; some of them didn't even speak a lick of English- Fran herself barely did when she first got to this country. Others were simply struggling with the new jobs that they had no other choice but to take.
All the while, Giordano would either stand by and watch them struggle or offer a "charitable" hand that always came at a steep, steep cost.
Francesca stands in front of the Cielo Blu Restaurant. To her, it almost feels like she was still working here just last night- the rolling decades of time are a mere blip to someone with eternal Unlife.
She cranes her neck and gazes at the building, studying the changes- quite the stark contrast from the sleek, retro aesthetic they used to have! The stucco seems to be the same, however.
Well, the time for reminiscing is up. Her actual job now calls to her. It is a nightly thing, and the hours and timings are remarkably flexible. As is the pay. And better yet, that old bastard Giordano had absolutely nothing to do with it.
Back at Pluto Enterprises...
Some of Lena's co-workers are still lurking and lingering about the place. They work overtime or hope to get noticed "working overtime".
Heh.
Gary uses a toothpick to pick his teeth. He flicks it into the dustbin, wipes his hands with a tissue, and trudges out into the main hallway. He nods at the armed guards and takes the steps down to the ground floor of this grand structure.
While at the ground floor, he takes a look around and heads towards the service lift. What's he up to?
The crowd of workers outside is gradually thinning out as people clamber into their vehicles and drive off to Caine knows where. Gary jabs his thumb into the elevator button and the door dings open.
Yet again, he looks over his shoulders to ensure that he isn't being watched. Good- no one seems to care. The bossman steps inside the service elevator. The doors hiss shut.
As he's rumbling down the shaft, he looks up at the dome-shaped surveillance cam and singsongs something or the other which he may have heard on a cartoon once.
The camera follows him as he keeps his eyes trained on its blinking red light. The elevator descends for about five whole floors. It then grinds to a stop.
Balaclava-wearing security personnel holding fully automatic SMGs and assault rifles are standing around the area. There are also some scientists and lab-coated technicians working on various machines and equipment.
The guards stop the bossman and ask to see his ID.
Gary complies by producing his company-issued badge, which the guards scrutinize closely. They curtly nod and hand it back to him once they're satisfied that he is, indeed, who he says he is.
A researcher with messy corkscrew curls accosts Gary and asks him, "Sir, sir! I--I heard that there was a leak earlier this morning? But--"
Gary holds up a finger, "We've dealt with it. You know, you people ought to work on containment a lot better than you do."
The scientist angrily squawks, "But--but Mr. Stuttgart, I was told that--"
Gary sighs and quickens his pace, but the scientist adamantly continues to follow him, "But it genuinely came as a surprise to us all. I mean, Post-Life Entities don't normally--"
The bossman shakes his head from side to side, "I don't wanna hear your excuses- I'm tired and it's been a long day. Go back to doing whatever it is you're supposed to be doing. Which is finding better containment protocols...or so I'd hope."
The scientist quietly mumbles, "Yes, Mr. Stuttgart," and slinks off to join their fellow smart people.
It's eerily cold and sterile down here. The walls are a dull shade of gray and there are numerous scratch marks on the floor. Some of the lights are flickering and others are completely dead.
The multicolored pipes on the walls pump strange fluids, and the ventilation fans hum as they work on full blast. Almost every door is locked and has a minimum of three guards (armed to the teeth) posted in front.
Gary sighs heavily as he stops by an office door on the farthest end of...wherever this place is. Whatever this place is, to be honest. He raps his knuckles on the shiny metal door. A deep and masculine voice inside beckons him to come in.
Gary straightens himself out, checks his tie, brushes his thumb against his mustache, and enters the office with his back slightly arched and his shoulders squared.
A well-built, square-jawed, decidedly middle-aged man with fair skin, dark hair, ocean-blue eyes, and chiseled features is sitting on an ornate desk in front of an array of screens that are displaying all sorts of data and graphs and such.
His three-piece suit is nothing short of immaculate. Custom-made by Italy's finest sartorial minds, as he is oft wont to boast. He's got a smooth voice, calm and cool demeanor, and a clean-shaven face.
He exudes the kind of intensity befitting of his position- the man in charge, the head honcho, the big cheese.
The CEO of Pluto Enterprises himself. Yet, he is also so much more than that, if he ever felt like bragging. And he has been known to do that quite a bit in the past.
The CEO gets up, walks up to his underling (heh), and shakes his hand firmly, "Gary...Gary...Gary. Tell me that you have this situation under control?"
Gary dutifully nods, "Yes, Mr. Blackwell. I've spoken to the employee, and she won't be making any--any waves, sir. But she--"
Dan Blackwell holds up a finger. An expensive ring with a gemstone glints on it. Gary immediately shuts up and awaits his superior to say something.
Dan turns on his heels and returns to his seat behind the desk. He sits back down and steeples his fingers together. He silently nods over to the swiveling chair right opposite him.
Gary takes a seat and continues, "She did get those emails, and she did read through them. The subsequent cleanup ensured that--"
Dan leans back into his own seat and murmurs, "It's all in the report, Stuttgart. No need to go over it again. I just wanna know one thing."
"Sir?"
