"Should I send a team to have Lena...Adams/Akira neutralized? Is she a threat to our...delicate operations?"
Gary gasps and emphatically shakes his head, "No! Not at all, s--sir! She's a--a valued employee. Our recruitment team poached her straight out of Indiana State University. She started off as an intern! Her first-ever job outta college was here. In the company."
Dan adds, "For Pluto Enterprises. Not for the Orpheus Group. We've been...we've been lax, haven't we?"
Gary points out, "Well, sir, in all fairness, we weren't expecting a--a uh...ghost to gain control of a computer and send an email containing classified Orpheus Group data to the email account of an employee of one of our many front-facing subsidiary companies, s--sir."
Dan begrudgingly agrees, "Yes, yes, you're correct. We have ourselves a very mischievous ghost in the machine. I'll let the Projectors do their thing in containing this mess- better to give them a wide, wide berth, hmm?"
"Yes, sir."
The bigger bossman regards the smaller bossman with a cold, hard stare. Gary gulps and shivers in fear. Dan prods, "Why the long face, huh?"
Gary sighs, "Lena Adams has asked for--for a raise, sir."
Dan intones, "Has she, now? Huh. So far, intelligence reports don't seem to indicate any kind of...collaboration between Ms. Adams/Akira and the PLE. You're certain that you closed off all avenues of curiosity that might've bubbled up within her mind?"
Gary replies, "If she tries anything, we could sue her into the ground. Or gently remind her of the many clauses in her contract. But let's not get ahead of ourselves. She's a...valuable employee."
Mr. Blackwell blithely asks, "Why?"
"Err...for certain...diversity...err...quota reasons, I suppose. And she's been with us for quite a while, too. She knows the ins and outs of PE pretty well, sir. I'd rather not resort to drastic measures. And the costs of recruiting and training somebody from scratch far outweigh the benefits, as my last email's CB analysis clearly demonstrates."
The bigger bossman nods approvingly, "Hmm, yes, I saw that- as did the Board. Excellent work. We really loved your creative use of colors. Heh. How does Lena help further our diversity goals?"
Gary explains, "Well, she is part Japanese. Our investigation into her reveals that her father- Yoshiro Akira- was very much in the corporate environment before ill health forced him to retire early."
Dan lazily waves a hand, "We have plenty of Asian Americans working for us, Gary. What makes Lena so...extra special?"
Gary isn't too sure what he's driving at. He nervously shuffles around in his seat and racks his brain for an answer.
"I know that you know the answer. But I wanna hear you say it. Go on. No one's gonna sue us for saying the wrong words or something."
Sweat beads on Gary's forehead. He slowly wipes it away and says, "I'd rather...not."
"Why? It's not like it's a wrong thing. C'mon, let's have some fun, huh? Just a bunch of execs kicking back and shooting the shit. We'll retain Lena until we can't or someone...better comes along. So, what makes her...extra special, hmm?"
Gary lowers his head and mutters, "She is a lesbian- out and--and proud."
"Exactly. See, that wasn't so hard, was it? Nothing wrong with being...different. It's not because we care, mind you. It's because we can use people like her to the max. Everyone comes with an expiry date...so, you gotta make the most of 'em while they're still useful."
A predatory smile creeps up Dan's lips. His pearly teeth glimmer in the dim office light. His brown hair shows signs of graying on the sides.
Gary rubs the base of his neck and nervously prods, "And--and what about our own expiry dates, s--sir?"
Mr. Blackwell clicks his tongue, "We got different roles in corporate America, Gary. Guys like you and me? We got all the time in the world to make our marks- so long as we play by the rules, of course. The rules that we...write, the parameters that we...define." A sly smile spreads across his face.
Gary gulps and nods, "Uh-huh. Sure, sure. So, it's just a box-ticking exercise with people like Lena, right?"
The CEO shrugs indifferently, "It sure is. You know what, for next year's pride parade, we'll look into sponsoring something. Something with glitter, maybe, hmm? If Lena's still with us, put her in the front, blast her airbrushed picture all over our websites and social media pages. Heh-heh."
Gary's eyes dart around as he struggles to find the right thing to say in response, "That uh...sounds reasonable, sir. A bit cynical, sure, but very...reasonable."
Mr. Blackwell waggles a finger, "It's about checking those boxes, Gary. I want this little indiscretion- this little kerfuffle- buried deep. Do you understand me?"
"Yes. But Lena is quite insistent on that raise."
"Most I'll authorize is a one-time bonus, maybe throw in a few free tickets to some WNBA games, hahahahaha!"
Gary awkwardly chuckles along, "Yeah, haha! Do you mind if I ask about the PLE--"
Dan firmly interjects, "It'll be contained. It won't be sending any more emails out anytime soon. Don't worry. You just do your thing topside and leave us mole people to our own devices. But we all know who's really on top." He chuckles at the end.
Gary tightens his tie and nervously nods, "Of--of course, sir. And the Board--?"
"The Orpheus Board is only interested in one thing. And if you went to business school- like I did- then you'd know exactly what I'm talking about, eh?"
"Maximizing shareholder value," Gary regurgitates like the good little cog in the machine that he is.
Dan points a finger at him and winks, "Bingo. And I've got myself a helluva payout by the time this year draws to a close. Hey, you try running two whole companies from under the ground and tell me how it feels, hmm?"
Mr. Stuttgart notes, "The front-facing subsidiaries are absolutely necessary to further our...expansion and...keep Orpheus safe from competitors, rival Projection Firms, and the law, of course." He even throws in a little laugh.
"What rivals? We've either flattened 'em or bought 'em all out. The law's on our side- always has been. No, what we do here...all that stuff with the dead...that's what we need to keep hidden."
Dan leans forward, placing his hands on the edge of his desk, "The front-facing subsidiaries serve many purposes. Recruitment numbers are down, Gary. The Board wants 'em up. Poaching universities is not exactly yielding the desired results. We wanna get 'em younger."
"Wha...?"
"Relax. We've got some pilot programs in Canada targeting high schools. Our friends across the forty-ninth parallel will help us out- as long as we help them out, too. Mind you, this isn't for Orpheus; we're not seeking Projectionists. Yet. We're filling the other vacancies, and then some!"
Gary nods along as he does his best to keep up, "I see. Uh...this is news to me, sir. And what about finding competent Projectionists? How's that avenue of recruitment doing, Mr. Blackwell?" There's a bit of an edge to his voice.
"Eh, that's way beyond your paygrade. Heh. All I can say is that we've got feelers out, and that we'll find our perfect people eventually. Yeah, eventually."
The CEO's bulky computer dings. He looks at the notification onscreen, then glances towards Gary. He trills his lips, "Ah, she's here. Our consultant. You're gonna wanna meet this one, Gary. She's an interesting lady. Oh...and...our shipments have FINALLY come through. Goddamn."
"Huh?"
Dan clicks his tongue and types something on his keyboard, "Cryogenic pods from our manufacturer in England. The company's called CODACORP in this funny city called Gloughchester. I went there for an investor and innovation summit back in 2009."
Gary timidly asks, "I...um...received a memo, an email about a--a lost shipment of Pigment? Should we be concerned, sir?"
Dan scratches his cheek, "Ah, nah, not really. If you're gonna do cocaine, just make sure it ain't black, OK? We'll cooperate with the MCPD's narcotics division to...either recover the Gunpowder or...make it disappear."
"Hopefully not into people, sir."
"Oh, please. The investigation into how our stash of special drugs wound up in the wrong hands is still ongoing. No, I refuse to give further comment. Don't worry, once there's anything concrete to share, it will be sent to you and the other executives."
Gary stands up and futzes around with his tie, "Well, uh...let's go and meet this consultant of yours." He fidgets around as Dan stares at him unblinking for a few seconds.
Dan plainly puts in, "We're thinking of getting you people lanyards. Thoughts? It's for PE employees only. The geeks down here are afraid that they might get garroted by a PLE if they started running around with lanyards, hahaha!"
Gary shifts his feet and twists his neck from side to side, "Uhhh...that sounds like something that my people would like. Just...um...after we're done containing the email-sending PLE."
"Duly noted, c'mon. And lemme make the introductions, OK? She's Italian and I'm thinking of asking her out for...wine and cheese, heh-heh."
(!)
Francesca holds onto her Gucci purse while the same elevator that Gary came in begins to descend deep down to where all the "action" is. Yeah, she's a consultant for the Orpheus Group.
It'd be more accurate to call her a "Death Consultant". As Morbid as that sounds, it's pretty much on brand for, well, all the parties involved from the Hecata Clan to Orpheus itself.
The Orpheus Group bends and harnesses certain pliable ghosts (and other spooky entities) to something...profitable, something turned and more attuned towards the corporate will. The Hecata Clan- as a whole- are deeply invested in the works of this Projectionist Org.
While Dan walks with Gary, the latter insists, "Lena is--is a very devoted and dedicated employee. Firing or--or neutralizing her wouldn't be necessary, sir. She's deeply valuable to our surface-level program, s--sir."
Dan dryly replies, "The Board is still pretty pissed that private Orpheus correspondence accidently made its way into her inbox- they don't give a damn if a ghost sent the email. But you're right, she's been with us for...a long time. Straight outta college. And she interned with us, too. But...most I can do is a one-time bonus, got that?"
"Y--yeah, that would work. Yes--yes, sir." Gary sighs and sweats, hoping that Lena's situation isn't going to be blown out of proportion by the Board.
The elevator door hisses open. The armed guards stand aside and watch as Francesca casually strides through the place. The researchers nervously look towards Dan and Gary, trying their hardest not to ogle at the attractive Italian woman with her Gucci bag slung over her shoulder.
Dan spreads his hands out, "Miss Livroni! How wonderful to see you again!"
Francesca plainly greets him back, "Mister Blackwell. Please, call me Francesca."
"Of course, of course. Francesca. What a beautiful name...for a very...beautiful woman. Heh." He grins, hoping for a positive reaction, but the vampire simply stares at him, uninterested.
She stares at Gary and extends her cold hand as way of introducing herself. Gary shakily reciprocates, "Gary Stuttgart. P--pleased to meet you."
Dan sighs and explains, "Gary's the one running things topside. We had ourselves a little incident earlier today, BUT! All fixed now, eh? Heh."
Fran nods, "I will leave that in your capable hands. I am here to...help you with your problems, Mister Blackwell."
"Yes! And if there's anything that I could do for you, then..."
"You could show me to the usual room. I take it that it has been sterilized and cleaned thoroughly since my last visit?"
Dan gives her a thumbs-up, "It sure has. This way, please."
The trio walk on down the hallways. Francesca asks Gary, "Do you mind telling me about this problem of yours, Mister Stuttgart?"
Gary tugs on his collar, "Eh, it's honestly not worth going over. Just a--a PLE acting up. N--nothing serious."
"I insist on knowing."
Dan nudges Gary and fixes him with a pointed glare, as if using him as a proxy to, hopefully, impress the vampire lady. Gary sighs and reluctantly explains, "There was a breach involving one of our surface-level subsidiaries. A PLE had, somehow, managed to send an email containing sensitive Orpheus-related documents to an employee of...mine. You know how these...entities are."
Francesca hums, "Hmm. Always eager to cause mischief, no matter which dimension they inhabit. Yes, I am all too familiar with our...otherworldly friends."
The three of them share a hearty laugh as they reach the end of a particularly long hallway.
There are four security guards standing in front of this door, one of whom is carrying a pump-action shotgun. She's clad in body armor and is holding the weapon in a way that suggests experience with firearms.
Dan confidently waves his hand and they quickly step aside. They respectfully nod their heads at Francesca and mutter, "Miss. Good to see you again. Room's all yours."
"Grazie. Grazie."
Francesca steps into a spartan room. It has no windows and is decently lit. The temperature in here is eerily cold. Gary and Dan both shiver while the immortal chick barely registers the change in temperature.
In the middle of the room is a high-backed chair with a few scratch marks on it. Other than that, literally nothing else is in the room. It's empty. Francesca runs her finger across a wall and rubs it against her thumb, making a face when she notices some dust particles.
Her gloved hand tugs on the single chair in the center of the room. She adjusts its position, turning it so that it's facing away from the two guys in the room with her. She looks over her shoulder and requests some privacy.
Gary's eyebrows go up in surprise. Dan presses his lips into a thin line, nods, smiles a little, and leaves her be.
The door clicks shut. Gary nervously looks at the CEO.
The CEO glares back at the smalltime bossman, "What? We deal with restless ghosts- we get all kinds of strange stuff thrown our way. I don't know why you're acting so nervous, Gary. Now, c'mon, let's go check those cryogenic pods out- I wanna see if the Brits still make good shit."
Francesca demurely sits down on the chair. She sets the Gucci purse aside and places her hands on her lap. She straightens her back, shuts her eyes, and begins to channel trace amounts of her Vitae into her Necromancy Discipline.
(It's in the name).
The room around her closes in. The lighting erratically dims and brightens. The floor begins to rumble, but she stays focused on her task at hand.
Fran takes off her leather glove. Her sharp claws pop out, and she pokes her right index finger with a wince. A singular drop of Blood oozes out and drips onto the floor. One drop is all that is needed. In this instance, at least.
She closes her eyes and focuses her supernatural energies once more. Thousands upon thousands of faint whispers tickle her ears- and with vampiric super-senses, it can get pretty damn overwhelming, even for a somebody like her.
Francesca pushes her way through the restless dead as they try to trip her up and distract her with various pleas and demands.
"I just...I just wanna tell my wife that I love her. One--one more time," a sad-sounding man begs.
"Did--did I lock the door--no?! Yes?! FUCK! Did I lock my door?! That's--that's all I wanna know!"
"Is Mr. Wiggles OK? I don't trust my niece with Mr. Wiggles- that kid always had a glint in her eyes. And not the good kind."
"Why am I still stuck here?! WHHHHHHHY?!"
"Hey, hey, turn up the volume. I gotta know the scores. I gotta know if the team that I blew ten large on won or--or not! Turn up the volume. Uh...please??"
Francesca tightly grimaces and pushes past the incessant voices of the restless dead.
The number of souls within this facility alone is...mind-bogglingly staggering. Fran seeks out a particular person amongst the ranks of the departed. A person who is among those with...certain unfinished business.
It isn't long before a howling wind begins to fill the room. A tear opens up in the floor as a ghostly hand reaches through and grasps at Francesca's ankle. The vampire doesn't flinch or jump out of the chair- she is as calm and still as a corpse cucumber.
The hand looks translucent. The fingernails are manicured, not calloused, and a few shiny diamond bracelets adorn the wrist. A second hand emerges from the floor and clamps onto Francesca's knee.
Francesca keeps her eyes shut. The ghostly being grunts and groans as she tries to pull herself up. When she is finally present, the Necromancer slowly opens her eyes. Her blurry vision takes a few seconds to adjust to the abrupt shift in lighting and atmosphere.
When her senses return to normal, she's met with the sight of an incredibly pretty young woman- early twenties, round face, full cheeks, graceful smile, smooth complexion, bright red lips, and sparkling eyes.
Her translucence, not withstanding, Francesca is taken aback by just how pretty this ghost still is.
The ghost's hollow voice echoes throughout the chamber, "Did--did you find my stashes?"
Fran greets her with a nod, "I did, yes." She holds up the Gucci purse and gestures to it.
The dead socialite smiles a little, "Good, good. I--I see that you've helped yourself to it. But don't help yourself to ALL of it, OK?"
"Yes. Of course. What you are doing is...admirable. I will be sure to distribute your stashed items to those who truly need them."
"T--thank you, Francesca. Thank you."
The ghostly being standing in front of the vampire was once a wealthy (and shallow) socialite who eventually overdosed on OxyContin and died in her sleep. Her life was so empty, so shallow, and utterly devoid of any meaning.
She had stashed certain valuable items all over this Domain- designer clothing, trinkets worth a lot of $$$$$, handbags, cash, and shit like that.
Francesca, for her part, is here to get the lowdown on ALL of this lady's stashes and hopefully put the contents therein to better use than she ever could.
It's not about exploiting this soul. And said departed soul is pretty keen on doing some semblance of good now that she has all eternity to ponder upon the shallowness of her life. Whether it'll actually fix a thing remains to be seen.
"Thank you again for all that you have done for me, Francesca. Please, please ensure that my stuff is put to better use, OK?"
"I will do my best," the Hecata vampire equably answers. And she does intend to follow up on her promise. That cash that she gave to Sal's caretaker was just a small portion of this ghost socialite's stash.
And there's gotta be plenty of needy people out there who would greatly appreciate a thousand bucks right about now. Yep, she stashed large sums of money away. Why is anyone's guess. Perhaps the ghost was afraid of losing her wealth in one way or another.
Francesca inquires, "Why did you do all of these? Would if not have been easier for you to simply write a check to a charity of some kind?"
The socialite responds, "When I was...not dead, I was a--a party girl. Morning, afternoon, evening, and night. Shots, cocaine, parties, other...stuff, you name it, I was into it."
"Hmm. Yes. You were very popular among your social circles. I was recently looking at your Instagram profile. You should know that many of those whom you called as your 'friends' do not even miss you- they have forgotten about you. You might as well have not existed at all in the eyes of your 'friends'."
The spectral entity lowers her head in shame. Something resembling tears stream down her ghostly face. She shrugs, "Yeah, I--I guess you're right. But it's too darn late for me to--to fix anything now, right?"
"Yes. And I am sorry. But you...had so much when so many had so little. And all you could think about was making these stashes like it was all some kind of game to you. Why? What reason could you have for doing such things?"
"It's all I knew. I...my daddy and mom always told me that taxes were--were a form of theft, a way for the government to steal money from honest, smart, and--and talented people like us. And they'd steal that money and give it to--to...lesser people."
Francesca shakes her head, disgusted with what she is hearing, "You truly have no idea of the suffering that people are going through, do you?"
"I...went to Africa for a photo op. My uncle's PR firm said it'd be good for my image. Does that count?"
"No. It doesn't. What I want to know is WHY did you keep these items instead of putting them to use for some good?"
The ghost's shrug comes off as slow and sluggish, "It felt...good? It felt...funny? I was gonna send a friend of mine on, like, this scavenger hunt. Or maybe I would start my own, like, YouTube channel or--or reality TV series where you have to find...my stuff!"
Francesca pinches the bridge of her nose and lets out a long sigh, "That is not how these things work. Hmph, never mind. What do you wish for me to do with the remaining items of your stash? And you must tell me where the remaining items are, I do not like this...this scavenger hunt."
The ghostly being looks around the empty chamber for a few seconds before her shoulders droop, "Um...c'mon, I--I thought it'd be more fun if I drip-fed the information to ya...rather than just dump it all on you at--at once."
"If that is your wish...but this Gucci bag is mine; I like it. Everything else I will gladly distribute to those in need."
The socialite shrugs, "OK. How has my family reacted to my...death?"
Francesca looks at the ground and the single drop of Blood that she used to summon this spirit, "They...cremated you--"
"WHAT?!"
The lights violently flicker. The room rumbles and shakes. The guards posted outside jump in fright. They clutch their weapons a little tighter and shoot each other furtive glances- should they bust in or wait outside here?
Francesca maintains her composure. The ghost, on the other hand, shrieks and wails, "WHAT DO YOU MEAN THEY CREMATED ME?!"
Fran stands up from the chair and begins to chant something in Italian. The ghost violently hisses, "YOU DIDN'T FUCKING ANSWER MY QUESTION! BITCH! BITCH! BITCH! BITCH! BITCH!"
The Necromancer keeps up her chanting and gestures at the spirit with a wave of her hand. The ghost shrieks louder, her wailing echoing throughout the room. Her voice becomes so shrill and ear-piercing that a few alarms from beyond the closed door are being set off.
The guards gulp and take a few steps back, but their fingers are right on the triggers of their respective firearms. Like that'll do a fucking thing to stop an incorporeal entity.
Francesca glowers daggers at the ghostly being and doubles her efforts, muttering and chanting louder. The being wails and darts around the place, her body leaving blurry trails as she goes from corner to corner of this room, desperately searching for a means of escape.
She tries to shoulder-bash the only door leading in and out of this room, but all it does is make the metal reverberate like a gong.
Francesca digs her claw deep into her wrist, violently twists it, and lets some of her Vitae pool on the floor. The creature's shrieks stop and she turns her full and undivided attention to the vampire. The lights flicker and go out completely, but the emergency red lights immediately kick in and bathe the chamber in their eerie glow.
The ghost growls and heaves with rage as she menacingly floats towards the Hecata. Francesca ceases her chanting and confidently strides towards the ghost. The vampire bares her fangs at the fuming socialite.
A hiss escapes from the ghost's mouth, "NO! NO! I--I thought that they'd preserve my body cryogenically! NOOOOOOOO! They promised! Daddy promised to freeze me if I ever died! Mom said that they'd at least freeze my head!!!! They spent millions--"
Francesca firmly commands, "Be gone to where you now belong! I cannot speak to you like this; I CANNOT deal with you when you are in such a state!"
"B--B--Bitch!"
"BEGONE!"
The ghost whimpers and ducks behind the chair, quivering with fear, "I'm..."
"GO! When you have calmed, then we shall speak again! Until then...go. NOW!"
The dead socialite hisses and is soon sucked back into the very same gaping tear in the floor. Numerous howls and growls emanate from it before it seals itself shut. Fran stands over the spot and sighs deeply. She places her hands on her hips and stares at the ground for a few seconds.
After gathering her thoughts, Francesca picks up the Gucci bag and walks towards the door with her head held high. She knocks on it a few times. The guards give her a once-over to ensure that she isn't possessed or anything before letting her out.
The shotgunner chick nervously asks, "Miss, h--how did it go in there? We--we were concerned that you may have been compromised. Wouldn't be the first time that someone went in normal and emerged completely taken over by...y'know..."
"No, no, everything is fine," the Italian vampire reassures her, "just a little bit of a tantrum, that is all. Grazie."
The Orpheus guards nod at her and step aside. Dan approaches her with outstretched arms, "Ah, you are back! Wonderful!"
Out of concern, he asks, "I got notified of some alarms getting tripped. I was this close to sending a tac team in to pull you out and subdue the...ghost, if need be."
Francesca shakes her head and smiles, "There is no need for that. I am fully capable of handling myself in any given situation, Mr. Blackwell. Please, wait for a few hours before sending people in there to sterilize the room. Lingering energies of the deceased can remain present for quite some time."
"Duly noted. Now, why don't we go to my office, hmm?"
"OK. And where is Gary?"
Dan walks a little faster, "He had to get home to his family. Oh, boy, am I glad to be...a bachelor again. An eligible bachelor." He's hinting at something.
The Necromancer faintly chuckles, "Hm. I noticed the prominent tan line on your ring finger, Mr. Blackwell. I am sorry for your loss."
"What? No. I--it was a divorce. Although, I wouldn't have minded if my now-ex-wife...ya know? And that's quite perceptive of you, noticing such things."
"It is not good to wish for such things on other people, no matter who they are or what they've done."
Dan awkwardly clears his throat chuckles, "Haha. Y--yeah. C'mon, let's talk business, hmm?"
Francesca sits on a chair. Dan sits on the edge of his desk (yikes) and looks down at her, "Did it all go well in there?"
"Sì. Are there any more restless spirits that you want me to speak with in this facility?"
He shrugs, "Nah, just the one for tonight. Her dad and mom aren't just clients of ours- they're investors, too. And not small-time chump change, mind you. So, we...uh...kinda wanna stay in their good graces for as long as we can."
"Hmm. And those whose interests I represent want you to know that they shall continue to lend their support to the Orpheus Group...provided that you uphold your end of the bargain."
Dan smiles and claps his hands together, "Of course, of course! Always! Um...do you mind if I ask you something?"
Francesca motions for him to speak his mind, "Please do, I will try to answer as best I can."
Mr. Blackwell directly asks, "Are you free for a drink? Coffee, wine, water, milkshake, it doesn't matter."
Francesca's face tics and she straightens her back a little more, "I--I apologize, Mr. Blackwell, but I have other obligations to tend to this night."
"Ah, c'mon, it's just one drink. I'll buy."
Francesca musters up a small smile and slowly gets out of her seat, "It is best if you did not do that. Sorry, I think I should be taking my leave now."
Dan desperately points out, "I--I'm divorced. It was as amicable as--as these things usually are. C'mon, you're single, I'm...also single. Let's go get a drink. Or maybe I could take you somewhere nice, hmm? No shortage of nice places in Magnolia City. What's your vibe, huh? Art gallery? Upscale lounge? An exclusive club?"
Francesca shakes her head, "Grazie, but no."
Dan sucks some air through his teeth and twiddles his thumbs together, "We've known each other for what, a few years now?"
"Mm-hmm."
"Your file doesn't mention any...special guy. Not--not that it's any of my business, of course. You came highly recommended to us from your...pals."
Francesca politely nods at him and keeps her distance, "Hmm. Yes. However, there is something that you should know about me."
Dan folds his arms and raises his eyebrows, "Oh? Oh, do tell."
Ms. Livroni plainly states, "I...go in the other direction, and only the other direction. If you are as educated as you are, then you will fully understand what I mean by that. And I'm not going to apologize for it."
Dan's lips form a round "O" shape before his face contorts with realization, "Ah...OK. I--I see. Wow. Whoa. Uh...I never clocked it. I--I mean, you don't seem...the type to uh--"
Francesca holds up a gloved finger, "Please, let us not discuss such things. I do not know you very well. It is a private matter and nobody else's concern. But I wanted to be honest and forthright with you. I suppose that there are other...interested people out there for you to try your luck with. Although, you might want to brush up on your..."
"Detection skills," Dan lamely murmurs.
"No. But I will leave you to your own devices, hmm? Buona notte (ITA: Goodnight)."
The Necromancer turns on her heel, winks, and leaves the office with a defeated and disappointed Mr. Blackwell leaning against his desk and shaking his head in dismay.
She lifts her chin, smirks a little, and casually sashays outta the underground Orpheus facility with a spring in her step. She fiddles with her leather glove while waiting for the elevator to make its way up to the ground floor.
Once she's out of the subterranean structure, the Hecata vampire takes a deep breath of the crisp night air and slowly exhales. It was quite stuffy down there in that room. But her...conversation with the ghost of the deceased socialite went decidedly well, all things considered.
For now, the Graverobber slowly walks to the subway station and decides to return to her Haven- it's been quite an eventful night so far.
(And it was about to get hella eventful in the nights to come!)
While Lena Adams is getting ready for bed (it's been a very long and taxing day!), her phone suddenly lights up, and the ringing soon follows. She sighs weakly and answers it, her tone sounding somewhat tired, "Hello, Lena speaking."
Elise shyly greets her, "Uh, hello...Lena?"
Lena rubs her tired eyes, "Oh, Elise. Hi. Hey. W--what's up?"
"I just wanted to thank you for, well..." Elise's voice fades away.
Lena gets what she's hinting at, "Yeah, I had a great time, too. But I dunno if things would've worked out between the two of us. And I'm not the type to lead people on for shits and giggles. You're nice and you're amazingly hot, but you--"
Elise sniffs, "I know, I know- I still haven't...moved on. It's not easy, you know."
"Grief was never meant to be easy, that's the whole point. You need time, you need space. I understand that. I get that. Believe me, I do. You're not ready. Not yet."
Elise weakly smiles, "Thank you for your candor. Can...can we at least remain friends? N--no sex, of course. Just friends."
Lena yawns and scratches her ear, "Um...sure, sure. We can work on that. Look, I don't mean to come off as rude, but it is in the middle of the night, and I've had a VERY long- and VERY stressful- day at work. So...can we catch up sometime later?"
Elise fervently nods, "Yes, yes. My--my bad. Sorry. I just wanted to properly know where we...stood after our...date. Your aptitude in the bedroom is quite extraordinary and--"
Lena cuts her off, "Yeah. I've been called remarkable before, heh. And you weren't too bad yourself. Goodnight, Elise. And...I do sincerely hope that you find your happiness, joy, and fulfillment one day. But that's not gonna come from me OR be me, for that matter. But we can try to be friends...without the benefits, of course."
"I understand. Yes, friends without THOSE kinds of benefits, haha! Goodnight, Lena," Elise softly whispers and hangs up.
Lena blows a raspberry and sets her phone aside. She flops into bed, pulls the blanket up over her, and rolls over onto her side. Sleep eventually finds the PI.
As per usual, her dreaming involves her being thrown into that infinitely endless ocean. She sinks deeper and deeper into this body of water. Yet, the drowning sensation doesn't fully consume her at all. But she is still afraid. Afraid of what or who, though?
She is unsure.
Chapter Five: Falsehoods
The sun rises and the Kindred in this part of the wide world begin to fall to their day-slumbering. The Childer of the night will have to remain hidden until the next sunset. Their bodies enter a near-Torpid state as they lay motionless and silent, like corpses.
The daylight hours will pass 'em by.
Francesca goes as still as the grave. Her chest doesn't rise or fall. Her eyes are closed and a relatively serene expression plays across her pale face. A few strands of blonde hair hang across her cheeks.
The searing light of the sun is blocked out by curtains and thick drapes that cover every single window in her Haven.
Lena, a mere mortal (for now), wakes up with a start a few hours later. It's a brand new, bright, and sunny Tuesday morning. Her eyes dart around the room for a few moments before her heartbeat calms itself down.
That infinite drowning-not-drowning dream always sets her on edge. She should see a therapist (or trained expert) about it, but...well...work. And mental healthcare is NOT covered under Lena's insurance plan, so she'll have to fork out the money to pay for therapy sessions if she wants to pursue it.
Lena sheds her clothing and steps into the shower. Her jet-black hair is soaked within seconds. She uses plenty of soap to scrub away all the dirt, grime, and sweat. As usual, the hot water turns lukewarm by the time the gumshoe is done cleaning herself.
While she wraps the towel around her naked body and heads back into her bedroom, she tells herself, "Tuesdays. Not as bad as Mondays. Just work, work, work, work, and more work."
She snickers and adds, "And an equal amount of coffee to go along with that. Heh."
As for Tyler's case, the most logical thing to do is to expand the search- somebody (or something) is bound to have seen something, namely the car that hit the poor child and left him to die on a street in Magnolia City.
The PI will dutifully poke around. But she's also got other cases on her plate as well; some people are paying decent bucks for her services. It's mainly tailing spouses who may or may not be cheating on their partners. Or looking into whether or not their children are messing with drugs or boozing.
Or fooling around.
Miss Adams swings by GROUND BEANZ for a cuppa coffee and a quick bite to give her energy to face the long day ahead. From there, it's a fairly simple walk to Crystal Street Station.
The train came one minute late. Lena shakes her head and steps aboard it, lightly jostled by the movement of the vehicle as it departs the station.
While sitting on a relatively clean seat, Lena taps her foot incessantly while she waits. She pulls out her phone and Googles: "Thrown into ocean infinitely falling dream meaning."
The results are...decidedly mixed (and this was before Google's somewhat annoying AI overview thing!). Some say that it symbolizes something bad happening in the near future, while others state that it's about a person's fears or some such. A few claim that it corresponds to feeling overwhelmed or scared.
She taps on the first link on the first page and starts to read the text aloud under her breath, "'Perhaps you are struggling with an area of your life and are drowning in the turmoil surrounding said area.' Wow, real helpful."
As the train chugs along, she keeps reading this short article while taking sips of her piping hot coffee. It goes on to suggest that she should try to gain a better understanding of her fears and what's causing her to feel overwhelmed.
"Yeah, no shit. If only I could actually afford something like that..." she quietly mutters, still skimming through the text.
When the train stops at the intended destination, Lena sighs, stands up, and makes her way out of the train and onto the platform. The underground air is cool and damp, but it smells kinda stale.
She quickens her pace and is soon going up the glass elevators that lead to the Pluto Enterprises office on the second floor. She admires the interior of the building (and its amazing atrium) through the glass panes, smiling to herself all the while.
"You don't always get a view like this," she tells the bored-looking intern standing apart a few feet from her.
The college kid nods politely and wordlessly. His suit looks quite oversized, and he keeps readjusting his tie. The young man is obviously new on the job and is still trying to earn his place in the big wide world of...whatever it is he's doing.
Lena passes the security check and gets ready for work. She can feel the eyes of her co-workers burrowing into the back of her head as she logs into her terminal and sees if there are any new messages for her in her inbox.
After yesterday's eventful events, everyone in PE is a bit on edge today. Still, Lena puts on a brave front which convinces everybody that everything is normal, although her co-workers aren't wholly buying it.
Some give her a wide berth or outright avoid her as though she were oozing infectious gas from every pore of her skin. Many others, however, are more sympathetic and kinder, wishing her well and saying that they hope she'll have a great day at work today.
Lena goes about her day job. When it's time for lunch, she heads downstairs and walks past numerous kiosks, pop-up stands, and vending machines before settling on buying herself a chicken sandwich from a bakery.
It comes packaged in a transparent container. She sits by a fountain and begins to eat. Her amber eyes scan the area for any familiar faces, but none stand out in the crowd. A half-consumed cup of coffee is beside her, slowly going cold as the minutes pass by.
The sammich is adequate, if a little cold. She tosses the empty and crumb-filled container into the colored bin, stands up, dusts off her pants, and goes to the bathroom to freshen herself up a little. She checks her teeth and her makeup, then heads back up to the second floor.
As she's about to step inside, a call comes in on her phone. Lena sees the caller ID and answers it, "Toby?"
"Hi--hi. Are you free to talk right now?"
Lena leans against a wall and looks at her co-workers who are going in and out of the office, "Sure. What's going on?"
Toby nervously replies, "It's Eddie. I--I'm kinda worried about him."
Lena's brows knit together in a frown of concern, "Did something happen to him?"
Toby takes a deep breath, "Well...he's been...showing off a lot lately. I dunno where- or how- he gets all of this cash from. He won't tell me. I--I think he might be into some stuff, ya know? I don't wanna say or--or assume anything nasty or unpleasant, but I'm concerned for him. I mean, we're best friends and all, and I just wanna look out for him."
Lena sighs, "Have you tried speaking to him about it and asking how he suddenly got a ton of cash?"
"Oh, I have, yeah. He just dismisses me and brushes me off. Sound familiar?"
Lena clicks her tongue, "Seriously? Look, I have work to do and I really should be getting back to it, alright?"
Toby desperately asks, "What should I do? I have to work with him at the garage day in and day out, y'know? I dunno how much longer I can just pretend that nothing's wrong. Every time I look into his eyes, I get the impression that he's hiding something huge from me...from US!"
Lena rubs the base of her neck and dryly smiles at a passing co-worker, "Listen, I'm gonna try and see what I can do, alright? I'll speak to him. But I can't make any promises- I'm just too busy right now."
Toby stammers, "And p--please leave my name outta this, alright? I don't want him to know that I...that I told you, ya know?"
"I won't. Don't worry."
"Have a great rest of the afternoon, Lena!!"
"Hm. You too, Toby. You too. Bye," Lena ends the call and shakes her head in dismay. She blows a raspberry as the questions begin to swell up in her mind. They swirl around up in her noggin along with all the other queries, doubts, suspicions, and concerns.
She slowly makes her way back to her desk and settles into her chair. The afternoon sun bathes the city in a yellow-white glow. People hurry to their homes, streetcars move at a snail's pace, and trains come and go.
If Ms. Adams has time, then she'll see what can be done about Eddie, but nothing beyond that, of course. As far as the PI is concerned, the guy probably broke his piggy bank open or saved up and is now spending it all on...whatever.
But a part of her is, admittedly, quite curious to know where all the money is coming from and how Eddie has been managing to spend so much so quickly.
This is just another task piled on top of her (already) mountainous list of to-dos and chores, however, and Lena isn't even getting paid to figure out the mystery behind Eddie's sudden influx of cold, hard cash.
By four forty-seven, Lena sluggishly stands up from her chair, gathers her belongings, and bids a good day to everyone who isn't actively ignoring or avoiding her. Tight smiles and waves come her way as she struts past everyone and eventually steps outside and onto the streets of the city.
Gary, her boss, walks past her. He's holding a messy toothpick in between his index finger and thumb.
Lena glares at him, "I'm done for the day."
Gary awkwardly shuffles on his feet and nods, "Yeah, sure. H--have a great evening. And uh...see you tomorrow!" He walks off with an anxious bounce to his step.
Lena clears her throat and turns towards him. She stares at the back of his shiny head, "About that raise?"
Gary stiffens and spins on his heel, "Uh--yeah. I'm still in talks w--with the relevant higher-ups. Make sure that you formally apply with HR for a raise, OK?"
Corporate speak for, "Fuck off, you're not getting it. Keep slogging away at the job or you'll be fired."
Lena presses her luck, "Am I or am I not a highly skilled and valuable asset to Pluto Enterprises?"
Gary nervously chews on the messy end of his toothpick and evasively answers, "Y--you are! Of course! But this is a highly competitive company. And there are a dime-a-dozen people out there who are more than happy to work for what we pay them."
Ms. Adams counters, "C'mon, Gary, thirty thousand dollars a year is--is hardly gonna make anyone happy, especially in this city. I don't know anyone who'd work for that kind of money. Most of the kids here are fresh outta college and are already mired in debt."
She steps closer to him, her gaze piercing and intense, "Prices have shot up like crazy, rents are insanely high, and a lotta people are struggling to stay afloat. I honestly don't know how I do it sometimes...I need that raise."
Gary gulps and intones, "The employee handbook expressly forbids discussions of compensations outside of a--a workplace setting." To reinforce his silly point, he gestures to the bustling city around them.
Lena grits her teeth, "Then why don't we have this discussion back inside, hmm?"
The bossman takes his toothpick out of his mouth, shrugs, and chuckles weakly, "We--we already did. And I said that I'm looking into it on your behalf. In the meantime, file the required paperwork- if we have a record of this, it'll go over a whole lot smoother. Trust me."
The PI sighs, "OK...but if I don't see anything by the end of this week--"
Gary sternly warns her, "Miss Adams...Lena, we already have you snooping through certain information that you had no business seeing, and the higher-ups aren't particularly happy with that little unfortunate...eh...screw-up. Go down this path, and your future here at PE will be very...unpleasant."
Lena narrows her eyes, "Is that a threat, Gary? It was just a bunch of stupid emails with even stupider mumbo-jumbo laced throughout. You're talking about it like it's some classified shit. Wait...is it? What...what 'Beach' are we 'On'??"
Gary's face tics and he quickly snaps, "It's nothing. It's nothing, alright? Drop this, please just...just...drop it. You should still be glad that you--you have a job after what happened yesterday, and that you'll continue to be employed in the foreseeable future. But only if you keep your nose out of places it doesn't belong."
Lena points out, "I'm a fucking Private Investigator. If I did that, I'd be a disgrace to the profession! Look, I want the raise, and you know damn well that I deserve it. I've been here since graduating!"
Gary removes his glasses and rubs the bridge of his nose, "Look, Miss Adams, this has gone on for too long. We'll look into your raise."
He jabs her shoulder with his index finger, "And you don't get to set the ultimatums around here. Do your job properly, and if we think you've earned a raise, you'll get one. You've been around long enough to know how these things work; I shouldn't have to remind you of this."
Lena gives up, sighs, wordlessly nods, and goes on her way. Gary heads back inside the building.
The PI heads to her PI office, pushes the door open, and plops down in her chair. She throws her bag and coat aside, rubs her face, groans, and spins around in circles, trying to burn off her frustrations.
"Ah, shit. I need some coffee if I'm gonna get through the rest of the evening...now, let's see what I can cross off the list..."
The sun soon begins its grandiose descent. The purple-orange skies soon turn into an ominous dark blue. The Kindred of this Domain begin to stir awake, their Beasts ravenous with the eternal Hunger.
And there is very little choice but to slake the thirst lest the Beast takes over and slakes it without any regard for niceties, secrecy, discretion, or making sure that the victim is still breathing at the end of it all.
Amos Greenberg resides in a duplex industrial loft-styled penthouse near the city's outskirts. Gray walls, black railings, drab floors, and off-white furniture create an odd yet functional design.
The False Assamite is busy doing push-ups in his home gym- a massive room with its own aircon system, bulletproof glass windows, and a bunch of state-of-the-art training equipment.
The shirtless man grunts with effort as his strong arms flex and strain, his dead green eyes locked onto the floor as watery blood drips down his forehead and falls onto the black mat beneath.
His muscles twitch as he lifts himself off of the ground and then pushes himself back up again and again and again and again. A few more droplets of blood-sweat trickle down his face, but the vampire doesn't even pause his exercise.
Amos growls, bares his fangs, and keeps going. Once he's done with that, he springs up with a preternatural speed. He ducks, leaps, twists, flips, and somersaults his way through the home gym, being careful not to overturn any of his precious equipment.
He picks up a big barbell, sits on the edge of an adjustable gym bench, and starts doing his reps, moving the weighted thing up and down as he grunts and strains. Amos tightly holds onto it with his hand. His arms are bulging with exertion.
The vampire muscle man grits his teeth and keeps going, pushing through the pain and agony that comes with working out like a madman. After a few minutes of this, he puts the barbell back in its place and wipes himself with a clean towel.
He grimaces at how bloody it looks, "Wow. Every single liquid in our body. Blood. Even those ones. Ah, you are what you eat. Never forget that."
Amos wraps some elastic bands around his knuckles and begins punching a sandbag suspended by a sturdy chain in the corner of the room, making sure that he maintains the correct posture as he delivers blow after powerful blow.
The chain itself rattles with each severe punch that the vamp lands on the sturdy bag. It wouldn't be the first time that Mr. Greenberg has smacked the bag right outta the chain and spilled its contents onto the floor.
"One...two...three...four...grrrrrah...five...grrrrrrhgh...six...hrrrrrr...seven...haaaaaaarrhhg...eight...nine...grrrr...ten! From the top...ten...grrrrfgh...nine...eight...seven...six...hffffrghgh...five...four...three...whoo...two...yeah...one!"
Muscle man does that at least five more times, each time the chain holding the bag slightly whining and getting ready to give way. Again.
Amos sighs with exhaustion and reaches does a few impromptu crunches. He drops to the floor and begins doing it. All the while, no breath comes in or out of his mouth- his lungs aren't working and don't have to.
Before he's about to shove his earphones into his ears and do a few minutes of weight-lifting, the doorbell rings- it's a loud and grating sound that echoes throughout his Haven.
He stands up and heads over to his closet while yelling, "Just a sec!" He puts on a fresh gray shirt, tosses the towel into a hamper, runs a hand through his blonde hair, and goes to see who the nocturnal visitor is.
A brunette woman of average height stands at the door, dressed in an off-black t-shirt and skinny jeans. A nice brown purse is slung over her right shoulder. She looks to be in her indeterminate twenties, sports a few spots on her pale cheeks, and has brown eyes that are devoid of warmth, let alone life.
Amos silently nods his head in greeting and lets her step inside.
Her oval-shaped face is expressionless. Her (somewhat) frizzy hair looks like it has been recently straightened. Her lips are as dry as a desert.
Amos claps her on the back and pulls her in for a side-hug, "Allie! Long time, no see, huh?"
Allison Moretta (or Morenstein) is also a Bnei Hashem. Like Amos, she vehemently denies her Banu Haqim heritage, instead favoring the made-up Jewish supremacist bullshit. She hails from Israel and moved to the States not that long ago (at least in Kindred terms).

She casually sits down on a couch and drapes a leg over the armrest, "And even if they did, they would not be able to tie it back to us." Her voice is somewhat monotone- she speaks without any real enthusiasm or joy. Her accent is plain and neutral.

Amos corrects her, "It's not merely an operation, it's a sacred task, Allie. They're obstacles who are in our way. Removing them is the most natural thing to do. And we're doing it pretty damn well, aren't we?"

Allison shrugs, "It's for useless twenty-somethings to come, gawk, ogle, and stare at my works like the sheep that they are. Given how abstract it all is, they'll assign whatever meanings they will, most of which will be wrong. As is usually the case."

Allison and Amos have been responsible for committing numerous grisly murders all over this Domain. Their main targets are port authorities, dockworkers, supervisors, cargo handlers, vehicle operators, office accountants, clerks...even managers.

The False Assamite is out to kill a port supervisor tonight. He elects to kill two birds with one stone by draining the poor person dry rather than resorting to his usual method of stabbing and slashing.

The mortal helplessly flails his arms about until they eventually drop to the ground with an unceremonious thump. His feet jerk around spasmodically a few more times and then go still.
Blood coats Amos's chin. He wildly screeches into the dark sky like an animal that just caught its prey. He ruthlessly bashes in his victim's face, just for the hell of it. Pummeling punch after pummeling punch turns that poor man's face into a mushy, incomprehensible mess.

Amos gets off the dead body, flicks some gory bits off of his own clothing, flexes his biceps, and saunters away from the grisly scene of violence that he's just created.
Bastard.

Tyler's uncle. And Tina's husband. He in his early fifties, has salt and pepper hair, and a double chin. He is in good health, however.
