His voice breaks and he struggles to fight the tears back. He finishes the remainder of his beer in one big swig and slams the empty mug back down onto the table.
The young lady seated next to him looks...unsure of everything. Discomfort is writ all over her face. She nervously glances at Amos, gives him a weak smile, and continues looking around.
Amos nods at her, subtly shoos her off, and clears his throat, "Erm...I couldn't help but overhear some things that you were saying. About your nephew..."
Tina's husband turns his head towards Amos, "Oh, r--right. Y--you are?"
Amos smiles and gives a fake name (one of many), "John Rollins." He even puts on a slightly (but not overly so) fake Southern accent.
The somewhat inebriated mortal introduces himself, "S--Samuel. What do you do, Mr. Rollins?"
"I'm a reporter with Vice." Another lie delivered with practiced ease.
Samuel stares at his empty mug and then looks at the vampire. The latter gets what he's hinting at- he snaps his fingers and orders a mug of beer.
The False Assamite inquires, "I was just wondering, Samuel, about your nephew. Um...what happened to him?"
Samuel skeptically glares at Amos, "What's it to you?"
"Like I said: I'm with Vice Media. Your story sounds like it might be up my alley."
The bartender gives Samuel his mug of booze and the latter takes a long swig from it. Amos waits for his reply. Patiently. His dead eyes intensely study the mortal's features with great "care".
Samuel finally responds, "What's it to you? Never heard of this...Vice before. But I'm not smart 'un. Not even by a longshot. Why are you so interested? Do you...even know the first thing about...about my nephew?"
Amos confidently answers, "I know that fourteen-year-old Tyler Woodward was killed in a hit-and-run."
Samuel's lower lip quivers. He nods and sniffles, "Th--that's correct, sir. Sumbitch who did it still hasn't been found. That's why his dad- Jake- hired some Asian PI to get to the bottom of it all."
"Interesting. Now, I'm certain that your story is right up my alley. Why don't we continue this conversation elsewhere, hmm?"
"Why?"
Amos can feel the gears turning in his head, "We got a studio nearby. We could get up setup with a mic, blur your face- if you'd like, and get you to tell your story. At Vice, we prefer to do things authentically, raw, and unfiltered. We want to hear YOU speak for YOURSELF. No agendas, no spin, no fancy edits, hell...no makeup or filters. Just you and a mic. What do you say, Samuel?"
Samuel takes another sip of his beer, "Will it--it help find the bastard who--who ran my nephew over and left him to die like...like some sorta garbage? Will it bring justice along?"
Amos figures that this grief-stricken man can't really see much of anything and his judgement is severely clouded by grief, anger, and booze. He flatly answers, "It'll be international news in no time."
Samuel wipes away his tears, "Well then. Y--you got a studio around here? Do I have to sign forms? I--I got a motorbike. My wife doesn't like me riding around in it, but I gotta go places somehow."
"Don't worry, I have my own set of wheels. Besides, look at you, you're in no state to drive."
"Wait, wait."
Amos stifles a groan, "What is it now? C'mon, the studio's a few blocks away. We'll get you in front of a camera and let you tell your own story--"
Samuel slurs his words together, "Hold on, hold on. You're a reporter. Where--where were you said you with again?"
"Vice. Vice Media Group LLC. John Rollins. I cover human interest stories pertaining to the soft white underbelly of America's often overlooked and undervalued communities."
Samuel's eyes light up, "Ooh. Big words, huh...Roll...ins."
Amos mutters, "You're gonna need to let the booze wear off. C'mon, let's get--"
"No, no, wait...wait. Another question."
"Yep?"
"How legit are you Vice people?"
"We're different. We do things differently, authentically, and raw. Just ask around on social media, buddy."
"I--I don't...none--none of that."
Amos impatiently pinches the bridge of his nose, "We're a reputable outfit, sir. That's all I can tell you. If you wanna stay here and drown your sorrows in beer, then hey, be my guest. But I'm givin' you the opportunity of a lifetime here, pal. Tell me a little more about this nephew of yours."
Samuel slides his mug across the table, "Smart kid, like...really smart. Came here with his parents to study in one of them special schools. Not that kinda special, mind you. But at a--a school where his wavelength is...is tuned into theirs. He was walking back home from extra classes when--when--"
Amos completes the man's sentence for him, "--when the hit-and-run occurred. Yes, I know. I read the police report, sir. I am so very sorry for your loss." Cynical insincerity laces every word of that.
Samuel nods his head and wrings his hands together, "Yeah. Sometime later, Tyler's mother- my sister- commits suicide. The weight of it was too much to--to bear for her. Now, Jake's...all alone. His family gone, torn apart..."
The False Assamite looks on indifferently, "All the more reason for you to come with me and let the rest of America know about this tragedy. I take it that the cops haven't been very helpful?"
Samuel shakes his head, "No, sir, they have not. I don't cuss often, so when I say fuck the Magnolia City Police Department, I do mean...fuck the MCPD."
Amos chuckles at that, "Wow. Heh. I'm inclined to agree, buddy. So, what say you? Sobered up and sober enough to get in front of a camera?"
"I'd prefer to keep my face and--and name outta this. Jake wouldn't appreciate me going behind his back like this. Ty was my nephew, but he was Jake's boy."
"Of course. But he'll thank you for getting the world's attention focused on what happened to his boy. We can...blur your face out, distort your voice a bit, if that'll make you feel better."
"I would very much appreciate that, Mr. Rollins."
"Call me John."
Samuel groans while adjusting his position on the stool, "I should probably get a cab or an Uber to bring me to this studio of yours. Oh, I should also call my Tina and tell her that I'll be runnin' late."
Amos reaches out and grips the mortal's wrist, "Oh, there's really no need for that, Samuel. It'll all be over even before you know it. Trust me. We'll go in my car. Once, I'm done, I'll drop you off wherever you wanna be. How's that sound, hmm?"
Samuel scoops up some peanuts from a bowl and deposits them into his open mouth. The munching-crunching sounds echo within the bar.
Amos keeps calm and hopes that he can pull this off without too many snags, complications, or (overly) messy disasters.
Samuel belches, excuses himself, and grunts while getting off the stool. His bum leg gives out under the weight of his body. Amos swiftly catches him and steadies him.
"Whoa, whoa, whoa. C'mon. How you even ride a bike is anybody's guess. I got you. I got you."
It really was all over even before poor Samuel knew it. Amos keeps a nasty hunting knife in the back of his sedan just in case. The stabbed man was...questioned. He, unfortunately, divulged the names of the individual who was hired to look into Tyler's murder.
Lena Adams, Private Investigator for the public good.
This does not bode well for her at all. Or those close to her, for that matter.
Amos then drove to a viaduct, got out of the car, cut up pieces of Samuel and started scattering the remains into the murky waters beneath. The mortal was already dead by the time his limbs and head got detached from his mangled torso.
(He made sure to drive well under the speed limit so as not to incur any unwanted attention. How very..."considerate" of him).
A few hours later...
Amos and Allison have reconvened at the former's Haven. Amos gets out of the shower with a towel wrapped around his waist. His chiseled abs are in full view. The two monsters soon get down to business.
(And there is quite A LOT to discuss, namely, this most recent turn of events. This...troubling turns of events, if anything).
Allie smugly relaxes on the sofa and hums, "They always scream, cry, beg, and plead. But it all melds into music for my ears, Amos. Music all the same, really. I mean, when you think about it...what is music except people- emotional ones- screaming and making random noises and sounds, hmm?"
Allison Moretta/Morenstein is the quintessential Israeli- as shallow as a kiddie pool and as bloodthirsty as Hitler and Mussolini combined.
Amos picks up a handgrip strengthener and starts working his fingers. He blithely comments, "Mm-hmm. We got ourselves a bit of a fucking problem. Anyway, how did your part of the night go?
Allison dreamily reminisces, "Well, I had the chance to kill, Amos. The looks on those faces...ah, it is something quite wonderful to behold. I love the terror- it makes the blood tastier, you know? The screeching flavors the blood."
Amos keeps working his fingers, "Hm. Sometimes, I ask myself if all this fakakta is worth it, but then I remember what I truly am, what WE truly are."
"Vampires. Kindred. Cainites."
Amos growls a little, "Hmm. You see, Allie, most people never have to face the fact that at the right time and right place, they're capable of anything. Any-fucking-thing. Just look at what be both achieved in a single night. Hell, by a stroke of dumb luck, I learned that we've got someone sniffing around and poking her nose where it doesn't belong."
Allison bites her lower lip, "Really? Ooh, do tell. Another one for us to...remove from this plane of existence? Who is she, anyway?"
Amos derisively answers, "Some Asian shiksa who happens to be a PI. I'm looking more into her, but I don't like what I've heard so far."
Allie prompts him, "Which is...?"
"Jesus Christ, Allie, I just got outta the shower. You try getting dead yeehaw hillbilly bits outta your hair, face, and everywhere else. He sang like a bird before he died, too."
Allie giggles, "I do hope he was as sweet as one."
Amos dismissively waves his free hand, "Ah, it's done. So, here's what I do know about this nosy bitch...but I'll find more out in due time, trust me. For starters, her name's Lena Adams. She moonlights as a Private Eye..."
By the end of it all, Amos makes sure to remind his fellow Bnei Hashem to be cautious, keep their eyes peeled, and ALWAYS keep him in the loop about every single thing they might learn about this Lena Adams chick.
But most of them are still quite busy waging their shadowy and secretive war against the ACTUAL Banu Haqim in this Domain. The true Assamites are slowly being peeled off or are sent packing. Till now, the Assamite Primogen has not been seen or heard from for weeks on end.
It's not known whether he is hiding out, locked away in an underground bunker, fell into Torpor, or has simply been slain by the False ones. Maybe he was Beckoned away?
The last person to see him was Dr. George Fateh, but he remains very tight-lipped about his whereabouts (and a whole lotta other things, too).
In time, Amos and Allison part ways. Sunrise is imminent, so Amos sets about to making the necessary preparations for his day-long slumber.
There will be plenty of time to get back to "business" once the new night arrives. And, hopefully, he'll be able to find a way to deal with the PI before she finds herself in too deep...with these dangerous waters that she's slowly being pulled into.
While Amos snuggles himself into bed, a shudder passes through his muscular body. He involuntarily spasms and grunts. The consequences of his rampant Diablerie have begun to make themselves known down to his very essence. Memories and experiences not belonging to him violently flash by before his mind's eye.
He clenches his jaws so hard that they hurt. Droplets of Blood trickle out of his nostrils. The coppery smell and taste of the stuff linger inside his mouth. The blackened veins on his forehead bulge and pulsate with a sickeningly malignant aura.
Distant memories of his own past flash through his consciousness. Memories that he'd long suppressed and pushed aside to make room for the "baggage" that he's acquired over the long decades.
His lips quiver, "R--Rivkah. No...no! NO!!!! NOOOOOO!"
Amos's claws pop out. His fangs elongate and he springs up from bed. Then, as abruptly as this nightmarish experience began, it ends. His wife's face fades from the forefront of his mind.
He has a dull, throbbing headache. He holds the side of his head and lightly groans. After a moment or two, he lies back down and pulls the sheets up. Within seconds of the approaching sunrise, he and all the other Kindred in this Domain are fast asleep.
Well, such is the nightly Unlife of two monstrous Zionists. It's no surprise (or shocker), then, that a vast majority (not all) of the world's Jews support the ethnostate of Israel and all its "glorious" actions. And the same could be said for a disturbingly large amount of Jewish Orgs out there.
The Zionists' long history of violence and terrorism against their neighbors and the Palestinian people in particular is well-documented for those humane and curious enough to give it a good, honest, and long look.
And it's no surprise that Israel itself, in the final analysis, is a nation of psychopaths. But there is a reason for all of this. People aren't born that way- monsters aren't born, they are made.
They are cultivated by powers and forces that are far beyond them. Cultivated for a variety of perverse purposes. Powers and forces that seek to prey upon the weak, the desperate, and the broken.
Powers and forces that are...insatiable in their conquest for raw materials, spheres of geostrategic influence, and abundant pools of cheap labor.
Powers and forces that are in a state of desperation and panic. They know what is coming for them- what is on the horizon- and they need more monsters in order to offset their inevitable collapse and doom.
That power has many names, many bases of operations, and an ultimate purpose. Washington DC, Canberra, Berlin, Ottawa, London, Madrid, Paris, New Delhi, Wellington, etcetera. These and many others stand to materially benefit from the status quo and thus seek to maintain it.
Israel is, if nothing else, imperialism's most faithful attack dog in the Middle East. Hence the need to cultivate a population of utterly psychotic savages and sadistic deviants. They will be needed in the years, months, and days ahead.
Their "services" to empire will be quite invaluable, to say the very least.
And the False Assamites are merely cogs in this vast machine of war, murder, repression, and backwardness. But the Zionist ethnonationalists are merely a small piece of a larger, far more malignant whole.
Jewish supremacy is merely a byproduct of this larger, far more sinister agenda. A supporting pillar for empire and its diabolical designs.
But there are still brave souls out there fighting- bravely, desperately, or otherwise- to put a stop to all of this and to carve out a path to a better and more dignified world.
A tomorrow without fear, division, hatred, and oppression.
Until then, however, one is left to contend with the true monsters amongst us, amidst our societies, communities, cities, towns, neighborhoods, streets, homes, schools, colleges, workplaces, and lives.
(Or Unlives).
The interregnum, after all, is a time of monsters.
And the False Assamites are merely the tip of the iceberg. They're murdering their way through a specific part of this city for a very specific reason- it's all part of a bigger picture, a larger scheme, if anything.
The rest will be disclosed in due time.
Chapter Six: RBF
The sun begins to rise over Magnolia City. It peeks over the horizon with an orange glow, coating the world beneath its radiance with warm light. The chill and dewiness of night fade away and give way to a (relatively) cool morning.
Lena awakens with a start- that damned infinite drowning/not-drowning dream haunted her again. She wipes the sweat off her forehead and tries to slow her racing heartbeat. She calmly breathes out, rests her tensed body on the mattress, and shuts her eyes.
For a few moments, of course.
Shower is next. But first, Lena checks out her clothing and checks her phone for messages and texts. A few are from Yvonne and she's just checking on her. The rest are work-related stuff, namely, stuff pertaining to her job as a PI.
Some clients wanna know about their respective spouse's (or partner's) activities, whereabouts, movements, and so forth.
Lena sighs. Her fingers glide across the virtual keyboard as she sends text after text to each and every curious (antsy) client who needs them answers.
The warm water brings a serene calmness to Lena's mind and body. She makes sure to use a nifty face wash that Yvonne absolutely swears by. Afterwards, Lena emerges from the shower looking (and smelling) fresher than ever.
She slips into her work clothes with a practiced ease, applies a bit of makeup, grabs her purse, locks the door, and shuffles outta her house in the 'burbs!
First stop?
GROUND BEANZ!
Like always, it is full at this time of the day. The Wednesday morning crowd is an eclectic mix of young hipsters, suburban moms whose SUVs are getting obnoxiously bigger (and nastier) with each passing year, baggy-eyed nightshift drones looking to stay awake long enough to get home, and some buskers preparing for the long day ahead.
Lena lowers her head while queuing up behind two geriatrics who are taking their time deciding on what to get. She takes a peek at her phone, skims through various news articles (with heavy skepticism), and then slips it back into the pocket of her duffel coat.
The baristas with aching feet and (slightly) hunched backs go about their usual routine with an efficiency born out of years of experience. If only their pay reflected that more accurately.
Lena, being the coffee fiend that she is, orders a double espresso and some ginger biscuits- those two people in front of her took quite a while, so she'll have to treat the biscuits as breakfast. She also texts Yvonne while patiently waiting for her stuff.
Yvonne texts back: "U looked gud in that dress, gurl. How was the date, anyways?? Deets, gurl, deets."
Lena chuckles faintly: "I don't see it quite working out. She's nice and all, but...ehh. Got too much baggage, to put it kindly."
Yvonne texts back with lotsa emojis: "Baggage? Hmmmm. Send her my way, then :-)"
Lena snickers: "Hm. Sure. But she wants to remain friends. just friends."
"We also started out as 'just friends' :-P and now look at us!"
"That's different, lol. How's life? BTW, we should catch up. U free 4 lunch 2day???"
Yvonne replies with a bunch of crying face emojis: "I fucking wish, gurl. Gotta pull off dat overtime. u know how it is. Retail's a bitch sometimes, but we should meet...and maybe..."
Lena's eyebrows go up at that. She can't help but giggle: "And maybe...?"
Yvonne texts back: "its a bit 2 early for a tease :-P. but u know wat i mean, rite? Anyways, gotta go now. If i had to take a guess, ur still waiting for your coffee. gurl, you got issues when it comes 2 caffeine."
Lena shakes her head: "Hah! I probably do. bye."
The PI pays for her stuff, nay a cent more in the form of tips and chomps down her on her ginger biscuits while washing them down with espresso. The morning sun paints a very picturesque sight.
Buildings of glass and steel reflect the glorious light from above. Streetcars chug along their tracks with mechanical precision. The ground beneath Lena's pumps rumbles softly as a metro train passes by. Pedestrians are either busy chatting away or talking on their phones.
Crystal Street Station looks as bustling as ever. It's business as usual here. People in suits flock to and fro the underground. The surrounding restaurants and fast-food joints are all crammed full of customers. There's a long line in front of a kiosk selling newspapers and magazines.
A nearby billboard promotes a new energy drink, of course. It's the most cookie-cutter neon sign imaginable- photoshopped white woman smiling eerily while "holding" a can of whatever that heart-exploding sludge is.
Ads flash on numerous screens positioned strategically around the subway station. Like all public transit hubs in this Domain, Crystal Street Station features countless small shops and kiosks (for commuters) who are either buying stuff or otherwise milling about.
Lena patiently waits for her train. The caffeine and ginger make her feel rejuvenated, but she's already getting the tingles. She ALREADY wants another cuppa joe.
A small one.
She shivers in place, licks her lips, and fidgets with her hands. To keep her mind off that itch, Lena decides to mentally go over her ongoing cases. The PI leans against a non-grimy portion of the wall and looks up at the ceiling.
Lena lowers her sunglasses and stares at the ceiling for a few moments. Her amber eyes slowly move from left to right as if she is reading something on the tiles above.
The choo-choo train soon comes to a halt. Lena dutifully waits on the side as the doors hiss open. The more impatient folks and the slow walkers hustle on out. She gives 'em a wide berth, keeping her head lowered.
At last, it is time to board! She holds onto a hanging strap and decides to call her mom up.
Susie answers, but there is a certain tiredness in her voice, "Mmm-hmm?"
"Hi, mom...you--are you OK? I can always call back later--"
Susie yawns and interjects, "No, no, it's fine, sweetie. It's fine. Yoshi's just resting his...eyes."
Lena concernedly asks, "How is dad really doing?"
"Oh, y'know. He's...hanging in there."
"Mom..."
Susie lets out a sigh of resignation, "OK, OK, he's...not doing too good, Lena. But listen, I don't want you getting all worried, OK? I can take care of my husband just fine. You go, live your life, meet someone who'll make you happy, and let me deal with things back home, OK?"
Lena sniffs and mumbles, "It doesn't have to be like this, mom. What's stopping me f--from moving back in with you two, huh?"
Susie points out, "Because this is America, sweetie. Living with your parents is...kinda frowned upon when you're at that age. Don't worry about us. Me and your dad are handling it."
Lena grits her teeth, "That's bullshit! I--I have no issues with living with you two; neither does dad!" Given how she raised her voice a little, some of the passengers give her varying looks from quirked eyebrows to sympathetic grimaces.
The PI, sensing the unwanted attention, takes a deep breath and continues in a much softer tone, "OK. S--sorry. I shouldn't have raised my voice at you, mom. I'm sorry. But the point is that I have absolutely NO issue living with you. Hell, I'm even ready to bring the both of you over here!"
Susie immediately dismisses the idea, "No. I--I dunno about that, sweetie. True, your dad would've liked it and all, but I feel that it might just affect your chances...at everything."
Mom chuckles and goes on, "Like imagine you wanted to bring somebody home and there your dad was, snoring on the couch. That'd be kinda awkward. Or wait, wait! What if the interviewer asked you about your living uh...arrangements? 'I live with my parents' is hardly gonna impress them, sweetie."
Lena rolls her eyes, "You're worried that the two of you would, what, slow me down? Or worse yet, jeopardize my future?"
The awkward silence on the other end of the line is quite heartbreaking. Lena does her best to avoid giving away her emotions and feelings, but it's quite difficult in the face of this...rabidly individualistic American society where adults living with their parents is viciously looked down upon.
Lena sardonically says, "No need to answer, mom. I--I get it. But I'm still sending some money over. And so is Roland."
Susie gratefully responds, "And we are grateful for it, sweetie. What you and your brother do for us is--is--"
Her voice breaks and she starts to gently sob. Lena bites down on her lip and waits for her mom to calm down.
Given that the gumshoe is almost nearing her destination, she hastily says, "Listen, mom. I--I gotta go. Take care, OK? And if things get...really bad, you can always call me up. Me and Roland are there for you- both of you. Just like you were there for us when we were little. Now, it's our turn to take care of you."
Susie snorts indignantly, "Aw, c'mon, we're not THAT old, y'know."
Lena briefly chuckles, says goodbye to her mother, and ends the call. It'll be quite a few more minutes before her station arrives. She wipes the tears away from her face and sniffs again.
Work is...work. The early hours of the early morning zoom by in the blurriest of blurs. Lena dutifully carries out the tasks that keep a roof over head and some cash in her pocket.
When she has the chance to do so, she routinely checks in with clients (PI clients, that is) via phone or text, schedules appointments with them (and others), updates them on various aspects of their respective cases, and otherwise stays on top of things.
(There is yet another clause in Lena's contract which unambiguously stipulates that engaging in any second job or side activities while on the clock are strictly forbidden).
But, eh, whatevs. Fuck that. Lena can literally see a co-worker of hers who is playing Counter-Strike (one of the older versions that runs on older machines) while giggling like a goblin coming upon a ton of shiny, shiny loot.
After what feels like an eternity of filing, pointless busywork, and even more spreadsheets, it is now time for lunch! Lena tosses her empty coffee cup into the bin, fixes her ponytail, and heads downstairs to see what she can fill her belly with.
After all, a hardworking gal like her needs her food. And her coffee.
ESPECIALLY HER COFFEE.
Miss Adams takes her scran at an open-air joint. It's more crowded than usual, but that just means that the food's good. Usually. She opts for a plate of extra-cheesy nachos and some churros. Hmm, yummy!
While eating, her facial muscles are as relaxed as they could possibly be. She rubs the side of her neck, slurps on her lemonade, and blandly looks around her while slowly chewing on her food.
Lena nods at passersby in the most cursory manner imaginable. And she does it because, well, it's just polite, innit?
Even then, Lena Akira has practically mastered her RBF (Resting Bitch Face) down to a tee- that's just how things are for her.
(It's worth noting that, in her younger years, she had an angsty emo phase that lasted for exactly five days).
Lena grunts out a chuckle when that memory resurfaces- it causes her to cringe a little bit.
Black makeup, stuck-out tongue, copious amounts of eyeliner ("borrowed" from the school's most popular girl), an attempt at a belly piercing (mom DID NOT like that), super-loud music at night (dad DID NOT like that), and shredding on the guitar (Roland laughed at that).
While still maintaining her RBF, Lena finishes up her food, collects her stuff, and walks out without leaving a tip. She already paid for her food and all, but tipping is just not something that she can, in good faith, do.
Lena cracks a smile for the first time that day. A small smile. As she heads back up to the second floor, her phone vibrates in the pocket of her duffel coat. She pulls it out and clearly sees the contact's name: "Toby Fetscher".
She sighs and answers it, "Make it quick, Toby. I'm getting back to work."
Toby is a little taken aback by his friend's tone of voice, "Um...wow. Not exactly how I'd greet a close friend, but OK. You do you, I guess. I uh...called because I'm worried about Eddie. You know, Eddie--"
Lena flatly responds, "I know who our friend is. Anyway, what's got you so concerned about him?"
Toby's cheeks puff out when he exhales loudly into the phone. He takes a second or two to collect his thoughts before going on, "Yeah, he's been...acting shifty lately. He said he was off to visit someone. Who? He--he wouldn't say. He's hiding something from us, Lena. I just know it."
"Maybe it's nothing. We already had this--"
Toby clicks his tongue, "C'mon, Lena don't be like this...all dismissive and--and catty and smart-mouthed all the time. Eddie's our friend. I mean, c'mon, we've got lunch planned for tomorrow. And you're, well, invited to join us. Anyways, back to Eddie. Are you getting my drift or what?"
"'Catty,' huh? Hmph. Never mind. Eddie has been...showing off. I scrolled through his Facebook profile and saw a whole bunch of pictures of him showing off."
"Heh, yeah, I took some of those for him. He still won't tell me how he suddenly came across all that cash, though. It's weird. And I--I'm worried. What if he got in w--with a bad crowd or--or--"
Lena finds it pretty odd that her friend is stuttering so much and being so paranoid, "Toby. Stop. Relax. Eddie's a big boy- he can take care of himself just fine, right? Besides, you work with him day in and day out. How is he when you're both at the garage?"
Toby shrugs, "He's...fine, I guess. He--he said that he wants to pay for tomorrow's lunch. And he wants to take us both to that fancy new place downtown. Said it'd be a treat for us."
"Wow. Um...OK. Great."
Toby makes a few grunting noises in his throat. He shuffles on his seat and grunts some more.
Lena frowns and asks, "Whoa, what's going on? You sound like you're in pain."
"Nah, just my old chest binder giving me some issues- y'know how it is. I've had it for--for quite a while now. We've been through a--a lot together. Look, regarding my previous comment about you being catty, I dunno if you intentionally mean it or not, but sometimes, you do sound kinda--"
The PI interjects, "Toby...let's not go there right now. I have work to do."
"Hmm. Fine. But we're not done having this conversation, Lena. Eddie's my friend- he's yours, too- and I just wanna make sure that everything's OK with him."
Lena reassures him, "Everything IS fine with him, Toby. But just in case it isn't, we can check on him later."
Toby chuckles, "'We'?"
"Who's being catty now, huh? Listen, I gotta go now. Take care, Toby. See you and Ed at lunch tomorrow."
"Mm-hmm. Bye!! And...just for the record, I wasn't gonna call you bitchy. But you can be kinda...dismissively cold and off-putting sometimes, y'know?"
Lena dully responds, "Never said I was perfect. Have a great afternoon."
After hanging up, Lena looks up at the ceiling and yawns a little.
She helps herself to a cup of coffee and gets busy on the remainder of her work. Her fellow Pluto Enterprises employees either work, watch the clocks (conspicuously), gossip by the watercoolers (inconspicuously) or otherwise sleep (and then get woken up by a grumpy co-worker who felt it their sworn duty to do so).
Lena- who is fueled by caffeine- powers through her work till the time's up. By four forty-four, she springs up from her seat, gets her erratic breathing under control, grabs her stuff, and prepares to head straight for her PI office.
There's work that needs to be done; there are people who need to be followed around!
Gary the bossman watches her saunter away with those oddly weary eyes of his. Lena shivers and slowly turns her head to meet his gaze. He musters up a smile, weakly waves, nods, and then goes back to looking through his iPad.
The afternoon sun beats down upon Lena's sunglasses-clad face. Her shades protect her from the glare coming from the vehicle's windows and other surfaces. Ms. Adams goes to her PI office and begins to prepare herself for the (long) evening ahead.
Lena checks that her DSLR camera is working perfectly and that the lenses are in tip-top shape. She also ensures that there's a fresh memory card inserted and that the batteries are charged up.
If she's lucky, it'll be an easy night- just follow her mark, get "the goods" on them (see if they're cheating or not), and then she can go home. If not, she'll probably have to hide herself in some bushes and wait a REALLY LONG time before taking those all-important (and compromising) shots.
Lena unzips her duffel coat, slings it over her chair, and leans against the desk while absentmindedly fidgeting with her hands. She paces around for a few moments, fetches herself a cuppa joe (or five), and settles back down into her seat with a long and drawn-out sigh.
The sun begins the unceremonious process of setting. For a moment, the city's landscapes and skylines are painted with beautiful shades of red, orange, and pink. Picturesque is the only way to describe it.
This is Magnolia City- sturdy brownstone buildings, a handful of stable brutalist structures, and a ton of magnolias.
The denizens of this Domain go about their lives. Throngs of people head home from work. The buses and subway trains are loaded full of tired people who just want to get home already, throw their (stinky) feet up on the couch, and unwind.
The sun soon fades away- entirely. Now is the time for darkness to engulf everything, to reign supreme, as it were. Once that great and terrible star of fire has completely retreated, nighttime firmly sets in.
And the Kindred begin to stir awake.
Lena counts down the seconds while shaking her leg up and down. She'll head out once it's a bit darker- she can't really be seen stalking people out in broad daylight now, can she?
(But she still has to keep the artificial light in mind. Then again, she didn't get to where she is by being careless and reckless).
In the meanwhile, the PI keeps herself occupied by scrolling through Facebook, liking some stuff from her friends (close, mutual, or otherwise).
Elsewhere...
Amos stirs from his day-sleep. He immediately hits his home gym and viciously begins pumping iron like there's no tomorrow. He pays no heed to the outside world, simply focusing on honing his physical body into the ideal- the peak- of Jewish masculinity (or so he thinks).
Minutes fly by as he steadily crushes more and more reps with each passing moment. It's no coincidence that he has a picture of Eli Roth (a thoroughly repulsive Zionist cretin) depicting the character of the "Bear Jew" from 2009's substance-less "Inglourious Basterds".
Amos mentally cheers himself on as he punches and kicks the shit out of his punching bag. Earphones are shoved deep into his ears as he listens to an avalanche of hardcore gym-adjacent music that even gets his dead heart to act up a bit.
Amos begins doing all manner of push-ups, burpees, and whatnot. He can practically hear the synths and drums as he increases his workout pace.
He howls with delight when he goes for a quick-draw on an imaginary opponent- unsheathing an invisible swipe of his claws and then following it up with a swift pounce that culminates in him gouging out said foe's eyes and pulling their innards out.
The False Assamite screeches into the air like a wild animal, smashes his fists on the floor, and starts sparring with an invisible enemy once again. He dodges left, right, ducks down, and sweeps the "opponent's" legs from under them.
As a finisher move, he rapidly punches their "face" in while knowingly smirking at the picture of Eli Roth/the Bear Jew. Amos finishes his intense workout by once again counting his reps and doing some weight-lifting.
When he pulls out one of his earphones, the sound of his bell being incessantly rung soon greets him. Amos sneers and glances at the clock. It's a little past seven PM.
Muscle man wipes the sweat-blood from his face and goes to check who it is. He clicks his tongue in frustration when he looks through the peephole and sees a familiar person standing on his doorstep.
Amos lets his visitor in and grumbles, "Evening, Avi. What do you want now?"
"I was out here for nearly ten whole minutes--" Avi indignantly huffs while storming into the vampire's Haven, "--and I rang your bell a dozen times! How'd you not hear me, huh?"
Amos dryly says, "I was working out."
Avi Shipman is quite old and short-ish. He has a bad tan, thinning, graying hair, and he talks with a slight Brooklyn accent. He is an Israeli diplomat posted at the Israeli consulate.
The mortal adjusts the kippah on his head, tugs on the lapels of his dark suit and takes a deep breath before going on, "We--we need to talk. Something's...come up." He sounds quite winded as if he ran up several flights of stairs to get here.
Amos plainly gestures towards the living room, "Sit down, take a deep breath, and we'll talk. What's wrong now, huh? I thought that you already took care of it?"
Avi sits down and buries his wrinkly face in his hands. He lifts his head up and blinks. His gray eyes are glassy and unfocused. He breathes in through his nose and responds, "I tried my best. But it's starting to bite me in the tuchus."
Amos sits adjacent to the mortal and regards him with some interest, "I'm listening. Talk to me, Avi. Remember, we're in this together. I can't have you bailing out on me. Not when I'm so fucking close to finishing my business here."
Avi nervously laughs, "Erm...that's gonna have--have to wait."
(The diplomat is a mere mortal aiding Amos- and the Bnei Hashem- in their...shared goals, so to speak. Avi specializes in trade, cultural promotions, visas, and documents. The last two are mainly for the Bnei Hashem Kindred lingering in this Domain like a cancer).
Avi wrings his clammy hands together, "Y--you mind getting me some water? Or something stronger?" He has an expectant look on his face.
The False Assamite shrugs and goes to get his guest a glass of water. Avi hastily gulps it down in one go, with some of the H2O spilling down the sides of his mouth and onto his suit.
Amos grimaces at this, "Whoa, what's gotten into you all of a sudden, huh? I thought that we'd already dealt with this, put this whole thing behind us. By now, I was sincerely hoping that it'd be in our rearview mirror- our work here is important and this little...mishap has already set us back enough."
Avi sighs heavily and pinches the bridge of his nose, "It's about that, yeah. I--I need your help."
Amos shakes his head and lightly scoffs, "You slapped my hand away the first time I offered. What changed, hmm?" A certain smugness laces his words.
The diplomat shakes his head back at him, "Sneer all you want, Amos. But we--we need each other. And right now, I--I need your uh...expertise. You and that girlfriend of yours."
Amos chuckles derisively, "Allison isn't my girlfriend, hahahaha! Don't ever let her hear you say that. Ah, shit, Avi...that got a good laugh outta me." He rubs his eyes a little.
Avi is getting all jittery and agitated again. His fingers keep tapping on his thighs and he keeps nervously looking around the room.
Amos not-so-gently clears his throat.
The mortal diplomat takes a deep breath, "I have a problem with this mechanic. After the...the...y'know, I panicked. Who wouldn't, right? So, I uh...drove around and drove some more until I came across this garage that was still open. Some Hispanic guy was there. I had to fork out quite a bit of...this." He rubs his thumb across his middle and index fingers.
"Huh. Go on."
"His name's...Eddie Rivera-Torres. The guy who fixed up my car after--goddammit, I can't even bring myself to--to say it."
Amos plainly says it for him, "After you flattened that hillbilly kid and fled the scene of the crime."
Avi averts his gaze, "Hmph. Yeah. After...that. Eddie fixed my ride up really good. He asked questions about the dent and the blood, but I told him that I uh...hit an animal and that my eyesight's been shit- old man troubles. He bought it. I think. But he asked for a lot more money."
"Interesting. Apart from Eddie the mechanic, does anybody else know of your little indiscretion?"
"Nobody. I swear. Well--well, apart from you and Allison, of course. What the fuck else was I supposed to do, huh?"
"Your diplomatic immunity is, in theory, supposed to protect you from any kind of legal troubles, right?"
"Yeah, but...they'll bray for my fucking blood like donkeys, Amos!"
The vampire concedes with a sharp nod, "True. Our people don't need any more blood libels- too much of that shit in the past. Not anymore. Never again. I'm gonna ask you again and this time, I want a straight answer. Apart from Eddie, does ANYONE else know? Was there anybody else in the garage?"
Avi shakes his head, "He was alone when I pulled in. My heart was--was pounding, but I had to act cool, y'know? So, I paid the money and then left. But he's...asking for more. I dunno if he put the pieces together or what."
Amos balls his hand into a fist and nods again, "And now you want me to take care of it, right?"
Avi shakily breathes out, "Y--you or Allison."
"In these past few weeks, how much have you been paying to buy that mechanic's silence?"
"..."
Amos purses his lips, "Hm. Fair enough."
Avi grumbles, "Eddie is--isn't blackmailing me. No. He's treating me like his fucking ATM. I agreed to pay the fucker in--in installments. I don't know what I was thinking, but after...wh--what happened, I completely zoned out and let myself go on autopilot for a bit."
"I can imagine. Now, you wish for a more...permanent solution. I'm gonna ask you again, Avi...were there any other witnesses? Did anyone see what you did?"
The diplomat locks eyes with Amos and says, "N--no. I swear. I swear on everything that is good and right. I swear on my own fuckin' bubbe's grave, if it'd help--"
Amos licks his lips and makes an encouraging gesture with his hands, "Alright, then. Calm down. I've taken care of you thus far, haven't I? Us Jews, we have to stick together no matter what. We have to take good care of each other- nobody else is gonna do it for us."
Avi hurriedly nods, "I know, I know. I need you to get rid of my problem, Amos. Before this mechanic gets wise and puts the pieces together. So far, he doesn't seem any wiser than a schnauzer. But..."
Amos grimly muses, "With each and every passing night that he continues to draw breath, he could very well be putting the pieces together; we can't allow that to happen. Gimme his address."
Avi gulps, "Right now, he'd be at his garage. All alone. I passed by it on my way here. He works really late. I--I dunno what you're gonna do, but I don't want my name- let alone my involvement- anywhere near what's gonna go down next. I know all too well what happens."
"Hm. You are absolutely right to be cautious and I'm glad that you're smart enough to already begin distancing yourself. We've come this far Avi. What happened with Tyler whatever-his-last-name-was was...unfortunate. But this is war. That kid, in the grand scheme of things, barely registers as collateral damage- just another unremarkable goy."
Avi nods along, "Yeah, just--just another goy. Plenty of 'em to go around."
Amos waggles his finger at the mortal, "If it were under different circumstances, I'd kill you where you stand for running a kid over and leaving him to die. Luckily for you, I need your help. And, heh, you need mine."
Avi mumbles, "We're in this together."
"That we certainly are, my friend." He tosses a pad and a pen to the mortal and wordlessly instructs him to jot down the garage's address.
Avi's hand trembles as he scribbles it down, but he gets the job done all the same. Amos's dead eyes scan over the address. He lets out a slight grunt, nods to himself, and tosses the pad aside.
The mortal stares at the vampire expectantly.
Amos bluntly says, "Consider it done. I'll head out once you make yourself scarce. Anything else you wanna tell me. Truth be told, I hadn't expected you to fuck up this royally. Instead of wasting money on this mechanic, you should've IMMEDIATELY come to me- we would've had this taken care of MUCH SOONER!"
Avi recoils as those last words are shouted directly at his face. He raises his hands in a defensive posture and squints, "I--I'm sorry. OK?! I wasn't thinking straight! My--my head was spinning. All I was worried about- at that fucking moment- was to get the hell outta there. Not much of a 'diplomatic' move, I'll give you that."
Amos shakes his head and tsks, "Tsk. It's done. Unlike you, I intend to be wholly transparent. You're gonna wanna be relaxed for what I'm about to drop on you next. And no, it's not good news. If anything, it further complicates things. And that's the last goddamn thing that I need right now."
Avi scratches his neck and gives the immortal fiend his undivided attention. He makes a silent gesture with his hands that signifies Amos to continue.
"Hm. A Private Investigator by the name of Lena Adams is...looking into your handiwork. Now, now, don't jump outta your seat just yet. So far, she's not on the trail. And we are gonna keep it that way. I am gonna keep it that way. I want a favor from you- and this is important."
"Shit! Shit! Are--are you sure that she isn't already crawling up my ass?! Should I triple my security--"
Amos glowers at his guest, "Will you please shut the fuck up and listen to me for once in your goddamn life? Geez. She's not onto you just yet. And I have a few plans to stall her--"
Avi blurts out, "Waste her, too!!"
The False Assamite sighs heavily, "It's not that simple, I'm afraid. Hey, maybe you have a point, but let ME and Allison take care of this Lena lady, OK? I want a favor from you. I want you to make some calls to your intelligence pals and get them to give you whatever they have on this PI. I want the works and I know that you can deliver on that front."
Avi rubs the base of his neck, "Th--that shouldn't be too hard. I got some contacts in the Mossad and the CIA. Yeah, sure, I can absolutely get you some dirt on this PI. Hell, it's probably the least I can do after...uh...what happened."
Amos has an oddly serene look on his face as he says, "Good. And I'm also gonna need you to get those visas approved a lot faster. We don't appreciate the slowdowns, Avi. Your people need you."
Avi clicks his tongue, "I was working with some synagogues on organizing some fundraisers for the IDF. That's just as important as whatever you've got going on over here, right?"
Amos equably says, "Of course. Always. Our brave soldiers deserve the best and nothing but the best. Indulge me, how much money did you blow off on this Eddie creature?"
"Not much, honestly. He wanted cash. Didn't do wire transfers. In retrospect, I--I guess that he knew that I was acting kinda squirrelly when I brought my car into his garage."
"Hmm. Why didn't you just keep driving?"
"And how the fuck would I explain the dent and the blood to consulate staff, huh? My diplomatic immunity would protect my tuchus, sure, but if other people got word of it...if the media got their hands on this story, I'd be lynched, feathered, skinned, and tarred by half this goddamn city!"
"Heh. You're not wrong there, Avi. And I know the history of our people well enough to know that the goyim won't just stop with you- it'll turn into a full-scale pogrom."
(Hyperbole much?)
The murderous diplomat lowers his head, "And in these past few months, I've--I've come to think of myself as more than just a humble representative of the state of Israel. Getting to know you, Allison, and what you stand for...it's been enlightening, to say the least. It feels like we have common goals, a common cause."
Amos comments, "We're Jews. We're stuck with each other, no matter how far apart we are. That's why we need Israel. Because when the shit hits the fan- and it will- we'll all have a place to go. We were never meant to be among the goyim for such a long time. Just look where it got us, right?"
Avi shrugs a shoulder, "Eh. Sure, I guess. I can't help myself...what exactly are you gonna do to the mechanic?"
Amos glares at him in a manner that could best be described as withering, "Having second thoughts? I'm not sending Allie in for this one- she makes too much of a mess sometimes. Don't worry, I'm personally taking care of this problem."
"And the PI? What if she gets wise, huh? What if she, somehow, gets the info on you, me, and the rest of this m--messy shitshow?"
"Stop kvetching; it's beneath you. That shiksa won't be poking her nose into our business- not if I can help it. I got some ideas. Now, can we not repeat ourselves over and over again??"
Avi sighs heavily, "It's not just my ass on the line. If--if I go down--"
Amos springs up from his seat, puts his hands on the mortal's shoulders, and interrupts him, "--then you go down alone. Now, quit the negative attitude and grow a fucking pair of balls already. Show some grit, man! It's unbecoming of a fellow Jew to act like this! You remind me of my dead wife, and I DO NOT LIKE TO BE REMINDED OF HER!!"
Avi trembles under the vampire's preternatural grip (and dreadful aura) but manages to choke out, "Right, right. S--sorry!"
Amos shoves him back and sits down once again, "My work here is not yet done. Far from it, in fact."
Avi remarks, "Y--you're a real mensch, aren't you? Y'know, till now, I still don't know what it is you've got planned. I don't know what the endgame is, but it's...in the interests of Israel and--and the Jewish people everywhere, right, right?"
The False Assamite asserts, "You'll see it for yourself once it has been realized. No one will be able to stand in our way once we're done here. We will have freed ourselves from a great burden. But only if we adhere to our roles and don't get ourselves carried away. Keep your wits about you, Avi."
"Hmm. And I--I don't have to tell you the same thing. So, I heard you recently bought this entire building, huh? Not bad. A testament to your...hmm, success and...business acumen."
Amos smirks proudly, "There's a reason why us Jews have such a reputation for being shrewd moneymakers. Heh. I got this and a few other properties scattered all over the Domai--city. Some people still try and goldbrick me when rent's due. Needless to say, I get rid of 'em pretty quickly."
Avi inquires, "Any issues on that front? You need any help with the paperwork?"
Amos states, "I have you on speed dial, old man. Now, go on. Get outta here. You look like you could use some rest. Consider your problem dealt with by the time you go to bed tonight. And sleep easy knowing that we have one less problem to worry about."
"And the other one? I'd sleep like a baby if you'd--"
Amos lifts his hand up, "If she somehow gets wise, then Allie will personally put her in the ground. She's a damn good shot, Avi. She never misses her mark. Like I said, don't worry about it."
The mortal diplomat asks, "Are you coming to shul this week?"
"I'll try."
"We could use a guy like you to inspire the younger generation- strong, successful, not afraid to get his hands dirty if he has to. In these modern times, I think that we need more guys like you."
Amos loves a good ass-kissing as much as the next motherfucker, so he smiles broadly, "I'll think about it. I'm busy. But I still make time- whenever I can- to keep the Sabbath, pray, and honor my family's traditions."
Avi warily glances around the room, "Hmm. We're all on our own journey- spiritual or otherwise. And, regardless of what has happened, it has been an honor to work alongside you. I may not know what the endgame is yet, but I hope that I'll be there when it's finally realized."
Amos calmly reassures him, "Don't worry. If I have to, I'll make sure that you stick around. Until then, do your part, and try to avoid unnecessary fuckups. You don't want us having to clean up after you too much, right?"
Avi fervently nods, "Right, right. How's Allison doing?"
The Bnei Hashem flatly responds, "You know her address, Avi. Why don't you swing by unannounced, hmm? She loves a good surprise and she's an even better host."
"She is very sweet, ain't she? Are you...?"
Amos snorts, "No. Oh, wow. No. Absolutely not. Don't get me wrong, she's a really pretty girl and all, but I'm not attracted to her. Let's just say that...I have other ways of propagating my lineage. So does Allie. She's great for what she does- and what she does, she does well."
Avi smiles lecherously, "Aha! I...see."
Amos shoots him a slightly perturbed look, "What?"
"Never mind. I--I should haul my tush outta here. I got some free time, so I'll work on those documents for all your uh...friends. Sound good?"
"You're a real mensch, Avi Shipman. I'll see you around. Feel free to close the door behind you."
"Cool. See ya!"
The vampire calls after him, "And Avi? When things have been taken care of...you'll know. And you're welcome." A certain menacing undertone laces those last few words.
As soon as the diplomat leaves, Amos puts his face in his hands and groans wearily. He trills his lips and gets himself ready for the macabre night ahead. He puts on a t-shirt, jeans, and work boots.
While heading out, he grabs a large knife. He has that disturbingly determined look etched all over his pale face. And it's not the Hunger of the Beast within that compels him.
This is something else entirely. Something malevolent and twisted.
As he starts up his sedan, he mutters to himself, "Most people never have to face the fact that at the right time and right place, they're capable of anything. Any-fucking-thing."
The engines of the sedan roar to life. Amos exits the musty basement and floors it.
Not even an hour later, Amos strolls into the garage where he knows the mortal mechanic is bound to be alone. Eddie has his welding mask on and is busy working on a small chassis of some kind. The only light sources come from a pair of bare light bulbs that dangle over him.
The garage's interior is quite muggy and grimy. Oily stains are strewn all over the dirty concrete floor. As Amos walks closer, he can see an assortment of old tools (spanners, hammers, wrenches, screwdrivers, you name it) scattered about on the worktable to Eddie's right.
The oil pit looks like it was recently cleaned. The lone mortal is whistling a cheery tune as he goes about his business.
Amos raps his knuckles on the table, causing Eddie to nearly jump out of his skin. He pulls up his welding mask and squints at him.
"C--can I help you, mister?"
Amos puts on a calmer facade, "Yeah, hi. My name's John Rollins. I'm a reporter with Vice. This is a bit awkward, but my car broke down and I REALLY need it up and running. Could you possibly take a look at it? It's only a block away from here."
Eddie casts a suspicious glance at him, "Uhh...sure. Do you have any idea what went wrong?"
Amos shrugs, "It just spurted and abruptly stopped in the middle of the street. Like I said, I'm a reporter, and I REALLY need to be on my way right now. So, if you wouldn't mind coming with me..."
The mechanic gulps, wipes his brow with the back of his hand, and says, "Fine. Lemme just get washed up. How come you didn't call a tow truck or something?"
Amos impatiently licks his lips, "This was the closest garage I could find. And to be honest, I'm working on something really important. I lost my cellular phone in a...uh...scuffle. Just the perks of being on the cutting-edge of modern journalism, y'know."
Eddie frowns, "A scuffle?"
Amos waves his hand dismissively, "Eh, you should see the other guys, haha! Now, c'mon. Just around the block, buddy. I'll pay extra. Money is no object for me."
Eddie puts down his welding mask and scratches his neck. When he hears that, he quickens his pace, "O--OK, Mr. Rollins." It's not because of greed but rather an unwillingness to press the issue further. And busters like Ed do need the money more than they'd care to admit.
(Amos does look pretty intimidating, if nothing else!)
Sirens wail in the background. They fade away as soon as Eddie returns. He wipes his soaking wet hands and grabs a toolbox. He puts his cap on backwards and motions for the man who is going to be his murderer to lead the way.
"Let's have a look, shall we? So, where you from, hmm? I get started with my pottery classes tomorrow..."
Once they arrive at the location, Amos quickly surveys his surroundings to ensure that no one is nearby- he made sure to avoid getting spotted on any surveillance cameras (or onlookers, for that matter) on the way here. He opens the trunk and pulls the knife out while poor Eddie takes a look at the engine.
The mortal quizzically glances at the innards of the vampire's sedan. He mutters something or the other under his breath. Within an instant, he feels the sharp blade lodge itself into his stomach. Blood starts to pour out from his mouth.
Amos bares his fangs and twists the blade. Eddie looks at him with wide eyes and lets out a small whimper as he attempts to grapple with his attacker.
His killer.
Eddie's pitiful attempt at resisting this preternatural menace only draws the knife deeper into his abdomen. Another squelching sound pierces the night air. And then another.
Ed puts his hands on the broad-shouldered psychopath and tries to shove him away, but Amos shoves him onto the ground, pins him down with his sturdy legs, and starts viciously stabbing him. Over and over and over again.
Eddie can't even scream. With each brutal stab, he feels the life slowly seep out of him. His vision is horribly blurry, but he still can make out some of the details. Glowing green eyes stare down at him with sheer malice behind them.
Amos finally stabs Eddie's neck. He doesn't stop until the blood spurts all over his face and clothing. As his victim bleeds out, the Bnei Hashem opens his mouth and attempts to lap up as much of the mortal's spurting blood as he possibly can.
Once Amos is done with the gruesomely grisly deed, he stands up with a heaving groan and looks at how bloodied he has become.
Eddie twitches for a moment or two, but it doesn't take long before he grows still. Completely still.
Amos derisively snarks, "Looks like you won't be coming for pottery classes tomorrow. Too bad, pal. Too fuckin' bad. Still, at least you got to meet a Kindred before you croaked."
He looks up at the night sky and screeches like a feral animal. A nearby stray cat yowls in terror and scampers away from this mysterious creature of the nocturnal hours. Amos's Beast WANTS more; it's Hunger will never be satiated, after all.
"Shut up. Just...shut up. I gotta take care of the body first. Or...I could just leave it here."
His Beast impels him to leave the mangled corpse and seek out a new victim. And there is no shortage of prey to choose from in the bustling metropolis of Magnolia City. Amos grunts, huffs terribly, and pulls himself together.
He can't lose control now. But his Beast's eternal demands scratch away at the innermost parts of his psyche- or whatever is left of it.
So, Mr. Greenberg gets in his car, starts it up, and swerves onto the road. Eddie's dead body is left lying on the ground. Someone will soon stumble upon the corpse.
But consequences be damned- Amos couldn't care if his handiwork was discovered. It's done; Eddie won't ever talk again. And that's what matters most to these foul fucking bastards.
The wailing sirens have gotten louder again. The ambulance speeds past him as he cruises by a nearby liquor store. Police cruisers follow after the ambulance, going in the opposite direction that the False Assamite came from.
Once again, Amos gets out of his car, checks his surroundings, and quickly makes his way into the alleyway near the liquor store. With his fangs and claws fully borne, he speedily charges into the closest warm-bodied mortal that he sees.
Much, much later...
Amos is back in the comfort of his duplex penthouse. He is freshly showered and is currently lounging on his sofa while wearing a soft, teal-colored bathrobe. In one hand, he has a hand-grip strengthener which he routinely squeezes till it almost snaps.
He frequently does this whenever he wants to keep his Beast in check and prevent himself from Frenzying completely. And so far, it's working. He can still control his (inexcusable) actions.
Amos is still in control of his rancid self. A knock on his front door snaps him out of his murderous musings- his repulsive reverie. He quickly puts his toy down and loudly calls out, "IT'S OPEN!"
Allison pokes her head in and grins at him, "Hey! Looking good. I swear, if you flash me--"
"Just get inside and lock the damn door."
The other False Assamite obliges. She shuts the door behind her, flips the latch, and slips off her boots. She throws herself on the couch and sighs heavily, "Geez. Tough night, huh? Social media's abuzz- they found some Hispanic guy stabbed to death. No sign of a murder weapon. The cops got nothing to go on."
Amos smirks fiendishly, "I'll explain everything, Allie. In the meantime, I got Avi digging into that nosy PI shiksa."
Allison clicks her tongue, "Why don't we just kill her as well?"
"We have to be sure about a few things first. If she knows something or is even remotely onto us, then we're only opening up a huge can of worms. We keep her at arm's length. And that's where you come in."
"Me??"
"While you were taking your time coming here, I was doing some, shall we say, research of my own. It seems that Miss Akira is...ah, lemme just show you. I don't have the patience to explain this shit. Hey, grab my phone, would ya?"
Allie pulls out the charging cable and gives him his device. He unlocks it with his fingerprint and explains, "I was scrolling through her Facebook. Here, why don't you spend a few minutes looking at what I was looking at, huh?"
Allison snatches the phone from her fellow murderous psycho and hums some bland tune while idly scrolling through Lena's public Facebook profile. She notices that there's a small photo of Lena with another woman who looks somewhat similar to her.
She muses, "Huh. I see. How intriguing. I think I know where this is headed. I can't say that I like where this is going, but I trust you, Amos. Still, I think we should just take the risk and kill her while we still have the chance."
Amos interjects, "And deal with the potential fallout if we're wrong? Just keep looking...you haven't gotten to the juicy parts yet. Keep scrolling. You'll see what I mean, heh-heh."
A plan is slowly forming in his mind. A sinister and loathsome plan that might prove to be a gamechanger of sorts.
Allison impatiently clears her throat and points out, "I know that look. C'mon, talk to me! What do you have planned for this bitch?" She pauses for a moment or two.
"Word on the street is that the others have used her. Other Kindred. So, you can understand why I'm not so keen on having you cut her neck just yet. Soon, sure. But not now. And yes, you'll get to watch her blood spurt out like a fountain. Until then..."
Allison smiles at the mental image he has put in her head, "Until then, I gotta get my kicks elsewhere, huh?"
He languidly gestures to his fellow vampire, "Well, you're pretty busy- just pace yourself out. I know that you can get 'em all at once, but that would only serve to incur more suspicion from our...erstwhile partners. Also, it might start to become a problem."
"Hmm. So, this Lena. I think I know how you want me to deal with her."
"Lemme hear it from you to see if you got it down pat." He knows that Allison can be VERY adept when it comes to this sort of shit.
Allison dryly states, "You want me to be gay for her. Gay for pay."
Amos chuckles and nods, "That's the idea down to a tee, Allie. You still have it in you, huh? Even after all this time. And it doesn't hurt that you're a great shot as well. But you're gonna have to scale that part of your personality down a bit. But the other thing? The arts and shit? Yeah, lean into that."
Allie nods and bites her lower lip, "Huh, sure. So, the plan is to...seduce her, get in her good graces...and her panties, find out what she knows, and kill her if necessary."
Amos grimaces, "Except the last part. Right now, I'd prefer if you just stalled her. Find out what makes this shiksa tick- you can only learn so much from a Facebook profile- and whatever Avi's gonna dig up on her."
Allison gesticulates and muses, "Some nights, I feel gay. I feel like a Toreador, a Malkavian. Sometimes, I feel like a redhead. Other times, I feel like an Ethiopian. And there are times when I even feel...alive. Tonight, I feel like an immigrant. Tomorrow, I may feel like I am disabled, who knows? And the night after that, I may feel like a man, or worse, an Italian."
Amos snickers at this utterly banal and meaningless diatribe of hers, "Don't just pop up outta the blue and message the bitch. Be subtle about it. And don't approach her on the street like some kinda wide-eyed hobo, either. Use your head."
She scoffs at him, "I always use my head- even when it hurts. I got a good grip on her. I don't know if my dead pussy will be able to get a reaction outta her. And what's more, we'll have to keep in mind that she might know something- something that can blow our covers."
"I don't need reminding about the Masquerade. But yeah, when you're...with her, just make yourself feel more alive, hmm? Put some Blood into it."
"Amos?"
"Hmm?"
"Is it necrophilia??"
Amos rubs his forehead with his palm and groans, "Oh, god. I do not need this kinda shit right now. What--what the fuck even spurred this on? Just stop it, Allie. It's all fake and pointless to our kind, anyway. And nigh impossible."
Ms. Moretta chuckles heartily, seeming to get a kick out of Amos's irritation. She assures him, "Relax. I'm a professional. I don't develop feelings for my...assignments. But if Lena...Adams gets to be too much of a problem, I'm cutting her neck. Or maybe I'll take my sweet time while draining every last drop of her blood. Oh, the choices, the choices..."
Mr. Greenberg reminds her, "Not until I explicitly give you the go-ahead. Until then, it's strictly...uh...recon. Yeah."
"Fuck-con, more like. And if she turns me down?"
He groans again, "Oh, god. Just lay on the charm, sheesh. Look, all this LGBTABCDEFG stuff is a headache to keep up with- they keep adding new letters every week! Look at some dirty videos online and see how they act. That's your best bet. Talk about how different each shiksa is and all that."
Allison grabs Amos's phone and notes, "Ah, she's...been around the block. Hm. Slut. I can work with this. Gay for pay, hmm? And what exactly do you intend to pay me with? Magic beans??"
Muscle man shrugs, "I dunno, do you accept that as a form of payment? Oh, and avoid using your maiden name. I don't think that 'Morenstein' will catch her attention. But we can only assume so much. To her, you'll be Allison Moretta. Easier that way, even though I don't like that kind of erasure."
"I have become all things unto all men...and women...and others."
Amos giggles lightly, "Yeah, hah! That's right! Now, go on. Get outta here. I got some company coming over. Hey, you're welcome to stay and all, but..."
Allie plugs his phone back in, stands up, and jests, "I'm gay for pay, not bi for free. Or het for...ah, my train of thought drifted." She has a glint of amusement in her dead eyes.
He sighs and waves her off without saying anything. It's times like these where he just can't deal with the oddball antics of the other False Assamite.
A few minutes later...
In hobbles none other than Laurence Lancaster, Whip to the Nosferatu Primogen. The Nossie is dressed in a fine suit that accentuates his deformed frame quite nicely. He waddles into Amos's living room and lowers himself onto the couch with a pained grunt.
Amos looks at him with a modicum of pity, "I take it that finding the place wasn't too hard, Mr. Lancaster?"
"You own the whole damn building, huh?"
"That I do, sir. And many like it all over this Domain. Yeah, drink it all up- I've been in this city for a long time." Amos proudly extends his arms and puffs his chest out a little.
Laurence gets straight to the point, "Well, I think it's high time that we came to an agreement. If Otto won't deal with you, then maybe I will. I want an alliance- Banu Haqim and Nosferatu."
Amos winces when those two words are said, "It's Bnei Hashem. Don't make the same mistake twice. The so-called 'Banu Haqim' are the fakes, the posers, the liars. It's ALWAYS been the Bnei Hashem. The greatest act of Jewish erasure in Kindred Society was supplanting US with those...animals!!"
He balls his hands into fists and lets out a loud grunt, "Those...fucking...liars!! It HAS ALWAYS BEEN THE BNEI HASHEM! NEVER EVER...EVER FUCKING AGAIN...will I ever utter the word 'Assamite'! And I sure as hell won't be called one!"
Laurence remains oddly composed, "Uh-huh. Whatever makes you feel better. Forgive me for making that error. Bnei Hashem it is."
(There's historical revisionism and then there's Amos Greenberg and the Bnei Hashem).
Amos's seething rage subsides. He gathers his thoughts and offers, "I'll be your eyes and ears in places where neither your eyes nor ears can reach. Heh. And I've got enough connections lined up to make you people blush. All you gotta do is ask."
The Nossie approvingly remarks, "You seem to know how this game is played, huh? Otto's not gonna be too pleased that I went behind his back like this, but...screw it. We might as well solidly this alliance. Now, I may not have been a Kindred for as long as you have, but I am no fool, Mr. Greenberg."
"Oh, I'm sure of it. In the grand scheme of things, I am just a facilitator."
"Of what?"
The question lingers in the air while the False Assamite smirks fiendishly without replying right away. When he does respond, he speaks very slowly and deliberately, "That's not really for you to be privy to. Yet. And don't go around poking your snouts where they don't belong. Because if you do, I'll know."
He leans closer and warns, "And I'll be a very, very sad man if that happens. And you don't ever wanna make me sad, do you? You don't wanna see me when I'm at my lowest, pal."
The two stare at each other for a few moments before Laurence is the first one to break eye contact. He sniffs loudly and assents, "Hrmf. Fair enough. Are you absolutely certain that you- and your people- can offer our Clan some support? You're not just all talk, right?"
Amos shrugs, "Only one way to find out. And what can YOU offer us, hm?"
Laurence ponders for a moment before saying, "We can make sure that you aren't interrupted by prying eyes or...prickly ears. You get our protection; we get your knowledge. If we're in the mood, you'll get some of our secrets, too. And it's only fair that I warn ya..."
He lifts his chin and growls, "Cross us, and we'll gut you from stem to stern. In this city, the Nosferatu do not fuck around."
Amos bites his lower lip and smiles widely, "My kinda crowd! C'mon...let's shake on it."
He does grimace when he feels how slimy and slippery Laurence's hand is. It's quite...grody. Still, he isn't deterred from having to forge this new alliance.
Mr. Lancaster pulls his twisted hand away and queries, "And what about our mutual acquaintance?"
"He and my Allison are getting pretty chummy, yeah. Pretty close. And no, I don't even wanna think about them getting any closer than is absolutely necessary. Ugh, the mental images alone. No--no offense to you people and all."
The Leper waves his hand dismissively, "I've been subject to worse, Mr. Greenberg. But it's all water off my back. I outlive them, I outlast them, hell, I probably own the building that they live in, huh?"
Amos agrees and jokes, "Makes for good protection, yeah. And I'm not slowing down- I got my eyes on a few more places. Downtown, uptown, wherever. the property values have fuckin' skyrocketed lately. You people know that better than anyone else, right?"
Laurence shrugs, "We sure do. And I gotta ask...is your paperwork in order? From a legal point of view?"
Amos seems to find that quite funny and guffaws, "I like to keep things 'legit', but as long as the money keeps rolling in and the rents get paid on time, I don't really care if my places are up to code or not. Y'know, the Court thinks that it knows me. All those self-righteous Salubri pricks--"
"Prince Cedric and his people have been good to our Clan, Amos. We don't necessarily wanna mess that up. Unless you can offer something substantial, we don't make a single goddamn move against the Salubri."
The Bnei Hashem snickers, "Fair enough, fair enough. Now, sit back and relax. Let's talk terms, hmm? Don't worry, we got time. Unless you have other places to be tonight? And hey...is that blood that I see on your lapel?" He squints and leans closer to get a better look.
Laurence moves away from him, "It's none of your concern, Greenberg. Certain...issues mean that transporting our meals in live form is easier said than done. A little disruption in our routes and what have you. We're open to any solutions that you people can offer us."
Amos rubs his chin, "I'll look into it from my end. Let's get into the nitty-gritty of our budding alliance, hmm?"
Mr. Lancaster dryly grunts, "Hm. Let's." The Neonate cannot wait to do some independent scheming.
So, it looks like the Clan of the Hidden is now (via Laurence Lancaster, at least) in league with the Bnei Hashem. Otto Weinbauer had his reasons for being skeptical about entering into such an arrangement with Amos Greenberg.
To him, the Bnei Hashem are more trouble than they're worth. If nothing else, the Nossie Primogen perceives them as being far too disruptive to a status quo that has served the Clan rather well for many decades now.
On the other hand, Amos has scored himself a valuable new ally in the form of the Nosferatu Whip to the Primogen. Risky (and ballsy), but in this Great Game, taking risks is almost like a prerequisite.
Risks that often involve innocent lives, livelihoods, bodies, and minds...but hey, it's all just collateral damage in the grand scheme of things, right?
To such moneyed monsters, it barely even registers as being wrong to just commit unspeakable crimes against other human beings. Besides, that's simply the nature of the profit system as a whole.
Decades of dog-eat-dog dogma, survival of the fittest, nice people finish last, success at all costs, winning no matter who (or what), and other such foul notions have warped the certain vamps beyond any semblance of recognition. They have lost so much Humanity that the Beast crawls right under their very skin.
And mortals themselves are far from immune to the ongoing socio-economic processes constantly at work. We cannot escape history, and only a fool thinks otherwise.
